# GATE-SPEC: the 28 gates, reconciled

Written 2026-11-13 (UTC) by workstream bt-gates and corrected the same day after two independent reviews (what was found and changed is in 11.4 and 11.5); reconciled again on 2026-11-14 (UTC) against revision 3 of `GATE-SPEC-A.md` and the fourth pass of `GATE-SPEC-B.md`, which both moved after 22:03 UTC on 13 November (what was brought over, what was found and what was run is in 11.6). This file reconciles `GATE-SPEC-A.md` (the gates defined from the governing text, with its evidence folder `spec-a-evidence/`) and `GATE-SPEC-B.md` (the gates seen from the implementation, with its evidence folder `spec-b-evidence/`). **Where the two differ, or where either differs from the code, the test files, the realization harness or the plan's own gate text, this file governs and the stricter reading wins; every such difference is listed in section 8.** The two inputs are left unedited in this folder as the record of what each said. Governing text: `BIBLE/plan-clean.txt`, the 144-page "Gettysburg Total War animation plan, version 2" (marker `=== PAGE n ===` equals the printed `page n of 144`). Project rules: `CLAUDE.md`. Decisions: `GOVERNANCE/decision_log.md` and `GOVERNANCE/decisions/DELEGATED-DECISIONS-2026-11-11.md` (entries DD-nn; DD-G01 to DD-G19 for gate settings and readings; DD-RZ-01 to DD-RZ-12 for the realization harness; DD-PM-01 to DD-PM-05 for photo-match).

What this file is: the definition a runner must implement and a reviewer must apply. It sets no `passes` flag, runs no gate, edits no data and lowers nothing. A number quoted here was read from a named page or file. Where the plan gives no number the file says so and marks anything it adopts as ADOPTED-PROVISIONAL under a recorded decision; it is never presented as the plan's. Statements about what exists today are true of the working tree at the time named and are marked as read from `GATE-SPEC-B.md` when this file did not re-run them.

**Citation form.** `(p. 136, Q)` is page 136, Part E, Section Q. `(p. 62, B.2.5)` is page 62, Part B. `(App. B, pp. 138-139)` is an appendix. **Quotation convention (checked by script, 11.1 and 11.6):** a string between straight double quotation marks is a quotation of the plan and was matched against the page it is cited to; text taken from a repository file, a decision, the owner's chat or the words of this file itself is written between backticks; a paraphrase carries no quotation marks.

**Ambiguity rule.** Where the text can be read two ways, this file takes the reading under which fewer items can reach PASS. The looser reading is never used silently; each choice is an A-nn entry (ambiguity of the plan's text, ids unchanged from `GATE-SPEC-A.md`, section 9) or an X-nn entry (disagreement between the two specs, the code or the harness, section 8).

## 0. Key rulings in one place

1. **K1 Pass is a conjunction.** `passes=true` needs every applicable gate to give PASS for the sub-requirement's items, the class rule (section 4), an admissible `source_status` (section 5) and U-02 P2 to P6. A gate PASS is necessary and never sufficient.
2. **K2 Linked is not passing.** A link to a register entry is never a pass. A non-passing sub-requirement is LINKED-ACTIVE only at link level L3 (complete entry, marking in the product, dated machine-readable search of the acquired library, reconstruction present in the product build); Q18 counts PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE (1.4, X-02, X-03).
3. **K3 COVERED has a narrow reach.** COVERED (the gate's own register or flag branch) exists and counts toward a gate verdict only where the gate text has that branch: Q07, Q08, Q17, Q19, Q25, Q28. For Q05, Q09 and Q14 a COVERED item is reported and keeps the verdict INCOMPLETE. COVERED never gives `passes` (X-09).
4. **K4 A card is not a visual realization.** A row with no scene consequence, and every open-gap row, is covered only by an L3 entry (and, for an open-gap row, the visible gap marker). Q17 rests on the strict reading (section 6, X-05).
5. **K5 Documented means matched.** `documented` needs a non-lead source and a short extract (under 15 words, p. 134, E.6) that the runner re-matches in the stored source text in the run; a stored flag is not evidence. Leads (Wikipedia, forums, blogs, auction pages, wikis) never support a documented datum (p. 134, E.1, E.2, E.7).
6. **K6 State rows are judged by every object they name.** An object no gate covers (a road, a cemetery ground) blocks the row; an empty domain is never a pass (3.2, A-12).
7. **K7 Top-level rows aggregate.** A row `R01.0000` to `R22.0000` is covered when every child (the other rows of the requirement, the populations of 4.11 and the requirement-level check items RC-Rnn.k of 7.1, so that no requirement is childless) is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE, and passes only when its Appendix E gates, its requirement-level checks and every child pass; R03.0000 never passes without a recorded owner interpretation (4.9 TOP-3, A-54, DD-G15).
8. **K8 Thresholds are recorded or unset.** A threshold is a recorded value (DD-G01 to DD-G06, DD-G09, DD-G10 and DD-G13, DD-RZ-01 to DD-RZ-03 as limited by DD-G09, the plan's own figures) or UNSET, and an unset one makes the gate INCOMPLETE. No runner loosens one (1.7).
9. **K9 Owed is owed.** Anything that needs the real GPU, a listener, the owner's caps or a purchase is NOT-RUN or INCOMPLETE, written as owed with the exact step, never claimed.
10. **K10 Every means every.** A check that says "every" runs over the whole domain: the scenario set S* and the time grid of 1.5. Work may be chunked and resumed; a sample never gives PASS (U-26).
11. **K11 A gate must be able to fail.** Every check has a negative control (an injected defect it must catch); a runner without one is not accepted (U-25).
12. **K12 One result file per gate.** `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Qnn.json`, per item `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`; `pass` is true only for result PASS (1.6, X-01).
13. **K13 No circular support.** Statuses are computed in strata, in this order: (1) every gate except Q18, then the requirement-level checks RC-R01 to RC-R22 of 7.1, each after the gates its items name (an RC item that names a gate reads that gate's record; none reads Q18's record or a `passes` flag); (2) the status of every leaf row; (3) the derived coverage of the 22 top rows; (4) Q18; (5) `passes` of the leaf rows; (6) `passes` of the 22 top rows, last. Q18 reads statuses and the records of step 1 and never a `passes` flag, and a top row's `passes` is an input to no gate and to no child, so R01.0000, which lists Q18 among its gates, is not its own support (4.9 TOP-5, X-42).

## Contents

1. Common machinery: terms and results, universal rules U-01..U-28, link levels, effective tier, scenarios and time, result files and runner conventions, thresholds, where things run
2. The 28 gates Q01..Q28 (definition with page citations, item and per-item pass rule, runner design, where it runs, sub-requirements it gates, state)
3. The derived gates OA-G01..OA-G12 for the twelve owner-chat rows, and the domain gates for state-schedule rows
4. Pass rules per sub-requirement class (TL, EP, SS, WK, GE, PE, CT, CF, TOP, OA and the populations still to be generated)
5. The rule for `source_status`
6. The visual realization record that Q17 expects
7. Requirement-level checks RC-R01..RC-R22 and the Section Z milestone test
8. Disagreements X-01..X-nn (stricter reading wins)
9. Ambiguity register A-01..A-57
10. Threshold registry, decisions recorded and inputs owed
11. What was run for this file; page index; repository files named; the corrections after the independent reviews (11.4, 11.5); the re-reconciliation of 2026-11-14 (11.6)

## 1. Common machinery

### 1.1 Terms, item results, verdicts

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Item | The smallest thing one gate judges: a unit in a scenario, a timeline row, one state change of one object, a datum class scanned exhaustively, a night frame region. Each gate in section 2 names its item. A class of data judged by an exhaustive scan may be one item whose evidence lists every failing member; an item is never coarser than the gate text allows (K10). |
| Sub-requirement (S-R) | A row of `requirements.json` `sub_requirements` (969 rows on 2026-11-14: 957 `origin: pdf`, 12 `origin: owner_chat`; there were 966 and 9 before OA10 to OA12 were added on 2026-11-13 by DEC-OA10-12). Its text is never edited: "you may never delete or reword one" (p. 134, F). |
| Subjects | The entity ids an item is about (unit ids, structure ids, work ids, row ids, content classes). The apply script joins items to S-Rs by `sub_requirements` (the item's own S-R) and by `subjects` (the entities a timeline row's realization record names, section 6). |
| Link level | L0 to L3: how far an S-R is tied to an open Evidence-Gap Register entry (1.4). |
| S-R status | PASSING, LINKED-ACTIVE, LINKED, UNLINKED (1.4). |
| Scenario set S* | Every selectable combination of source-conflict values that can reach an item (1.5). |
| Renderer class | `python`, `node`, `swgl` (software GL in a browser here) or `gpu` (the owner's GPU, NOT RUNNABLE HERE) (1.8). |

**Item results** (the value of `result`; `pass` is true only for PASS):

| Result | Meaning |
|---|---|
| PASS | The property holds for this item and the run record names the evidence. |
| COVERED | The item does not meet the PASS branch but the gate's own register or flag branch is fully met (an L3 entry, the visible flag, the stated window). Counts toward a verdict only for Q07, Q08, Q17, Q19, Q25, Q28 (K3). Never toward `passes`. |
| FAIL | The property is violated (an entity before its arrival window, a modern object in the scene, an unmarked procedural item, a missing datum, an object not drawn). |
| NOT-RUN | The check could not be executed here (real GPU, missing tool, blocked host, a spec that printed `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU)`, a skipped test). The record states why. Non-passing. |
| BLOCKED-INPUT | An input the gate names does not exist or is not eligible (no verified OOB table, no documented camera position, no cap set, a source text not on the machine). Non-passing. No vacuous pass (U-03). |
| N/A | The gate's domain excludes the item. N/A never satisfies an item whose every listed gate is N/A (U-03). |

**Gate verdict.** PASS: at least one in-domain item exists, every in-domain item is PASS (or COVERED where K3 allows it), and none is FAIL, NOT-RUN or BLOCKED-INPUT. FAIL: at least one FAIL. INCOMPLETE: no FAIL, but at least one NOT-RUN, BLOCKED-INPUT, a COVERED item of a gate outside K3, an unset threshold, an incomplete scan, or an empty domain. Exit code of the runner: 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 INCOMPLETE, 3 runner error.

### 1.2 Universal rules

Each rule cites the text it comes from. U-01 to U-24 keep the ids of `GATE-SPEC-A.md`; U-25 to U-28 are added by this file.

- **U-01 Gate pass is necessary, not sufficient.** "A sub-requirement passes only when its gate passes" (p. 136, Q). `passes=true` needs (a) every applicable gate in `gate_ids` plus the domain gates of 3.2 and 4.x to give PASS for this S-R's items (A-02: `gate_ids` with several gates is a conjunction), (b) the class rule of section 4, (c) an admissible `source_status` (section 5), (d) P2 to P6 of U-02.
- **U-02 What PASS means for an S-R.** P1 the item results are PASS (U-01 a). P2 evidence sufficiency: the tier the product shows does not exceed the evidence status (1.3, section 5). P3 marking: every non-documented content of the item is marked (the Q23 item is PASS). P4 conflicts: every source conflict that reaches the item is carried as a selectable scenario and the item passes in each (A-06). P5 no content gap: an item whose own content is an open gap, a failed lookup, an unsupported claim or lead-only support never passes. P6 provenance of the run: command, versions, input hashes, renderer class and date are recorded (1.6) and any renderer-dependent part is listed as owed on the GPU.
- **U-03 No vacuous pass.** An empty domain gives BLOCKED-INPUT, never PASS. An S-R whose every listed gate is N/A for it cannot pass by that; it needs the domain gate of 3.2 or section 4 to pass, otherwise it stays LINKED.
- **U-04 Linked is not passing.** Section Z item 1 reads "every in-scope sub-requirement passes or is linked to an open register entry with an active reconstruction" (p. 138, Z); F (p. 134) and Q18 (p. 136) say the same: passing and linked are two different states. A link never turns a FAIL, NOT-RUN or COVERED item into PASS (A-03).
- **U-05 No lowering, no skipping.** "Never lower a threshold to pass" (p. 136, Q). A gate, threshold, scenario, row or class is never narrowed, disabled or skipped to make an item pass (p. 138, Z; Law cl. 1 and 8).
- **U-06 Scope of time.** The whole window: 1863-06-26 00:00 through 1863-07-14 23:59 local mean time (LMT = UTC minus 5 h 09 min, p. 9, B.2 and p. 111, B.10.2) plus the epilogue days 1863-11-18 and 1863-11-19. The window starts June 26 or the owner's chosen start and ends at the owner's chosen end, "the notes cover the retreat through about July 14" (p. 137, T.4); the epilogue is R22 (p. 133, A; pp. 116-117, B.12). Narrowing is an owner decision (p. 130, D.8.2); DD-20 keeps the full window. All three battle days and all nights are in scope (p. 138, Y.12).
- **U-07 Scope of scenarios.** Every check that depends on a source conflict runs over S* (1.5). "Scan every frame range of every scenario" (p. 136, Q04).
- **U-08 Evidence.** A datum is `documented` only with a non-lead source that states exactly it and a short extract (p. 134, E.2 and E.6; p. 136, P.3; App. B, pp. 138-139). Wikipedia, forums, blogs, auction listings and wikis are leads (p. 134, E.1, E.2, E.7). A quotation is under 15 words (p. 134, E.6); the repository's working limit is 14 words.
- **U-09 Never fabricate.** No invented quotation, measurement, name, time, number, citation or URL; a failed lookup is recorded as failed (Law cl. 4, p. 132). A gate result never contains an extract the runner did not match in the stored source text.
- **U-10 Renderer class.** A node or python result cannot certify a property defined on the picture. A software-GL result is valid for properties that do not depend on the GPU (pixel statistics of a deterministic shader, draw-list contents, pixel identity between two runs) and is recorded `PASS@swgl` with `real-GPU confirmation owed`. Frame time, GPU memory and the real-GPU baseline need `gpu`; the runner records NOT-RUN with the reason (this container has software rendering only). A visual result is claimed only for frames that were looked at (each picture item carries `looked_at: false` until a person or an engineer viewed the saved image).
- **U-11 Independent verification.** A fresh-context verification run per milestone, told to flag only correctness and requirement gaps; "Do not self-certify what you have not run" (p. 136, Q, last paragraph; p. 137, X.5). Its record is part of the milestone (Q27.5).
- **U-12 Records decide.** `passes` is a pure function of the latest valid run records (1.6). A record expires when an input hash it names changes. A failing, expired or missing record sets `passes=false`.
- **U-13 Literal compliance impossible.** The item is not exempt (Law cl. 3 and 5): the strongest evidence-based reconstruction is built, registered (tier, marking, owner question), marked in the product and escalated; the S-R is then at most LINKED-ACTIVE.
- **U-14 Visible gaps.** A missing-evidence item is shown as a marked gap (Law cl. 7; p. 9, B.2: "Nodes with tier open gap generate no behaviour and surface as map markers in the evidence layer"). A concealed gap is a violation: FAIL for Q23.
- **U-15 Reading the product.** Gates test what the page loads and draws (the shipped data, draw lists, the audio graph), not only source data. The history layer is read-only to renderers (p. 134, D). A result obtained on the placeholder layer (`?history=stub`) says nothing about the history and never counts as evidence for an item (X-31).
- **U-16 Owner-only acts.** A gate that needs an account, licence acceptance, payment, third-party contact, deployment or a cap value (p. 130, D.8) is INCOMPLETE with the exact owner step written next to it. Claude never performs the act (Law cl. 2; CLAUDE.md standing constraints).
- **U-17 Delegated defaults.** The owner delegated the default view of conflicts on 2026-11-11. A default chosen under that delegation is the default for Q02, Q19 and class CF only while (a) it is recorded as an entry of section A of the delegated decisions and as `conflicts.C-nnn.default_scenario_id` in `decisions/conflict_defaults.json`, (b) the product labels it `Default chosen under delegated authority, reversible by the owner` with its tier, (c) the plan's default and every other value stay selectable. The word DECIDED is never written to `decision_log.md` for it.
- **U-18 Clocks.** All times are LMT as printed. The roughly 20-minute Union and Confederate clock difference "is not applied to any row, it is carried as uncertainty" (p. 9, B.2). Nothing in a gate applies it: Q04, Q05 and Q06 are exact, and so is the match of a realization record to the time its row allows, which has no margin at either end (6.4 V4). The wider window that the realization harness uses as its own necessary condition (DD-RZ-02, a 20-minute margin at each end of a printed clock time) is not adopted by the gate (X-36, DD-G09).
- **U-19 Row identity.** Day-header rows of the timeline are not events (p. 9, B.2) and are not sub-requirements. Row ids T1-001.., T2-001.., T3-001.. (T3-131 absent) and E-001..E-014 are stable.
- **U-20 Thresholds.** Only recorded thresholds are used (1.7); an unrecorded one makes the gate INCOMPLETE.
- **U-21 Fresh state.** A run starts from the committed inputs plus the working-tree data it names by hash. It never reads a result file as if it were an input and never runs a script's `main()` that writes outside `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/` (X-30): it imports the functions or runs the command without its writing flag.
- **U-22 Heavy runs** (over about 30 s of CPU, every browser run) go through `tools/horde/bench.sh`, at most two at once, a timing measurement alone (`--timing`). A run waiting for the lock is not a failure. The job is started in the background, writes to a file and is waited for; it is not killed. The wait can be hours: at 00:11 UTC on 14 November a locked job joined a queue of about 16 `bench.sh` jobs behind two slots, one of them held for hours by the browser-spec owner's queue runner, and it started 33 minutes later (`GATE-SPEC-B.md` 1 and 1.1); a runner therefore has no timeout that turns waiting into FAIL or PASS, records the state `queued` or `running` in its result file, leaves the verdict INCOMPLETE until the run ends and reads its output file when it exists.
- **U-23 Copy audit.** Gate outputs under `GOVERNANCE` are not shipped. A gate assertion that reads shipped files (Q02, Q23) never writes or quotes how the site was produced into shipped files (CLAUDE.md; FINAL-ACCEPTANCE copy audit).
- **U-24 Product marking after DEC-006.** The "reviewed by automated subagents" label is dropped (DEC-006). Tier marking of non-documented content stays (Q23), as does the register.
- **U-25 A gate can fail.** Every check Qnn.k has at least one negative control in `tests/governance/gates/`: an injected defect (a unit placed before its arrival, a fence that does not change state, an unmarked procedural item, a quote that is not in the source) that the runner must report as FAIL. The test runs in the same suite as the runner.
- **U-26 Exhaustive or incomplete.** "Every", "at every" and "scan every" mean the whole domain and the whole grid (K10). A run that completes a fraction records the fraction and the verdict is INCOMPLETE. Results are cached by content hash (inputs, scenario, time, view) so that a resumed run skips finished chunks.
- **U-27 Skipped is not passed.** A test or spec that is skipped, or prints `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU)` (`t.gpuOnly`, `t.notRun` of `app/tests/lib.mjs`), counts as NOT-RUN for the part it names (X-32).
- **U-28 Existing greens are inputs.** `check_structures.py` "ALL PASS", the node suite and the other self-checks validate data and engine consistency. They are evidence for an item and never a gate result (SPEC-B 5.4).

### 1.3 Evidence statuses and tiers

The vocabulary of `source_status` and its rule are section 5. Tiers (App. B, pp. 138-139): `documented` (at least one non-lead source with a short extract, the claim matches the source); `inferred` (`reasoning` required, premises documented); `plausible-procedural` (a complete `procedural` block: generator and version, seed, distribution, documented context, substitute; and a register entry when the gap affects a requirement). Items affecting a visible result need two independent sources (distinct `independent_of` roots) or a flag. `render_flag`: 0 documented, 1 inferred, 2 plausible-procedural. "Open gaps are not a tier: they are register entries" (p. 133, B); the timeline uses the category `open gap` for rows (p. 9, B.2) and it is shown as a marked gap, not as content. Substitute content (modern motion bases, synthesized audio, open textures) never receives the documented tier and is recorded in the asset manifest (p. 121, C.10).

**Effective tier** of a timeline row or datum, in order of precedence: (1) an accepted correction record (`timeline_corrections/`, `evidence_corrections/`) with a citation and passage; (2) `timeline_verified.jsonl` `tier` as adopted by DD-08 and DD-09; (3) the seed tier (`timeline_seed.jsonl`, never rewritten). The effective tier can only be lower than or equal to what the evidence status allows; a demotion is always allowed, a promotion needs a matched citation. The overlay `timeline_verified.jsonl` also holds the value `unclassified` for five rows (T2-121, T2-133, T2-148, T2-176, T2-178; all `status_effective` gap). It is not a tier of the plan: such a row has no effective tier, is treated as an open gap and cannot pass until a correction record gives it a tier (4.1 TL-1, A-53, DD-G15). The rule for how correction records combine and which one gives a row its status is in section 5.

### 1.4 Register links: "open", "active reconstruction", link levels

"Open register entry" uses the statuses of Appendix A (p. 138): `open`, `awaiting owner`, `decided`, `closed`. A link counts only for `open` and `awaiting owner` (A-04, X-02). A `decided` entry means the owner or the delegate chose: the decision is implemented and the S-R must then pass on its own. `closed` entries do not count (EG-0007 and EG-0025 today). "Entries are never deleted" (p. 137, R).

| Level | Condition (all runner-checkable) |
|---|---|
| L0 UNLINKED | No `register_ids` entry with status `open` or `awaiting owner`. "Unfinished work, not an exception" (p. 134, F). |
| L1 LINKED-RECORD | At least one open entry whose fields are all present and non-placeholder: `title, rids, cat, desc, why, searched, subst, recon, tier, marking, status` and the owner-decision field `owner_q` (Z.3, p. 138: "a reconstruction, a marking and an owner-decision field"; App. A, p. 138: "Owner decision requested"). A placeholder is empty, `-`, `none`, `n/a` or the marker `not stated by the shard proposal`. |
| L2 LINKED-MARKED | L1, and the marking is in the product for this item (the Q23 item is PASS; for a gap the visible gap marker with its register link). |
| L3 LINKED-ACTIVE | L2, and (b) the entry carries a dated machine-readable search log: a sidecar `GOVERNANCE/register/search_logs/<EG id>.json`, a list of `{source_id, locator, date_searched, result, note}` with `result` one of found, negative or unreachable naming the acquired sources that could settle the gap, each searched on or after the date that source was acquired (Law cl. 3a: exhaust the free primary-source library before settling for less); and (c) the reconstruction named in `recon` is present in the product build for this item: a datum, draw object, scenario or realization record that lists the register id and is loaded by the page, or, where the reconstruction is a marked gap with no generated behaviour, the marker object exists. |

**S-R status.** PASSING: the conditions of U-01 and U-02 hold for the row on the latest valid records (`apply_passes.py` then writes `passes=true` from it; the flag is an output, never an input, K13). LINKED-ACTIVE: not passing, L3. LINKED: not passing, L1 or L2. UNLINKED: L0. The coverage condition of Q18 is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE for a leaf row (every row except the 22 top rows below) and COVERED-BY-CHILDREN for a top row (K2, A-05, K13). The existing `check_governance.py` and `check_coverage.py` count any entry whose status is not `closed` as a link (and `check_governance.py` omits `owner_q` from its field list): that is the weaker reading, reported as such, never as Q18 coverage. Today the register has 487 entries (485 open, 2 closed); counted with the placeholder rule above, 129 have no usable `owner_q` (108 empty), so L1 fails for them; no entry has a search log (the format above is new), so L3 is empty today. The register folder holds 496 entry files and `register_index.json` 487 entries: nine files are not in the index (EG-E-84PA and EG-W3D-G01 to EG-W3D-G08, written by other workstreams). The runner reads the folder as well as the index, reports every file that is not in the index, and treats a link to such an entry as unresolved until the file has all the fields of L1 and an index entry (Q18.4, A-57, DD-G15).

**Top-level rows (`R01.0000` to `R22.0000`).** Their text is an aggregation (`all child sub-requirements pass or link ...`), so their coverage is derived and is never read from their own register link or their own `passes`: COVERED-BY-CHILDREN when the set of children is not empty, every child is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE and every expected population of 4.11 exists. The children of `Rnn.0000` are (a) every other row of requirement Rnn in `requirements.json`, (b) the populations of 4.11 once generated and (c) the requirement-level check items RC-Rnn.k of 7.1, one per test the plan prints for the requirement (71 items; they are what covers the 12 requirements that have a single row and no population, 4.9 TOP-1). The twelve rows `OA01.0000` to `OA12.0000` are leaf rows (3.1, 4.10), not top rows. The `passes` of a top row follows 4.9.

### 1.5 Scenario space, time grid, entity classes

- **Scenario.** A function from every conflict to one of its values: C-001..C-047 and the further conflicts the plan prints elsewhere (A-31). Values are the "All values" column of the register (pp. 62-63, B.2.5); "no alternative is deleted; every value ... remains selectable" (p. 62). Two scenarios are always in the set: the plan's recommended defaults (still PENDING OWNER) and the delegated defaults (`decisions/conflict_defaults.json`, a project decision of 2026-11-11, not the plan's).
- **S\*** (the scan set). The assignment of values to conflicts is expanded as the full Cartesian product inside each interaction group (conflicts that reach a common unit, event, object or channel; `layer.meta.linkedConflicts` and the ledger's gate chains give the groups) and independently across groups. The runner emits the group structure and the argument that the checked property factors over groups. S* also contains the two extremal scenarios (every conflict at its earliest value, every conflict at its latest) and both named defaults. A property that does not factor over groups is checked on the full product. Existing tests sample seven scenarios (`history.test.mjs SCENARIOS`); that is evidence, not S* (X-23).
- **Time grid.** Every integer minute LMT of the window of U-06, plus both sides (minus one second, plus one second) of every window boundary of every event, state change, arrival, departure, sunrise, sunset, moonrise, moonset and light-source start or end. "Every time" in a gate text means this grid. Rows without a clock minute (295 `phrase_only` and 1 `no_date` in `timeline_windows.jsonl`) are tested on the bounds of their date or part-of-day window and on three seeded interior times (A-18).
- **Entity classes.** Soldiers (officers, colour bearers, musicians, stretcher bearers), horses (ridden, led, draught), guns, limbers, caissons, wagons, ambulances, forge and battery wagon, flags and guidons, casualties on the ground, hospital contents, effects and sounds caused by a unit's actions. A gate that says "no entity" or "every unit" covers all of them in one scan.

### 1.6 Result files, the `passes` protocol and runner conventions

**Layout** (all under `docs/battle-theatre/GOVERNANCE/gate_results/`, my ownership):

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `Qnn.json` | The latest run record of gate Qnn (and `OAnn.json` for a derived gate of section 3, `RC-Rnn.json` for the requirement-level checks of requirement Rnn, 7.1). Authoritative. Written atomically (temporary file, rename). |
| `Qnn.prev.json` | The record it replaced (regression detection). |
| `evidence/Qnn/` | Sidecars (`.jsonl`, images) named in items by path and sha256 when evidence exceeds 2 KB. |
| `logs/` | Run logs of background jobs. |
| `thresholds.json` | The threshold registry of section 10, each with its metric and decision id. |
| `SUMMARY.md`, `SUMMARY.json` | Written by `run_all.py` after a run: per gate the verdict, the counts of results and the renderer class, and the list of gates and parts that are not runnable here, with the reason and the owner step. |
| `SR_STATUS.json`, `MILESTONE.json`, `status_log.jsonl` | Written by `apply_passes.py` and `milestone.py` (below). |

**Record** (`schema` `bt-gate-result/1`):

```
{ "schema": "bt-gate-result/1", "gate": "Q07", "run_id": "20261113T204500Z-Q07",
  "started_utc": "...", "finished_utc": "...", "renderer_class": "python|node|swgl|gpu|mixed", "runnable_here": true,
  "command": "python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q07", "tool_versions": {"node": "...", "python": "...", "chrome": "..."},
  "inputs": [ { "path": "...", "sha256": "..." } ],
  "thresholds": [ { "id": "T-Q07-1", "value": "...", "decision": "DD-G02" } ],
  "scenario_set": { "id": "S*", "groups": [ ... ], "count": N }, "time_grid": { "step_s": 60, "boundaries": true, "fraction_done": 1.0 },
  "verdict": "PASS|FAIL|INCOMPLETE", "counts": { "PASS": 0, "COVERED": 0, "FAIL": 0, "NOT-RUN": 0, "BLOCKED-INPUT": 0, "N/A": 0 },
  "owed": [ "real-GPU confirmation: ..." ],
  "items": [ { "id": "Q07/Q07.4/FEN-emmitsburg-road-1/default", "check": "Q07.4", "pass": false,
               "result": "COVERED", "reason": "window unresolved: ...", "evidence": { ... },
               "subjects": [ "FEN-..." ], "sub_requirements": [ "R14.S-062" ], "scenario": "default",
               "renderer_class": "node", "register_ids": [ "EG-..." ], "looked_at": false } ] }
```

`id`, `pass`, `reason` and `evidence` are required of every item; the other fields are required where they apply. `inputs` carries the sha256 of every input file and `counts` the totals per result. `runnable_here` is false for a gate that cannot run in this container (Q15 always, and the picture parts of Q10, Q24 and the rest when no browser is available): the record then states the reason, every item of that part is NOT-RUN with `pass: false` and the exact owner step is in `owed`. `time_grid.fraction_done` is below 1.0 for a run that finished a fraction of its grid (U-26: the verdict is INCOMPLETE). `pass` is `true` only for `result` PASS: COVERED, NOT-RUN, BLOCKED-INPUT, N/A and FAIL are all `pass: false` (K12). Two runs on the same inputs give identical `items` (seeds recorded; timestamps and durations live only in the header).

**Commands.** Every gate has one entry point: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py <Qnn|OAnn|RC-Rnn> [--scenarios default|extremal|S*|<id>] [--resume] [--only <id prefix>] [--out DIR]`. A gate whose check needs node runs `node tools/bt/gates/<module>.mjs` as a child and merges its JSON; a picture part runs as `tools/horde/bench.sh node ...` in the background; the driver writes `logs/Qnn.<stamp>.log`. `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_all.py` runs the light gates directly and the heavy gates through the lock, in the order of K13: first every gate except Q18, then the 22 requirement-level checks (`run_gate.py RC-R01` to `RC-R22`), each after the gates its items name and all of them before Q18 (Q18 reads their records, 4.9 TOP-5), then Q18 (its runner derives the statuses of K13 steps 2 and 3 from those records and the register and reads no `passes` flag), and last `apply_passes.py` (steps 5 and 6). Module names in section 2 are planned names, not contracts; the contracts are the entry point and the output files.

**Writing `requirements.json`.** Only `python3 tools/bt/gates/apply_passes.py` writes `passes`, `source_status`, `register_ids` and `owner_decision_ids`, by a byte-exact textual patch that leaves every other byte unchanged and refuses to write if the re-parsed non-owned fields differ; it never writes `text`, `id`, `origin`, `gate_ids`. It is idempotent, recomputes `passes` from the latest valid records at every run (a regression sets it back to false and the change is appended to `progress.md` with one shell append), and refuses `passes=true` for a row with no valid record, with a record older than an input, with NOT-RUN or BLOCKED-INPUT in an applicable gate, or with `seeded_unverified` on a PDF row. It computes the leaf rows first and the 22 top rows last, each after all its children, and reads no `passes` flag as an input (K13, 4.9 TOP-5). `tools/bt/link_subreqs.py` is brought into line (X-08). `python3 tools/bt/gates/milestone.py` computes the Section Z test of 7.2 into `MILESTONE.json`.

**Reading existing tools.** Runners import the pure functions (`check_environment.gate_q11`, `gate_q22`, `night_luminance_floor`; `check_structures.state_at`, `check_q07`, `check_q08`, `check_q09`, `check_scrub`; `check_arrivals.presence_ok`, `gate_time`; `check_kit.manifest_for`, `validate_manifest`; the functions of `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md`) or run a command without its writing flag. `check_environment.py main()`, `check_figures.py --write`, `app/tests/run-all.mjs` and every browser spec with its default output location write outside `gate_results/` and are not run as they are (SPEC-B 2.4 item 6; `BT_OUT=<name>` redirects spec output).

**What a runner must not do** (`GATE-SPEC-B.md` 5.4, kept as rules). Run a script of another workstream that rewrites its own outputs when run as a command: `tools/bt/photomatch/run.mjs`, `tools/bt/vegetation/build_vegetation.py` without `--check`, `tools/bt/kit_verified.py build` without `--out-dir`, `tools/bt/realize/check.mjs` without `--out` (it rewrites `GOVERNANCE/realizations/RESULTS.json`), `tools/bt/kit_overrides.py compile`, `tools/bt/photomatch/build_registry.py`, `build_statements.py`, `catalogue_additions.py` and `make_doc.py`. Count a browser test that printed `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU)` as run (U-27). Count a test on the placeholder layer as evidence about the history (U-15). Read a red test whose assertion is a count of a data file that its owner is still extending (`app/tests/photomatch-q16.test.mjs`: 129 records found against an expected 127 at 00:13 UTC) as a verdict on the gate or the product: it says that test and the data disagreed at that time (U-28). Treat `check_structures.py` ALL PASS as Q07, Q08 or Q09 passing for a row (it validates the schedule and the engine, not that the product shows the row). Treat a stored result file (`figures-validation.json`, `results-perf.json`, `ephemeris_agreement.json`, `check_environment_results.json`) as a fresh run (U-21). Start more than the lock allows (U-22).

### 1.7 Thresholds

"Never lower a threshold to pass; thresholds are set at gate creation from the vertical slice and changed only by owner decision" (p. 136, Q). The plan prints a number only where section 10 cites a page. For the others, section 10 lists the threshold id, the plan's words, the value in code, the value adopted and the decision id. A runner reads `gate_results/thresholds.json`; a threshold with no decision id makes the gate INCOMPLETE (`threshold not recorded`), never PASS. A value may be tightened by a recorded stricter one and may not be loosened without a recorded owner decision. A value taken from a measurement (frame time, reprojection error) is re-measured at the first run on the real GPU and the stricter of measured and recorded applies.

### 1.8 Where things run

| Class | Meaning here |
|---|---|
| `python` | python 3.13 in this container (numpy, Pillow, shapely, pyproj and OpenCV present; scipy absent; astropy only in the scratch environment `tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy`, created from PyPI, never in the system python). |
| `node` | node 22 in this container, no browser. |
| `swgl` | A browser through `tools/horde/bench.sh`: Chrome for Testing 153 with software rendering (SwiftShader; WebGL2 and a fallback WebGPU adapter). A frame costs 4 to 5 s on the placeholder crowd, so picture grids are chunked, cached and resumed (U-26). Playwright is preinstalled and never installed. A browser run of hours reads a frozen copy of the tree, not the live one that other workstreams keep editing: `BT_SNAPSHOT=<folder>` serves a `git archive` of one commit and `BT_DIST=<folder>` a packed staging dist (`app/tests/lib.mjs`, SPEC-B 1.1), and the gate's record names the commit or the dist it ran on. |
| `gpu` | The owner's GPU (`GOVERNANCE/hardware_profile.json`, NVIDIA RTX PRO 1000 laptop GPU). **NOT RUNNABLE HERE**: a runner records NOT-RUN with that reason, claims no frame time and lists the part as owed. |

Disk: free space was 7.0 GB at 01:04 UTC on 14 November (83 % used); scratch trees stay under `tmp/` and below about 1 GB per task.

**Measured at the fourth pass of `GATE-SPEC-B.md` and again for this file (14 November, 00:09 to 01:05 UTC).** python 3.13.18, node 22.23.4 and Chrome for Testing 153.0.8010.12 are unchanged by the container restarts of about 23:22 and 00:05 UTC; numpy 2.5.3, Pillow 12.3.0, shapely 2.2.0, pyproj 3.8.0 and OpenCV 5.0.0 are present and scipy is absent (checked again for this file); astropy, skyfield, ephem, matplotlib, PyMuPDF and pytesseract are absent from the system python, and the scratch environment `tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy` (astropy 8.0.2) was still there at the fourth pass (read from `GATE-SPEC-B.md`). The browser probe of the fourth pass (`spec-b-evidence/pass4/browser_caps.out`) found the same SwiftShader renderer, `MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE` 8192, a WebGPU adapter `google swiftshader` with `isFallbackAdapter` true and `timestamp-query`, `EXT_color_buffer_float` offered and `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` not, 4 hardware threads, and `AudioContext` and `OfflineAudioContext` present (read from `GATE-SPEC-B.md`, not repeated). The load average was 14 to 16 at 00:12 and 00:23 and 8.6 at 01:04, so every wall time quoted from `GATE-SPEC-B.md` is an upper bound.

## 2. The 28 gates

Every gate section has the same parts. **Text**: the gate's words from the Section Q table (p. 136) as stored in `gates.json`. **Definition**: the property split into numbered checks Qnn.k with the pages they come from. **Item and per-item pass rule**: what one item is and when its `result` is PASS (COVERED, where K3 allows it, is stated). **Runner**: inputs, command, evidence recorded, weight. **Runs**: renderer classes and what is owed. **Gates**: the sub-requirements whose `gate_ids` list it (counted from `requirements.json`) and the further ones it judges by the domain rules of 3.2 and section 4. **State**: what exists at the time that line names (2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14), from SPEC-B unless this file says it re-ran it; a runner never takes a count from a State line, it reads the files when it runs. All commands are `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Qnn` (1.6); "heavy" means it goes through `tools/horde/bench.sh` in the background (U-22). Test titles quoted from `app/tests` are those of `GATE-SPEC-B.md` section 4 (verified there against the files).

**At a glance.**

| Gate | Item | COVERED branch (K3) | Runs | Weight | Owed or not runnable here |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q01 | unit, check, scenario | no | python, node | light | needs the verified OOB table |
| Q02 | datum class, check | no | python, node | heavy | census of every datum class |
| Q03 | manifest, structure, ground class, regression case | no | python, node, swgl sample | heavy | modern-ground audit |
| Q04 | unit, scenario | no | node, swgl sample | heavy | none |
| Q05 | entity segment, scenario | no (COVERED items keep INCOMPLETE) | node | heavy | wagon, limber and column ranges |
| Q06 | unit, scenario | no | node | light | 108 open-gap leaf units |
| Q07 | object, transition, scenario | yes (flagged window) | python, node, swgl | heavy | real-GPU confirmation of pictures |
| Q08 | work or prepared emplacement, check, scenario | yes (completion flagged) | python, node, swgl | heavy | real-GPU confirmation of pictures |
| Q09 | building part, transition, scenario | no (COVERED items keep INCOMPLETE) | python, node, swgl | heavy | real-GPU confirmation of pictures |
| Q10 | night time (every night grid minute, about 12,000), view, scenario, entity or activity | no | node, swgl | heavy | frames at every night minute (days of locked time here); real-GPU confirmation; two thresholds unset |
| Q11 | date or minute, quantity | no | python (scratch venv), node | heavy | third independent reference; days 7 to 14 July |

### Q01 Unit counts and strengths match the verified OOB table

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Unit counts and strengths match the verified OOB table."
**Definition.** O.1 (p. 135): ingest the order of battle from the Official Records and Busey and Martin, cross-checked with the NPS and Wikipedia lists; "verify every regiment, battery and troop; commander; brigade assignment; and strength"; the coverage test compares unit counts and strengths in the history layer with the verified OOB (Gate Q01); cavalry and horse artillery are first-class units. N (p. 135): carry ranges and sources for every count, never collapse a range without a decision-log entry; soldiers are instanced one-to-one up to the strength figure with no capping. B.1 (p. 9): "Any "number of soldiers to animate" must therefore be a range with a declared basis." K.1 (p. 135): a kit manifest for every regiment, battery, troop and company-level unit including cavalry, horse artillery, engineers, signal, provost, ambulance, ordnance, headquarters and medical staff. D.7 phase 2 exit tests (p. 129): "Unit counts reconcile with OOB; every strength has a source range; no unit without presence window".
- **Q01.1 The table.** `kit/verified_oob.json` exists: per unit id, name, army, corps, division, brigade, branch, commander(s), strength as `[low, high]` with declared basis, source ids and tier, and a presence-window reference. Built from the Organization of the Army lists (OR27-1-R009, OR27-2-R424) and the abstracts of returns (OR27-1-R008, OR27-2-R425). Every documented value is `verified_primary` (section 5). Covers every K.1 class.
- **Q01.2 Unit set.** The layer's unit set (drawn and registered not-drawn) is in bijection with the table through an id map, level by level (army, corps, division, brigade, regiment, battalion, battery, troop, staff, medical, signal, provost, ambulance, ordnance). A unit in the layer and not in the table, a table unit missing from the layer, or a mismatch of commander or brigade is FAIL. Units documented as never or only partly on the field (13th and 58th Va, 44th NC and Corse's brigade, Robertson, Jones and Imboden; p. 62) are table rows with that status and are never drawn except in the detached role stated.
- **Q01.3 Strength.** For every unit and every strength scenario the layer's strength is a member of the table's value set or lies in its range, with the same basis; a single printed figure is allowed only as one source's value, labeled; no range is collapsed (today the layer holds 21 printed single figures and procedural shares below them: X-29).
- **Q01.4 Roll-ups.** Sums per brigade, division, corps and army equal the table's roll-ups within the sum of the ranges; army totals lie inside the verified totals. B.1's secondary figures (Union 93,500 to 104,256; Confederate 65,000 to 75,000, possibly 80,000; p. 9) are leads and not the reference.
- **Q01.5 Presence window.** Every unit has an arrivals record with window, place, route and sources (O.2, p. 135; D.7 phase 2).
- **Q01.6 One-to-one.** For every unit, scenario and grid time, rendered instances equal present strength (strength minus those removed or departed); impostor and LOD tiers count as instances; no cap constant exists (shared with OA02).
- **Q01.7 Commander and assignment** equal the table.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (unit, check, scenario); plus the table-level item Q01.1. PASS iff the property of the check holds for that unit in that scenario. No COVERED. A unit or strength the Official Records do not give is a table row with a gap range and an open L3 entry (tier plausible-procedural); Q01 then compares against that flagged value and the S-R stays LINKED-ACTIVE (the unit's strength item is PASS against a flagged value only when the table row says so). With no table every item is BLOCKED-INPUT.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q01` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q01.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the verified table and its id map to `history/units.json` ids; `history/units.json` (726 units), `history/arrivals.json`, `history/not_on_field.json`; the strength scenarios of the layer; the local Official Records text (`tmp/library/or27`, `library/or27_index.json`) for the extract re-match of Q01.1. Python (`q01_oob.py`) compares table and layer; a node child (`q01_layer.mjs`: `loadRawFromDisk`, `createRealHistory`, `nodeWorld`/`worldFromUnits`) dumps per scenario the layer strengths and the instance count per unit and time. Weight light. Evidence: table row reference, layer value, scenario, basis, roll-up sums. The table is built by `python3 tools/bt/kit_verified.py build --out-dir <scratch>` (never without `--out-dir`: it then writes `kit/verified_oob.json` and `kit/verified_batteries.json`, which are outside this workstream, 1.6) or read from `kit/verified_oob.json` once its owner writes it; `python3 tools/bt/kit_evidence_check.py --all --quiet` is an input to Q01.1 (its accepted evidence lines), never a verdict (U-28). Existing self-checks (`check_arrivals.py`, `history.test.mjs`, `ledgerE.test.mjs`, `anim3d-onetoone.test.mjs`, `casualties.test.mjs`) compare the layer with its own source (the plan's B.2.4 and B.3 tables) and are evidence only (U-28).
**Runs.** python, node; no GPU.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R04.0000, R10.0000 (2 rows); also the populations R10.U-* and R17.U-* when generated (4.11) and OA02 (Q01.6).
**State** (re-run for this file at 01:04 UTC). `kit/verified_oob.json` absent: every item BLOCKED-INPUT, verdict INCOMPLETE. `python3 tools/bt/kit_evidence_check.py --all --quiet` (exit 0, 13.5 s): `us-1c` 445 lines, 424 accepted (392 documented, 32 inferred), 0 rejected, 0 errors; `us-2c-1d` 265 lines, 235 accepted (229 documented, 6 inferred), 21 superseded; the other 24 of the 26 research groups have no evidence file. `python3 tools/bt/kit_verified.py build --out-dir <scratch>` (exit 0, 13.1 s): `verified_oob` 825 units, 645 evidence lines used, 41 verified, 27 partly, 757 unverified; `verified_batteries` 131 batteries in 33 formations, 0 verified, 131 unverified. `kit/evidence/INVENTORY.md` (rebuilt 00:00 UTC, read from SPEC-B): strength 0 documented, 21 inferred, 545 plausible-procedural, 250 empty. `history/undrawn/roster-gaps.json` lists 125 units of the OR organization tables that the roster lacks or splits; none applied, `units.json` still holds 726.

### Q02 Provenance completeness

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Provenance completeness: no datum without source and tier; no documented tier without a source extract."
**Definition.** D (p. 134): the history layer's tables are `units, people, places, events, orders, positions, movements, activities, arrivals, departures, equipment_items, kit_manifests, uniform_specs, ordnance, structures, terrain_features, vegetation_states, weather_states, sources, claims, claim_support, conflicts, register_entries`. P.1 (p. 136): "No datum without provenance enters the history layer or the product. Render-time flags carry the tier into the engine." App. B (pp. 138-139) rules; E.2 and E.7 (p. 134).
- **Q02.1 Census.** Every datum class the page loads (the history tables above, the data files behind the shell's load list, every audio sample, texture, model and clip, and every interface text that states a fact) is in a datum-class registry with its provenance mapping. The census is checked by diffing the page's load list (`app/src/data.mjs loadHistoryData`, the `URLS` of `app/src/config.mjs`, the modules of `app/src/ext/index.mjs`) against the registry; a class loaded and absent from the registry is FAIL.
- **Q02.2 Tier and source.** Every datum has a tier in {documented, inferred, plausible-procedural} and a source id, or the reasoning of an inferred datum, or the `procedural` block of a plausible-procedural one. Open gaps are register entries and render as gap markers, not as data.
- **Q02.3 Documented needs an extract.** tier documented: a source whose type is not `encyclopedia-lead`, `forum-lead`, blog, wiki or auction page; a `quote_or_extract` under 15 words that the runner finds in the stored source text at the cited page or span; every number of the claim inside the span (citation checker, p. 126, D.4.3 stage 11); and the claim equals what the span supports.
- **Q02.4 Inferred** has `reasoning` whose premises are documented data with claim ids. **Q02.5 Plausible-procedural** has the complete `procedural` block and, where the gap affects a requirement, an open register id.
- **Q02.6 Sources.** Each source id resolves in the Source Manifest with `date_written`, `rights`, `reliability_tier`; copyrighted works store spans and pointers, not text (App. B).
- **Q02.7 Conflicts.** Every conflict lists all values with sources (B.2.5); feeds class CF.
- **Q02.8 Quarantine.** No documented datum rests on a lead alone (E.7); a lead-supported datum is inferred or plausible-procedural and flagged weak.
- **Q02.9 Validity.** Every kit item has `valid_from` and `valid_to`; every structure, fence, field and vegetation state change has a `state_schedule` (App. B).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (datum class, check), judged by an exhaustive scan of every member of the class (rows that number in the hundreds of thousands are not listed one by one: the item carries `scanned`, `failing` and a sidecar of failing ids; K10). PASS iff the census is complete and the scan finds zero failing members. A missing source is never COVERED: the datum is removed, re-tiered with its procedural block, or shown as a gap marker. The existing test is weaker than Q02.3 (it checks that a documented row's source cell is not empty, and that tier and source fields exist); the extract re-match is new work (X-22).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q02` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q02` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q02.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the datum-class registry (`tools/bt/gates/datum_classes.json`, written by the census), all loaded data, the claim and claim-support ledgers, the Source Manifest, the local texts (`tmp/library/or27`, `ws9`, `web`). Python census and scan (`q02_provenance.py`) plus a node child (`q02_contract.mjs`: `app/src/history/contract.mjs` `validateLayer`, `validateUnits`, `validateArrivals`, `validateEvents`, `validateKit`, `validateProvenance`, `validateDayPositions`, `validateCasualties`, `validateGuns`). It imports `check_kit.py` item 5 (tier completeness) and `check_structures.py` INTEGRITY, and `history/undrawn/tools/check_undrawn.py` and `fidelity/tools/check_quotes.py` where a group file exists. It also reuses, as functions and never as commands that write, three validators that `GATE-SPEC-B.md` found at its third pass: `tools/bt/validate_provenance.py` (`validate(datum, visible_result=True)`: a documented datum needs a non-lead source with a supporting extract of under 15 words and, for a visible result, two sources whose roots through `independent_of` and `derived_from` do not overlap or a single-source flag; an inferred datum needs reasoning; a plausible-procedural datum needs a procedural block and a register id; `render_flag` 0, 1 or 2; run as a command it runs only its own self-test and validates no file, so nothing feeds it yet), `tools/bt/verify_positions.py <A to E> [sample] [seed]` (counts by tier and kind, every unit id in `units.json`, a quote on every documented claim, none of 15 words or more, a sample of quotes re-verified by `or27_find.py --verify`) and `tools/bt/terrain_check.py` (the R12 catalogue: 45 rows, 71 URLs). Their output is input to an item and never the item's result (U-28); the extract re-match of Q02.3 is the shared function of section 5, run over every documented datum and not over a sample of 12. The extract matcher is one shared function (`tools/bt/gates/lib/extracts.py`, section 5). Weight heavy. Evidence: per class `scanned`, `failing`, first 20 failing ids, sidecar path and sha256.
**Runs.** python, node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R03.0000 and R03.C-001..C-047 (48 rows; Q02.7 and the CF rules of 4.8).
**State.** No single scan over every datum class exists (SPEC-B). Position ledgers (`history/positions/*.jsonl`, 3,703 lines in five files) and four troop-map files (484 claims) carry a verified quote per claim; kit items, structures rows and weather readings carry source keys, not extracts; the 47 conflict rows carry labels with source keys in brackets, no extract. Expect FAIL on Q02.1 (no census) and Q02.3. Re-run at 00:12 to 00:14 UTC (read from SPEC-B pass 4, all exit 0): `validate_provenance.py` self-test OK; `verify_positions.py A` to `E` with sample 12 and seed 3 (688, 1,864, 538, 413 and 200 claims, none missing; ledger E 61 documented and 139 inferred); `check_undrawn.py --all` (635 records, 0 errors, 1 warning); `kit_evidence_check.py --all` (0 errors); `terrain_check.py` OK. None of these is a scan over every datum class. The correction files cover 5 of the 47 `R03.C-` rows with 9 rows in `evidence_corrections/web-lookups.jsonl` and `web-newspapers.jsonl` (all partially supported).

### Q03 No anachronisms

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "No anachronisms. Every item in every manifest is valid on its date and for its unit (dress, arms, badges, flags, equipment, vehicles, structures); every 1863 structure exists on its date; no modern object (monuments, avenues, fences, signs, wires, markers) appears."
**Definition.** K.9 (p. 135): every kit item has `valid_from` and `valid_to`; the build rejects items outside July 1863 for the unit. B.6.12 validation test (p. 76): seven failure classes. E.4 (p. 134): the known corrections. B.7.3 (p. 92) pre-existing features; B.7.6 (p. 94) and B.8.3 (p. 97) the modern rebuilt walls; B.8.7 (p. 102) "no avenue exists before the park era"; G (p. 134) reconcile modern and historic ground.
- **Q03.1 Kit validator over every soldier drawn** (not only the unit specs), the seven classes of p. 76: (1) presence (a soldier whose unit has not yet arrived or has already left is absent at that time); (2) the anachronism list (Type III Richmond Depot jackets; M1863 rifle-musket; Henry rifle; Spencer carbine on any unit before June 30 or on any unit other than the 5th Michigan (all) and part of the 6th Michigan after that date; Zouave items on the 5th NY, 140th NY, 155th PA or any Louisiana soldier; Berdan green on any other unit; Wheat's Tiger or Coppens' Zouave dress; green hatband on the Irish Brigade or medics; Whitworth rifle in infantry hands by default; kepi on a Confederate private by default); (3) wrong-unit items (every weapon equals the unit assignment or the documented default distribution for that army; every badge shape belongs to the soldier's corps; every uniform item belongs to the unit's spec; no Union soldier carries a Confederate item); (4) branch correctness (artillery scarlet, cavalry yellow, infantry sky blue; the green band only for the Ambulance Corps); (5) tier completeness (every field has a tier, a field with no tier fails, "a documented field must have a source key"); (6) reproducibility (regenerating the manifest from the same seed yields identical kit); (7) a seeded sample audit of soldiers per unit rendered on a turntable and checked against the unit spec image references. Validator flags (`DISPUTED_FLAGGED`, `FLAG_BADGE_UNRESOLVED`, `FLAG_KEPI_PRIVATE_PURCHASE`) are acceptable only when every variant passes and the flag is marked (Q23) and registered; a validator failure is logged to the register "rather than waived" and the gate stays FAIL.
- **Q03.2 Validity on the date.** Every item is valid on each scene date of U-06, including 1863-11-18 and 19 for the epilogue crowd and Lincoln. The national colour's star count is checked against the statute for each scene date (the figures evidence records 34 through July 3 and 35 from July 4; the plan prints no star count); the ANV battle-flag star count (12 or 13) is a selectable alternative (DD-01).
- **Q03.3 Structures exist on their date.** Every drawn building, fence, wall, work, road, bridge and hospital has an existence interval and a state schedule; none is present before it was built or after it was destroyed or removed (the Rose barn burned in 1910, the Codori barn came down in 1882, the McPherson house burned in 1895: all stand throughout).
- **Q03.4 No modern object**, checked twice: (a) the object registry (any object with `valid_from` after the scene date is FAIL, and the scene's draw lists contain no object of a class absent in 1863: monuments, avenues, modern roads and lots, post-1863 buildings, towers, signs, wires and poles, modern fences, markers); (b) a ground audit: the terrain and aerial layers are classified and every modern feature is removed, painted out or replaced by the 1863 reconstruction layer with a recorded correction (G.3, p. 134). A disclosure that `the ground is the modern landscape` is not a pass (A-33, X-11). The global assertion that no avenue exists before the park era (p. 102, B.8.7) applies.
- **Q03.5 Pre-existing features** (class PE): present at frame one, never constructed.
- **Q03.6 Regression set** of the known corrections (p. 134, E.4; p. 126, D.4.3 stage 12), each its own item: 5th NY not at Gettysburg; 140th NY regulation blue; 146th NY and 114th PA Zouave; McPherson barn not burned on July 1; Bliss house and barn burned the morning of July 3 by the 14th Connecticut; the Angle wall pre-existing; Spencers not on Buford's line on July 1; Cope's survey August to October 1863; the Devil's Den photograph staged on July 6; Louisiana regiments as ordinary ANV infantry. The set must still pass after every data change.
- **Q03.7 Vehicles and equipment.** Limbers, caissons, wagons, ambulances, forge and battery wagon (B.6.14.4, p. 80) are types valid for 1863 and for the army.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a manifest group (unit, variant), a structure, a ground feature class, a regression case. PASS iff the check holds; disputed items are carried as variants and each variant passes. No COVERED. Q03 cannot pass while Q03.4(b) is unmet.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q03` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q03` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q03.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `kit/*.json`, `app/data/kit-crosswalk.json`, `figures-validation.json`, `structures/*.json`, `app/data/structures3d.json`, the ground layers and their classification (`GOVERNANCE/ground_audit/`, to be produced by the terrain work), the correction regression set. Python (`q03_anachronism.py`) imports `check_kit.manifest_for` and `validate_manifest` over all 23,112 generated manifests and runs `check_figures.py` without `--write` (the stored `figures-validation.json` is a file, not a fresh run); a node child (`q03_scene.mjs`) reads the scene's draw lists and the ext hooks (`sceneObjectsAt`, `checkLaterMemorials`, `checkTourBookmarks` of `app/src/ext/site-places.mjs`); a swgl child renders the turntable sample (heavy). Evidence: validator FAIL and FLAG counts by code, per variant results, registry diff, audit file hash.
**Runs.** python, node; the sample audit on swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R13.P-01..P-14, R13.0000, R02.0000, R04.0000, R10.0000, R17.0000 (19 rows); also the structure rows R13.<id> when generated.
**State.** `check_kit.py`: 0 ERROR and 4 WARN, 45 of 45 self-tests, 23,112 manifests validated; `check_figures.py` exit 0 (SPEC-B 7). Open: no scan of the scene for modern objects of the other classes; `app/src/config.mjs GROUND_NOTE` says the ground is `the modern landscape (2022 aerial photograph and 3D Elevation Program terrain), not the 1863 landscape`. Expect Q03.4(b) FAIL (no audit), verdict FAIL or INCOMPLETE. Pass 4 of SPEC-B (00:12 to 00:19 UTC, read from that file and not re-run for this one): `ext-loader.test.mjs` 28 tests, `ext-monuments.test.mjs` 38, `ext-tour.test.mjs` 13, `ext-site-data.test.mjs` 14 and `ext-site-places-loader.test.mjs` 5 pass; `node app/tests/ext-site-source.mjs --check` (the copies equal the site's data in 10 trees) and `ext-site-years.mjs --check` (33 memorials named in the Park Service reports by the site's own name, each with its year, tier, report and page) pass; `manifest.test.mjs` 6 of 6 pass although the four `kit/*.json` tables were rewritten at 23:56 UTC, because the stored `figures-validation.json` still carries the digest of what the shell draws, and `check_figures.py` without `--write` (locked job, 00:46 UTC) printed `check_figures: OK` after the kit rewrites of 23:56 and 00:21; `check_kit.py --quiet` gave the same figures as before. The vegetation layer adds a test of this gate's kind for trees (`vegetation.test.mjs`: the shipped data names no later tree as a tree of 1863). The correction files cover 12 of the 14 `R13.P` rows (`terrain-2.jsonl` has row-level records for `R13.P-01` to `R13.P-09`, counted at 01:05 UTC: 9 of 14 row-level) and the shared rows R02.0000 and R13.0000 carry the Park Service statements (section 5).

### Q04 Arrival check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Arrival check. No entity of a unit exists in the scene before its arrival window opens; scan every frame range of every scenario."
**Definition.** O.2 (pp. 135-136): "No unit, soldier, horse, gun, wagon or flag of a unit appears in the scene before its arrival window opens. Ranges are carried: a unit is not shown before the earliest documented arrival time unless an owner decision chooses another scenario." Units whose arrival is unrecorded get an inferred window from their documented march, with a register entry; before arrival the unit may exist only as an approaching column on the road network at the correct place and time. The day-2 arrivals (V Corps, parts of III and VI Corps, Longstreet's divisions, Stuart's cavalry on the evening of July 2) are absent before then; the B.2.4 ledger is "the arrival source of truth; conflicts are scenarios". B.2.5 gating rules (p. 63): never-on-field units are not drawn; Stuart's brigades gated to the evening of July 2 (Hampton's rear guard and Hunterstown exempt); Pickett to the morning of July 3.
- **Q04.1 Absolute invariant.** In every scenario of S*, presence is zero for every entity class (1.5) of unit u at every grid time earlier than the minimum over all documented values of u's arrival window.
- **Q04.2 Scenario-local invariant.** Presence in scenario s is zero before the arrival window start that s selects (A-35).
- **Q04.3 Entry.** The first presence of a unit is an approaching column that enters the loaded ground at the road and time its route gives, not a deployed unit.
- **Q04.4 Departure.** After a documented departure the unit leaves by a march or is replaced by its dead and wounded where documented; an entity never vanishes.
- **Q04.5 Unknown arrivals.** Not earlier than the next documented event that requires the unit; tier inferred; register entry.
- **Q04.6 Named cases.** V Corps, II Corps, Longstreet, Stuart's brigades, Pickett, the VI Corps; units never or partly on the field.
- **Q04.7 Effects.** A sound, shot, smoke or casualty caused by a unit's action does not exist before the unit's arrival.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (unit, scenario) with the entity classes inside; granularity may be (unit, entity class, scenario) where a class fails. PASS iff presence is zero for every class at every grid time before the earliest documented arrival (Q04.1) and before the scenario's own window (Q04.2), and Q04.3 to Q04.7 hold. A unit that is not drawn never appears, so the scan says nothing about it (that is Q06 and Q17 debt). No COVERED; the arrival time of a unit with no record is an inferred window and the check runs against it. A FAIL item lists the first failing (class, time, scenario).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q04` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q04` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q04.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `history/units.json`, `arrivals.json`, `not_on_field.json`, the conflict scenarios (`app/src/scenario-defaults.mjs defaultChoices`, `pdfChoices`, every value of `layer.meta.linkedConflicts` and their combinations, 1.5), the layer, the draw lists, the audio and effect events. A node child (`q04_arrival.mjs`: `loadRawFromDisk`, `createRealHistory`, `layer.both(scenario)`, the stage tables, `Structures.drawLists` for guns and train, horses, casualties, effects, sounds) scans the grid; python cross-checks with `check_arrivals.presence_ok` and `gate_time`; `sim/cache.mjs EventCache.add` refusals are recorded. A swgl child runs `arrival.spec.mjs` on a sample of times with `BT_OUT=gates-q04` (pictures confirm that the renderer's table equals the stage's). Weight heavy. Evidence: scenario count and group structure, grid size, per unit first presence minute per class.
**Runs.** node; picture sample swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R16.0000, R17.0000 (2 rows). The check runs over every unit and every entity class although only these two rows carry Q04; a TL row passes only when Q04 holds for the units it realizes (4.1, TL-6); R17.U-* when generated.
**State.** Python and JS gates agree and per-class tests pass (`history.test.mjs`, `unit.test.mjs`, `claims`, `daypos`, `sibling`, `ledgerE`, `casualties`, `structures-ws6`, `cavalry-ws10`, `fx3d`, `arrival.spec`); they sample seven scenarios, not S* (X-23). Pass 4 of SPEC-B (00:12 UTC): no code or test of this gate changed; `check_arrivals.py` exit 0 (726 units, 44 rows, 9 sub-unit gates, 56 gates, 4 unparsed cells) and `sim.test.mjs` 6 of 6; the specs' owner has one browser pass of this gate, the test of a unit that has not arrived (`arrival.spec.mjs`, 935 s on a frozen copy of commit 9e1e1777), and no result for the other five tests of that spec (SPEC-B 1.1).

### Q05 Position-time plausibility

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Position-time plausibility: speeds on foot, horse, limber, wagon and in column within documented ranges; no teleport; continuous trajectories."
**Definition.** M.1 (p. 135): drill motion is encoded from the Hardee and Casey manuals; O.2 (p. 136): march rate checked (Gate Q05). The plan prints no speeds; the documented ranges are the drill manuals (common time 90 steps of 28 in. a minute, quick time and route step 110 of 28 in., double quick 165 of 33 in., urgent 180: `app/data/anim3d/drill-timing.json`) and the horse and horse-artillery gait tables (Wheeler 1863, Cooper 1861, Instruction for Field Artillery 1863: `gait-sources.json`).
- **Q05.1 Speed ranges by mover class.** Mover classes: foot individual, foot column, foot line (wheel and deploy), cavalry mount, horse-artillery team, limber and gun team, caisson, wagon and ambulance, stretcher team, led horses. Each has a documented range with a source for its declared gait. At every grid step no entity's speed exceeds the upper bound of its class and gait; a segment declared constant-gait has its mean speed inside the gait's documented range at both ends (A-32, X-10).
- **Q05.2 No teleport.** Displacement between consecutive steps is at most `v_max * dt` plus the position resolution, for every entity in every scenario; the only discontinuities are documented entries and exits off the loaded ground and mount and dismount at the same place.
- **Q05.3 Continuous trajectories.** Each path begins where the previous ended within 1 m (T-Q05-1) or at the unit's entry; heading change is bounded by the formation's extent (a rigid train does not turn faster than its far end can move).
- **Q05.4 Documented time against documented rate.** Where documented place and time imply a rate above the class bound the layer keeps the bound, reports the lateness as an offset and holds an open entry; it never speeds or teleports to meet the time. The item is COVERED and keeps the verdict INCOMPLETE (K3, X-09).
- **Q05.5 Missing ranges.** A mover class with no documented range (the plan gives none for wagons, limbers or "in column") has no range to test against: its item is BLOCKED-INPUT until an open L3 entry is registered and the conservative bound (the foot range) is applied with tier inferred and recorded in the evidence; with both in place the item is COVERED until a source is read, and the verdict stays INCOMPLETE.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (entity or unit, segment, scenario). PASS iff Q05.1 to Q05.3 hold. Q05.4 and Q05.5 give COVERED at best. Nothing may be registered in place of a speed, teleport or continuity failure.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q05` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q05` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q05.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the layer's path events and stage tables, `SPEED` of `app/src/history/real.mjs` (today `{ march: 1.25, double_quick: 4000 * 0.9144 / (25 * 60) }` m/s, asserted as `v <= SPEED.double_quick * 1.0001 + 1e-9`), `drill-timing.json`, `gait-sources.json`, `kit` train data, `layer.report(scenario)` (`offsets`, `state_offsets`, `defects`). Node (`q05_speed.mjs`), heavy over S*. Evidence: per segment class, bound and measured maximum, offsets with their register ids.
**Runs.** node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R17.0000 (1 row); R17.U-* when generated; CF downstream (4.8 CF-6).
**State.** The tests bound every mover by the foot double-quick and check the upper bound only; no wagon rate exists in `app/src` or `app/data` (SPEC-B). No input of this gate had changed by 00:2x UTC on 14 November (`app/src/history/`, `app/src/stage.mjs`, `app/src/config.mjs` and `docs/battle-theatre/sim/` have no file modified since 18:30 UTC on 13 November, by modification time); the pop test of the scrubber spec (no unit pops while playing at 60x) has no result because its run was killed by the container restart (SPEC-B 1.1).

### Q06 March-to-position check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "March-to-position check. Every unit has a continuous documented or inferred path from its previous position to its fighting position; no unit appears already deployed."
**Definition.** O.2 (p. 136): "Arrival is a march, not a spawn." Units march from their previous documented position to their fighting position along documented or inferred routes (roads, lanes, fields), at documented or plausible rates, in column of march, then deploy into line or the documented formation, then occupy their position. D.3.4 (p. 125); D.7 phase 7 exit tests (p. 129): "Per-unit time continuity; no unit before arrival; no unit in two places".
- **Q06.1 Path chain.** For every unit with a fighting position, an ordered chain of legs from its previous position (documented, or the last documented night position, or the edge of the loaded ground for a unit coming from outside) to the fighting position, each with route, times, rate (Q05), tier and sources, continuous in space and time.
- **Q06.2 Not deployed on appearance.** The first presence is column of march; a deployment event with a formation change precedes line; then occupation. The deployment duration comes from the drill manuals where they give one; the repository holds none (DEC-008 uses 90 s as a tuning value), so it is plausible-procedural, marked and registered (A-38, T-Q06-1).
- **Q06.3 Every unit.** Every unit of the verified OOB that was on the field has a chain. A unit with no documented previous position gets an inferred chain from the ledger (nearest road of its line of command, sibling roads, EG-0070), tier inferred or plausible-procedural, marked. A unit with no path is FAIL: a path can always be inferred, and "too many units" is never a reason (Law cl. 1) (X-20).
- **Q06.4 Departure** by a march in reverse (Q04.4). **Q06.5 Outside the map.** A previous position outside the loaded ground becomes an entry at the edge at the correct place and time.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (unit, scenario). PASS iff the chain exists, is continuous and its legs are documented or inferred with their tier shown. No COVERED (the gate text itself allows "documented or inferred").
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q06` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q06.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `layer.units[].render` (`moving`, `edge_only`, `not_drawn`, `aggregate`, `not_on_field`) and `approach`, `layer.plans`, `app/data/placement.json`, `sibling-approach.json`, `daypositions.json`, `history/undrawn/status.json`, the ledger, and the four group files of `history/undrawn/` (`us-1c-1d-art.jsonl`, `us-1c-2d-3d.jsonl`, `us-2c-1d-art.jsonl`, `us-hq-artres.jsonl`) read through `python3 docs/battle-theatre/history/undrawn/tools/check_undrawn.py --all` as a function or a command (it writes nothing): a record the checker refuses is not accepted, and a record that the layer has not consumed gives a unit no path (the checker's verdict is input and never the item's result, U-28). Node (`q06_path.mjs`), light. Evidence: per unit the leg list with tier, or the reason there is none.
**Runs.** node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R17.0000 (1 row); R17.U-* when generated.
**State.** Layer v8 draws 439 of 726 units; 108 open-gap leaf units are not drawn and 21 are edge-only (`history/undrawn/status.json`, SPEC-B): each is an item with no path, FAIL at gate level and LINKED-ACTIVE at S-R level only with an L3 entry. No `<group>.jsonl` evidence record exists yet to build their paths. What the four research files hold moved while this file was written, which is why the runner reads them at run time: at 00:13 UTC they held 635 records for 85 units with 0 errors and 1 warning, and 2 of the 85 units (4 of 357 items) were still owed a word, because `groups.json` was rebuilt at 23:49 (SPEC-B pass 4); at 01:05 UTC `check_undrawn.py --all` (exit 1, 14.5 s) found 940 records (296, 217, 137 and 290), 1 error (`us-hq-artres.jsonl` line 258: a statement is required) and 6 warnings, in a file its owner was still extending. `history/undrawn/status.json` still has the layer's v8 counts (726 units: 165 aggregate, 418 moving, 108 not drawn, 21 edge only, 14 not on the field; 127,572 figures drawn), so no merge of the group files into the layer build has been made (`app/README.md`, section `Units the layer does not draw`).

### Q07 Fence and wall state-change times

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Fence and wall state-change times. Each damaged, dismantled, burned or flattened fence, wall or gate changes state at its documented time window (or is flagged with an open register entry); intact before, damaged after, and the transition is visible."
**Definition.** V.2 (p. 137): fences are modular segments (post-and-rail, worm or snake rail, board, stone wall) with a state schedule "intact, rails pulled for breastworks or fuel, rails knocked down by shot, flattened by marching units, burned"; each state change has a time window and a source; "the animation shows the damage events (soldiers pulling rails, shell strikes, crossing and breaking of fences, for example along the Emmitsburg Road) at the right time (Gate Q07)". B.8.3 (p. 97) fence types; B.8.7 (pp. 100-102) schedule; B.8.8 (p. 102) variants (fence segments intact, splintered, breached, missing; stone walls intact, scattered stones, "seeded per segment"). G.7 (p. 134): every fence segment and wall is a `structures` row with a state schedule keyed to time. D.2 V7 test (p. 124): render a fence before and after its damage event.
- **Q07.1 Registry.** Every fence, wall and gate of the 1863 landscape (class P ids, `FEN-` records, the B.8.3 types, gates, yard pickets) has a `state_schedule`: for each damaged, dismantled, burned or flattened event the sources document, a window `[earliest, latest, default]`, trigger, tier and source, or an open register entry that flags the unresolved window.
- **Q07.2 Order.** `intact` before the window start, `to_state` after its end, windows not reversed, states in a legal order. **Q07.3 Pure state and scrubbing.** `state_at(object, t, scenario)` is a pure function of time; backward scrubbing reverses every transition (p. 100, B.8.7).
- **Q07.4 Visible.** The draw state before the window start differs from the draw state after its end, and an intermediate or progressive state exists strictly inside any window longer than one minute; for a point change the visible span is the product's declared transition duration (T-Q07-1, DD-G02); confirmed on pixels for a software-GL sample and on draw lists for all.
- **Q07.5 Cause events.** The plan's damage events appear in the window (men pulling rails, shell strikes, crossing and breaking, burning); an unsourced cause is shown plausible-procedural and marked.
- **Q07.6 Named assertion.** The Emmitsburg Road fences are not destroyed before July 2 to 3 (p. 102): intact at the step-off, splintered or breached inside the windows of S-062 and its neighbours. **Q07.7 Scenarios.** Every alternative window is selectable and passes. **Q07.8 Drawn.** Every fence or wall that has a documented state change is drawn; a record with no geometry is FAIL (Law cl. 1) and is registered as an unfinished build.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (object, transition, scenario). PASS iff Q07.2 to Q07.8 hold with a documented window. COVERED (allowed by the gate text) iff the window is unresolved and an L3 entry, the visible flag and the plain-reading window are present. An object that is not drawn is FAIL until built. The gate verdict PASS counts COVERED items.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q07` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q07` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q07.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `structures/structures.json` (16 `FEN-` and 14 `P-` records), `state_schedule.json` (88 rows), `app/data/structures3d.json`, the draw lists, the Warren, Bachelder and NPS sources, the evidence-correction rows `evidence_corrections/fence-*.jsonl`.
