Python (`q07_fence.py`) imports `check_structures.state_at` and `check_q07`; node (`q07_state.mjs`) evaluates the JS `state_at` buffer per object at every grid time and bound ±1 minute; a swgl child (`q07_pixels.mjs`, `BT_OUT=gates-q07`) draws before, inside and after for each fence of a sample. Evidence: per object the schedule row, window, draw-state digests before, inside, after, saved image paths with `looked_at`.
**Runs.** python, node; pictures swgl (real-GPU confirmation owed).
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R14.S-001..S-088, R14.0000, R02.0000, R13.0000 (91 rows); an S row is judged by this gate for its fence objects (3.2).
**State.** `check_structures.py check_q07` PASS (191 checks); `structures3d.spec.mjs` shows one fence breached in pixels; 30 fence and wall lines are drawn and the README lists 362 of 392 fence candidates as not drawn (SPEC-B). Expect FAIL (objects not drawn) until built. Pass 4 of SPEC-B (00:12 UTC): `check_structures.py` exit 0 with the same figures (INTEGRITY 649 checks, Q08 186, Q07 191, Q09 1,306, scrub 1,441; RESULT: ALL PASS); `structures/` and `app/data/structures3d.json` unchanged since 4 November. The correction files now cover every schedule row of this gate (counted for this file at 01:05 UTC: all 88 `R14.S` rows have a row-level record, 32 of them also supplementary ones; `fence-1.jsonl` S-001 to S-016, `fence-2.jsonl` S-017 to S-052, `fence-3.jsonl` S-053 to S-088, and `web-nps-landscape.jsonl` and `web-lookups.jsonl` add supplementary rows). None changed `structures.json`, so `check_q07` still tests the schedule as the plan printed it. OA10 is mapped to this gate by DEC-OA10-12 (3.1 OA-G10).

### Q08 No pre-placed earthworks

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "No pre-placed earthworks. Every earthwork, breastwork and gun emplacement is absent before its construction start, passes through documented construction states (staking, digging, piling, completion), and carries a work crew in the construction interval; completion times match sources or are flagged."
**Definition.** B.7.1 (p. 90): "No earthwork, breastwork, barricade, lunette or gun pit is pre-placed in the scene." Every work is constructed on screen by the unit that built it inside the window the sources give; a work whose builder or time the notes cannot give is still built on screen by a labeled stand-in crew and flagged "builder unknown". B.7.4 (p. 93): stages 0 bare ground, 1 layout, 2 felling and gathering, 3 stacking, 4 head logs and concealment, 5 completed, 6 damaged; "the state is a function of scenario time, so scrubbing backward un-builds the work". V.3 (p. 137): construction state machine (layout, digging or log laying, parapet build-up, headlog and traverse where documented, completion). B.7.5 (pp. 93-94) engine rules for G rows; B.7.6 (p. 94): no profile from modern rebuilt works.
- **Q08.1 Absent before start.** For every work of the construction type and every prepared emplacement (G-03, G-12 and those DD-04 builds) in every scenario, progress is 0 and no mesh exists at every grid time before the start window; an unknown start is the earliest time the builder's presence or the row allows, flagged.
- **Q08.2 Documented states, in order.** The work passes through the stages in order (staking = layout, digging = felling or digging, piling = stacking or parapet build-up, completion), plus head logs, traverse and extension where the row documents them; stage shares and offsets are plausible-procedural, seeded and marked (A-14).
- **Q08.3 Crew.** During every interval in which progress changes at least one crew is present and working at the work: the builder unit's soldiers (present by Q04) or the stand-in crew flagged "builder unknown"; tools as printed (E-02: about a dozen spades, the tool count capped at 12 for that regiment, p. 93, T-Q08-1) (A-51).
- **Q08.4 Completion.** The completion time equals the source's window (E-05: start about 06:00, end 09:00 to 10:00 as default, outer bounds about 10:00 and about noon) or is flagged with an open entry.
- **Q08.5 No pre-existing work built.** Class PE objects never get construction; rail piles on Cemetery Ridge (E-17) are not animated as documented unless an accepted correction (DD-02) makes them documented; Little Round Top stone breastworks carry the NPS versus Wikipedia dating conflict as scenarios.
- **Q08.6 Emplacements** by B.7.5: prepared built on screen, improvised no earth, "no works described" open ground with a logged gap. **Q08.7 No modern profile**: thickness and profile numbers are labeled assumptions, tier plausible-procedural. **Q08.8 Damage**: stage 6 appears inside its documented window (E-11).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (work or prepared emplacement, check, scenario). PASS iff Q08.1 to Q08.8 hold for the work's type (4.4: construction, occupation, damage, no construction, open gap). COVERED (allowed: "or are flagged") iff the completion time is flagged with an open L3 entry and a visible flag. A work not yet drawn is FAIL until built. The prepared emplacements also pass Q14 (X-12).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q08` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q08` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q08.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `structures.json` (E-01..E-18, G-01..G-18, P-01..P-14), `works_stages.json`, `app/data/works3d.json`, DD-02 to DD-05, the OR reports, `evidence_corrections/fence-1.jsonl` and `fence-2.jsonl`. Python (`q08_works.py`) imports `check_structures.check_q08` and extends its loop to the G rows; node (`q08_state.mjs`) evaluates stage and crew at every grid time per scenario; swgl (`q08_pixels.mjs`) draws the stages of a sample. Weight heavy.
**Runs.** python, node; pictures swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R14.E-01..E-18, R14.0000, R02.0000, R05.0000, R13.0000 (22 rows); also the prepared G rows (4.5) and R14.S rows whose objects are `E-` or prepared `G-` (3.2).
**State.** `check_structures.py check_q08` PASS (186 checks) but loops over E-01..E-18 only; the progress note of 2026-11-11 lists works not drawn (E-10, E-11, E-13, E-15, E-16, E-18, G-12, G-11b, the XII Corps batteries of G-18 and the two P-14 walls); the structures data does not yet follow the evidence corrections (SPEC-B). Pass 4 (00:12 UTC): `check_q08` run again, the same 186 checks, 0 failures; the work data (`structures.json`, `works_stages.json`, `works3d.json`) are unchanged; at 01:05 UTC all 18 `R14.E` rows have a row-level record in `fence-1.jsonl` (partly supplemented by `web-nps-landscape.jsonl`) and `R13.G-01` to `G-16` have row-level records in `terrain-1.jsonl` (`G-17` and `G-18` have none). Open as before: the gun emplacements are outside the loop of `check_q08` (they are gated by Q14, X-12), the works that are not drawn, and the work meshes and crews, which nobody has looked at in a browser. OA11 (soldiers building positions and doing the work of a camp) is mapped to this gate by DEC-OA10-12 (3.1 OA-G11).

### Q09 Building states

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Building states (intact, damaged, burning, burned, loopholed, hospital use) change at documented times; the Bliss buildings burn July 3 morning; the McPherson barn does not burn July 1."
**Definition.** B.8.2 (pp. 94-97) the building table; B.8.7 (pp. 100-102); B.8.8 (p. 102) damage-state classes: barns and houses with documented fires (Bliss house and barn, Sherfy barn): intact, burning (progressive), burned shell or ruin; buildings with shell or bullet damage (Sherfy house, Trostle barn, Leister, Wade, Seminary, Rummel, gatehouse): intact, lightly damaged, damaged, decal sets at documented locations; hospital props. V.4 (p. 137): fire, shell damage, loopholing, hospital conversion and looting follow the buildings schedule. E.4 (p. 134): the Bliss barn and house were burned deliberately on the morning of July 3 by the 14th Connecticut; the McPherson barn did not burn on July 1 (the house burned in 1895).
- **Q09.1 Registry.** Every building part has a state schedule; the vocabulary is the six of the gate plus looted and ruin where the schedule says so.
- **Q09.2 Transition times.** Each transition happens inside its documented window; backward scrubbing reverses it. A date-only or "morning" window is the plain reading of the source, flagged (p. 100, B.8.7 header); its item is COVERED, not PASS (A-46, X-09).
- **Q09.3 Progressive.** Burning is progressive and ends as a burned shell or ruin; shelled and loopholed states are decal and geometry sets at documented locations; decal counts beyond a documented count (the Wade house 150 and more strikes) are plausible-procedural and marked (p. 102).
- **Q09.4 Named assertions** (global; any violation fails the gate): the Bliss house and barn burn on the morning of July 3 and not on July 2; the McPherson barn never burns in the window; the Sherfy barn fire lies inside its flagged window (July 2 evening or July 3, C-026); the Rose barn (1910), Codori barn (1882) and McPherson house (1895) are unchanged inside the window.
- **Q09.5 Hospital use.** A hospital site is in `hospital use` exactly in its documented interval (the activity is Q26). **Q09.6 Drawn.** Every building is drawn with geometry; the structures README counts 11 records without geometry and 5 with only a point: FAIL until modelled. **Q09.7 Cause.** Fires have their cause event (shelling, the 14th Connecticut detail) in the window.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (building part, transition, scenario) and the global assertions. PASS iff Q09.1 to Q09.7 hold with a documented clock window. COVERED (a flagged plain-reading window with an L3 entry) does not lift the verdict: the gate text has no flag branch, so a COVERED item keeps Q09 INCOMPLETE (K3, X-09). FAIL for an undrawn building.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q09` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q09` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q09.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `structures.json` (41 `bld-` records), `state_schedule.json`, `hospitals.json` (23 sites), `app/data/structures3d.json` (40 footprints, 229 borough masses, 117 point markers), `app/data/fx3d/fires.json`, conflict C-026. Python imports `check_structures.check_q09`; node evaluates `state_at` and the fire windows per minute (`fx3d` fires) per scenario; swgl draws before, during and after for a sample (burning shell, decals). Weight heavy.
**Runs.** python, node; pictures swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R14.S-001..S-088, R02.0000, R13.0000 (90 rows); judges the `bld-` and `H-` objects of an S row (3.2).
**State.** `check_structures.py check_q09` PASS (1,306 checks); `structures3d.test.mjs` and `fx3d.test.mjs` pin the Bliss, McPherson and Sherfy rules; 11 records have no geometry (SPEC-B). Pass 4 (00:12 UTC): `check_q09` run again, 1,306 checks, 0 failures; all 88 schedule rows have a row-level correction record (Q07 state); `state_schedule.json` and `hospitals.json` (23 sites) are unchanged. OA10 is mapped to this gate by DEC-OA10-12 and OA-G12 uses it for the hospital use of Q09.5 (3.1). The workflow scripts `tools/horde/workflows/bt-town-buildings.mjs` and `bt-medical.mjs` (written 23:59) are to produce the building and hospital content; at 01:05 UTC `docs/battle-theatre/town/` and `medical/` hold evidence folders and source lists in preparation and no building-state file exists.

### Q10 Night visibility check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Night visibility check. At every night time, render test frames and assert minimum luminance and contrast for every unit and activity in view; the sky is dimmed; moon and stars follow the ephemeris."
**Definition.** L.5 (p. 135): "Night must not be implemented as black"; exposure and tone-mapping with a guaranteed luminance floor "so that no unit, structure or activity falls below a minimum readable level (Gate Q10)"; light sources (campfires, lanterns, hospital lights, burning buildings, moon, muzzle flashes) are physically placed and flicker-modelled; L.6: every night has an activity schedule per unit. B.10.5 (p. 114): the clock drives one timeline value; at astronomical night the sky is clearly dark with stars (where cloud permits) and the moon where it is above the horizon; criterion 3 "Readability floor. At every instant, a viewer can identify units, activity and terrain features in the free camera at gameplay distance", an artistic exposure choice stated openly; cloud per night follows B.10.2 (overcast July 1 to 2, cirrus July 2 to 3, 7/10 cumulus with gaps July 3 to 4, wet July 4 to 5); unit markers and tier tags stay legible at night. D.2 V11 test (p. 124): the luminance histogram stays inside the readable band at all night hours; every night activity cites a source or a plausible-procedural tag.
- **Q10.1 Night set.** Every grid time with the sun's altitude below -0.833 degrees (twilight included; the timeline's NIGHT marker, civil dusk to civil dawn, is a subset, p. 9). Its size, counted for this file with the product's own low-precision `app/src/astro.mjs sunMoon` called at each integer-minute instant of the grid of 1.5 (26 June 00:00 to 14 July 23:59, 27,360 minutes, and 18 and 19 November, 2,880 minutes; a scratch count, not a Q11 result; the runner recomputes the set from the Q11-verified values): 12,074 minutes, 10,380 of them between 26 June and 14 July and 1,694 on 18 and 19 November. Called at the middle of each minute instead (`sunMoon(m + 0.5)`, the first count of this file) the split is 10,381 and 1,693 and the total is the same; the grid instant is the integer minute (1.5), and the runner records the instant it samples.
- **Q10.2 Readability floor on frames.** At every night time, that is every grid minute of the night set of Q10.1 and one second either side of every state change of a night activity or light source (T-Q10-6; the plan says "At every night time" and a sample never gives PASS, K10, U-26, X-41), and for every weather scenario, for the free-camera views at gameplay distance and the framing of each night activity (every unit and activity present at that time is in view, at least T-Q10-7, in at least one of that time's frames: the runner computes the cover and an unframed unit is an item NOT-RUN): the frame metrics of `app/tests/sky.spec.mjs` on the 8-bit sRGB frame (luminance Y = 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B): mean at least 24 (T-Q10-1), 5th percentile at least 6 (T-Q10-2), under 2% of pixels with Y under 8 (T-Q10-3), Michelson contrast (p95 minus p5) over (p95 plus p5) at least 0.2 (T-Q10-4); and for every unit, structure and activity in view (projected area at least T-Q10-7, UNSET): the region's mean Y at least T-Q10-1 (the guaranteed floor of L.5 is the minimum readable level for every region, not only for the frame) and the Michelson contrast of the region's mean against the mean of its surrounding ring at least T-Q10-9 (UNSET). The analytic floor (exposure, tone map, fill) is also checked at every grid minute.
- **Q10.3 Night reads as night.** Night luminance is under 0.75 of the same view by day (T-Q10-5), the sky dims monotonically with the sun's depression, stars are present where the cloud table permits. **Q10.4 Ephemeris.** The moon in the frame is where the Q11 table puts it, with the phase; absent when below the horizon or hidden by the night's cloud. **Q10.5 Light sources.** Fires, lanterns, burning buildings, muzzle and shell flashes that the timeline places exist at their place and time, are visible, flicker and are physically placed (L.5); an unsourced light is inferred or procedural and marked. **Q10.6 Tier display legible.** Unit markers and tier tags stay legible at night (p. 114: "unit markers and tier tags stay legible at night"; the plan gives no figure). On the night frame as rendered with the overlay drawn, each marker and tag meets T-Q10-8 against the frame pixels around it: its text (a unit's short label, the word of a tier) at least 4.5:1, or 3:1 where the text is large (at least 24 px, or at least 18.66 px at weight 700 or more), and its non-text parts (the outline, fill and glyph of a tier mark) at least 3:1. These are the WCAG AA figures (1.4.3 for text, 1.4.11 for non-text) as the project's own tests apply them (10.1); the earlier flat 3:1 for every part is withdrawn (X-43). **Q10.7 Night activities.** Each NIGHT row and each night's per-unit schedule (fires, cooking, sleeping, picket, march, digging, artillery moving, burying, carrying and treating the wounded, foraging, wagons) is realized and passes Q10.2 where in view; where sources are silent the unit's documented posture plus a labeled routine is shown and the gap registered (L.6). **Q10.8 Disclosure.** The product declares the readability boost in its settings (p. 114: "the boost factors are artistic, not physical"); the default is the readable setting.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (night time, view, scenario, entity or activity in view). PASS iff every metric of Q10.2 meets its recorded threshold for that frame region and Q10.3 to Q10.8 hold. A frame with any entity below the floor is FAIL. No COVERED. A pixel result from software rendering is `PASS@swgl`; the same frames on the real GPU are owed. The gate is INCOMPLETE while T-Q10-7 and T-Q10-9 are UNSET, while any night minute of Q10.1 lacks its frames (a run that has rendered a fraction records the fraction), or while no region method exists (region = the entity's pixels from an id pass; until the renderer offers one the region is the projected bounding box of its instances and the method is recorded).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q10` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q10` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q10.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `environment/ephemeris_1863.json`, `weather_timeline.json`, the layer's night activities, the exposure and tone-map code, `app/src/config.mjs DEFAULTS.nightFloor` (0.34), `check_environment.night_luminance_floor`. Node (`q10_floor.mjs`) evaluates the analytic floor at every minute; swgl (`q10_frames.mjs`, `BT_OUT=gates-q10`) renders the frames of every night minute on the real layer (not a sample, Q10.2), chunked, cached by (input hash, time, view, scenario) and resumable, because a frame costs 4 to 5 s: one view in one weather scenario is already about 12,000 frames, about 15 hours of locked time, and the whole set over the views and weather scenarios is days of it (a scratch estimate). The same grid on the real GPU is owed and is the quicker route; no GPU time is claimed here. Heavy. Evidence: per frame the metrics, per region the metrics, saved image paths with `looked_at: false`.
**Runs.** node (floor); swgl (frames); real-GPU confirmation of the frames owed.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R18.0000 (1 row); R11.0001..0691 rows with NIGHT through K6 (4.1).
**State.** `sky.spec.mjs` samples ten night times on the placeholder layer with one unit (SPEC-B): two logged values are mean 168.0 at 3 Jul 13:30 and mean 28.7 at 3 Jul 20:30 (software rendering, placeholder layer); DD-G03 records T-Q10-1 to T-Q10-5 and T-Q10-8 (its 10-minute picture grid is replaced by every night minute, DD-G10, X-41; its flat 3:1 for T-Q10-8 is tightened for text by DD-G13, X-43); T-Q10-7 and T-Q10-9 are UNSET, so the verdict is INCOMPLETE at best. Pass 4 of SPEC-B adds, read from that file and not re-run for this one: `sky.spec.mjs` passed 5 of 5 on SwiftShader in the specs' owner's log (started 19:19 UTC, 2,977 s; placeholder layer); the WebGPU path of the weather and effects layers has its own harness (`app/tests/weather3d-gpu*.mjs`, which `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md` lists as a source of numbers for Q10, Q22 and Q24) and `weather3d-gpu.test.mjs` (26 tests, all pass at 00:15 UTC); its stored run (`app/tests/results/weather3d-webgpu/`, 13 November, 640 x 360, 12,000 placeholder soldiers, software adapters on both APIs) has 16 cases, eight of them at night or dusk by name, with the luminance statistics of each read-back picture in 8-bit values: the means of those eight run from 34.0 (`stars-jun28-0200`) to 106.5 (`moon-zoom-jul3-2330`), and the black share is 0 in 15 of the 16 cases and 0.00001 in `flash-night-jul2-2330` (about two pixels of 230,400; an earlier line of SPEC-B said 0 in each). Those pictures show sky, fog, weather and effects in one stand-in view per case and contain no unit: they are evidence for the floor of the sky and effects passes, not Q10.2 over every unit and activity (SPEC-B 5.2 item 17).

### Q11 Ephemeris check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Ephemeris check: sun and moon values equal an independent ephemeris at the site and times."
**Definition.** L.1 (p. 135): compute sun and moon positions and phases from an ephemeris for the exact latitude, longitude and times for the whole window; "verify the moon phase independently. All sky, shadow and moonlight values derive from the ephemeris (Gate Q11)". B.10.2 (p. 111): time is local mean time, UTC minus 5 h 09 min; the plan's table was computed with the astropy builtin ephemeris at 39.83 N, 77.23 W, 170 m with sun thresholds -0.833, -6, -12 and -18 degrees; "The only independent check is the full moon time, which matches the published value to within one minute; rise and set times are not independently checked against USNO"; the computed times are astronomical-horizon times and the engine should apply the local horizon from the terrain model. Full moon on July 1 at 06:46 UT (p. 3).
- **Q11.1 Values.** For every grid minute of the whole window (June 26 to July 14, November 18 and 19) the product's runtime functions give sun altitude and azimuth, moon altitude and azimuth, illuminated fraction and phase, and per day sunrise, sunset, civil, nautical and astronomical twilight, moonrise and moonset; each equals the reference within T-Q11-1 to T-Q11-5.
- **Q11.2 Independence.** At least two reference methods that share neither code nor coefficient tables with the product and differ in theory, plus the anchor facts above; a shared quantity (the Delta T model for 1863) is named and its effect bounded against an independent Delta T source. Agreement between the product's table and its own generator is not independence. Today method A (astropy builtin ephemeris through ERFA) and method B (the repository's own Meeus implementation) share one Delta T polynomial and, for the moon, a theory of common ancestry (the checker says so); that pair is evidence and is not sufficient (X-18). A reference of different theory (for example a numerical ephemeris from a PyPI package, installed in the scratch environment; the runner records what could be installed and obtained from an allowed host) is required; if none can be had the gate is INCOMPLETE with that reason.
- **Q11.3 Site and clock.** One site (39.83 N, 77.23 W, 170 m per B.10.2) and LMT = UTC minus 5 h 09 min everywhere; the S-086 row prints 39.82 N, a defect of the plan (p. 118, A-17). **Q11.4 Local horizon.** Rise and set times in the product include the terrain horizon; the check compares the astronomical-horizon values to the reference and the product's local-horizon values to the terrain-derived horizon. **Q11.5 Used values.** The rendered sun and moon in frames sit at the table's altitudes and azimuths and cast shadows in the right direction (shadow azimuth equals sun azimuth plus 180 degrees within T-Q11-6, on swgl frames).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (date or minute, quantity). PASS iff the product value is within tolerance of every reference. A value outside tolerance is FAIL; nothing replaces a numeric disagreement. Named limits are registered with their bound (the shared Delta T, the lunar ancestry, the terrain horizon model, tier inferred).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q11` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q11` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q11.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the references (python in `tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy/bin/python`, created from PyPI, never the system python), `tools/bt/ephemeris.py` and the functions `gate_q11(recompute)` and `pdf_figure_checks()` of `check_environment.py` (imported, never `main()`, which rewrites `environment/check_environment_results.json`), the product's `app/src/astro.mjs`, `weather3d` sky geometry and the stored table (`environment/ephemeris_1863.json`). A node child exports the product's values at every grid minute; python compares. Recomputation takes about 30 s: heavy, through the lock. Evidence: per quantity the largest difference per reference, the grid size, the Delta T bound.
**Runs.** python (scratch environment), node; the frame check Q11.5 on swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R15.0000, R18.0000 (2 rows).
**State.** In the scratch environment (astropy 8.0.2, pyerfa 2.0.1.5) `gate_q11(True)` gave 13 of 13 checks ok, largest A minus B 0.0023 degrees (sun altitude), 0.0083 (sun azimuth), 0.00016 (moon altitude), 0.0006 (moon azimuth), rise and set at most 0.013 minutes; `pdf_figure_checks()` 134 printed figures, 6 FINDING rows about the plan (SPEC-B 7). The stored table covers 26 June to 6 July and 18 to 19 November; 7 to 14 July rests on the JS formula compared with the table where both exist (X-17). With the system python the checker exits 1 (`No module named 'astropy'`). Pass 4 of SPEC-B (00:2x UTC, by modification time and not by re-running everything): no input of this gate changed (`docs/battle-theatre/environment/`, `tools/bt/check_environment.py` and `tools/bt/ephemeris.py` have no file modified since 18:30 UTC on 13 November), so the figures above stand; `tmp/bt-gates/venv-astropy` survived the restarts.

### Q12 Scrub test

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Scrub test. Random-access seeks (forward and backward, including jumps across the whole window) produce state identical to sequential playback at the same time; no unit pops; the cache header seed and code version match."
**Definition.** T.2 and T.3 (p. 137): event-sourced cache with snapshots; the header holds "the deterministic seed, code version, scenario id and the input hash"; two-way navigation "with state identical to forward playback (Gate Q12)"; fence, earthwork, building, vegetation and weather states are functions of time, not accumulated side effects. D.3.2 item 1 (p. 125) counter-based generator keyed by (run seed, agent id, time bucket). D.2 V12 (p. 124): from any time t1 to t2 the world state equals the state reached by playing forward; "jump latency within budget". D.7 phase 12 (p. 130): "Jump tests at 100 random times". Coverage map 12 (p. 4): the scrubbing round trip is bit-identical.
- **Q12.1 Seek identity.** For every sampled pair (t1, t2), forward, backward and across the whole window, the digest after a random-access seek to t2 equals the digest of sequential playback at t2. **Q12.2 Round trip.** The seek A to B and back to A reproduces A's digest bit for bit.
- **Q12.3 Samples.** At least 100 random jump times (T-Q12-2, p. 130), every timeline row's seek targets (4.1 TL-5), both window ends, every snapshot boundary and one step either side, every event boundary; over the default, the two extremal and every other scenario the property factors over (1.5). The runner records the seed.
- **Q12.4 The digest.** `StateDigest(t, scenario)` is a hash over the canonical serialization of every time-dependent layer: unit presence, positions, formations and activities; every entity table (soldiers, horses, guns, vehicles, flags); casualties and hospital contents; structure state buffers (fences, buildings, works, hospitals); vegetation states; weather and environment channels; sun, moon and light sources; effects by cause; scheduled sounds; figure animation phases. The layer list is a registry (`tools/bt/gates/time_layers.json`); a time-dependent layer outside the digest is a FAIL of the census (as Q02.1). Today identity is proven layer by layer with no common hash (X-13).
- **Q12.5 No pops.** Between consecutive frames no entity appears, vanishes or jumps except at documented entries and exits; checked also immediately after a seek. **Q12.6 Header.** The cache header's seed equals the project seed, its code version equals the running code's, its scenario id equals the requested one, its input hash equals the recomputed one; a mismatching cache must be refused and using one is FAIL. Both code strings in the tree (`sim/cache.mjs CODE_VERSION` `bt-sim-0.1` and `app/src/config.mjs SIM_CODE` `bt-app-0.1`) are read from the running modules and compared; comparison with literals typed in a spec is not enough (X-14). **Q12.7 Determinism.** The same seed and inputs give byte-identical caches and digests on two runs; the digests are also recorded for the owner's machine (floating-point differences across machines: the cross-machine comparison is owed). **Q12.8 Latency.** A jump completes within T-Q12-1 ("within budget", p. 124; no figure: INCOMPLETE until recorded; measured on the real GPU). **Q12.9 UI.** The scrubber, jump-to-event, speed change and bookmarks end in the same digest (swgl, `scrub.spec.mjs`).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (seek pair, scenario); per-row items for the rows' seek targets. PASS iff the digests are equal and no pop is seen. No COVERED: a failing seek is a defect and a layer outside the digest is a FAIL, not a register item.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q12` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q12` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q12.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `sim/cache.mjs` (`EventCache`, `header()`, `scrubTest`), the stage and every layer function, `app/tests/node-world.mjs nodeWorld()`, `check_structures.check_scrub` and its `Gate` class, the scenario set, the row windows (`timeline_windows.jsonl`, the realization workstream's `timeline.mjs` for the whole printed cell). Node (`q12_scrub.mjs`), heavy; a swgl child (`q12_ui.mjs`, `BT_OUT=gates-q12`) for Q12.9. Evidence: seed, pair, both digests, the mismatching layers by name.
**Runs.** node; the scrubber swgl; latency and cross-machine digests need the real GPU or the owner's machine (owed).
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R11.0001..0691, R11.0000, R01.0000, R07.0000, R19.0000 (695 rows). A timeline row passes Q12 when its seek targets pass in every scenario of its group.
**State.** Purity tests exist per layer (SPEC-B Q12); the cache header is compared with literals in `scrub.spec.mjs`; 295 phrase-only rows and 1 no-date row have no clock minute (4.1 TL-5 gives their targets). Pass 4 of SPEC-B: `scrub.spec.mjs` (started 23:37 UTC on a frozen copy) was killed by the container restart and has no end line, and the owner's queue file (`tmp/bt-spec/queue2.txt`) does not list it; the casualties picture test failed once on SwiftShader (`the men stand up again`: the picture at 14:59 differed by 48.951 % from the first 14:59 picture; its owner had not classified the cause). New pure-function evidence: `checkVegetation` asks every unit's state twice in different orders and reports `pure: true` over 555,000 states, and the stored WebGPU weather run has a `pure` field whose first entry is `identical: true, bytesDiffering: 0, maxDiff: 0`; neither is a seek test of the real layer.

### Q13 Audio

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Audio delay equals distance divided by the speed of sound at the modeled temperature; attenuation and occlusion tests; silence in empty woods except documented ambient."
**Definition.** S (p. 137): S.2 "Delay = distance divided by the speed of sound at the modeled temperature. Apply distance attenuation, frequency-dependent atmospheric absorption, ground effect and terrain occlusion." S.3 acoustic shadows and refraction are scenarios, tagged plausible-procedural, the gap registered. S.4 (R06) sources outside the viewer's range are rendered with correct delay and attenuation; woods without events are silent except documented ambient sound. S.5 voice culling and aggregate beds only as optimizations that preserve per-source timing and distance relationships. S.6 music only as the period sources document. D.2 O6 test (p. 124); D.7 phase 10 (p. 130). C.6 (p. 119): about 343 m/s at 20 C is a hypothesis flagged "unverified background"; the runner computes c from the temperature the weather state holds.
- **Q13.1 Delay.** For every sound event of the real layer (each shot, burst, explosion, hoofbeat, voice), listener position and grid time: scheduled arrival minus emission time equals `d / c(T(t))` within 1 ms (T-Q13-1), `d` the straight-line distance from the source at emission to the listener at arrival, `c(T)` the speed of sound at the temperature the weather state holds at that time (the value the scene uses, Q22). The baseline carries no wind or refraction term (A-20, X-15). Tested on fire events from the real layer and on an analytic grid.
- **Q13.2 Attenuation, absorption, ground, occlusion.** Level falls monotonically with distance in every band; absorption is frequency-dependent; a listener behind a ridge or building receives additional attenuation against the same listener in the open at equal distance; ground effect is present for grazing paths. **Q13.3 Silent woods.** A listener in woods with no active source in range hears only documented ambient (weather, insects, birds, horses where a source exists, otherwise procedural and tagged); no gunfire bed plays where the cache holds no source; checked over the real woods cells across the whole timeline. **Q13.4 Per source.** Each source has its own position and time; culling and aggregate beds keep per-source arrival times and distance relationships, and the optimization is documented (p. 137, S.5). **Q13.5 Shadow scenarios.** Acoustic-shadow and refraction models are selectable scenarios, tagged plausible-procedural, the gap registered; the baseline excludes them; the documented anchors (Ewell's party not hearing Longstreet's signal on July 2; the Pittsburgh claim; the Ellis attribution unverified, p. 119) are comparison cases shown as such. **Q13.6 Music** only where a period source documents it (regimental bands, drums, bugle calls); sourced bugle calls and guidon patterns were not found in the notes and are a register item (p. 137, S.6). **Q13.7 Provenance.** Every sample or synthesis recipe is in the audio ledger with licence, source and method; none is CC BY-SA, NC or ND where the release forbids it; synthesized sound is flagged as modern reconstruction per DD-14 (panel and overlay; never called period sound). **Q13.8 Audible from anywhere** (R06): no culling radius changes a source's arrival time or order, and a far source is heard at its delayed time.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (source event, listener, time) and (woods cell, time), plus the provenance rows. PASS iff the property holds within the recorded tolerance. No COVERED. Black-powder acoustics have no sourced study (p. 137, S.1; p. 119): the synthesis stays plausible-procedural with a register entry, and the S-R OA05 stays LINKED.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q13` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q13` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q13.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `app/src/audio3d` (`physics.mjs speedOfSound(tC) = 331.3 * sqrt(1 + tC / 273.15)`, `planner.mjs`, `atmosphere.mjs atmosphereFromWeather`, `terrain.mjs`, `woods.mjs`, `offline.mjs`), `app/src/audio-host.mjs`, the weather state (`Environment`, `weather3d/timeline.mjs`), the layer's fire events, `app/data/audio3d/*.json` (ledger, woods mask of 276 polygons), the DEM. Node (`q13_audio.mjs`) for every numeric check; a swgl child (`q13_offline.mjs`) renders with an `OfflineAudioContext` (no GPU involved) and reads onsets. The two temperature paths (`weather3d/timeline.mjs out.temp` and `audio3d/atmosphere.mjs`, which falls back to `DEFAULT_TEMP_C = 20`) must be proven equal end to end (X-16). Heavy.
**Runs.** node; offline render on swgl; listening to the mix is owed to a person.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R03.0000, R06.0000 (2 rows); OA05 (3.1).
**State.** `audio3d.test.mjs` (38 tests), `audio-host.test.mjs` (18) and `audio3d-round2.test.mjs` (8) check the physics and a mock Web Audio graph; the planner's delay is the moving-air time `d / (c + w.u)`, not distance over c (SPEC-B Q13; X-15). Pass 4 (00:2x UTC): no code or test of this gate changed by modification time (`app/src/audio-host.mjs`, `app/tests/audio3d.test.mjs` and `audio-host.test.mjs` are unmodified since 18:30 UTC on 13 November; `app/src/audio3d/woods.mjs` was edited at 00:10 for a comment on its line 3); `audio3d-round2` (8 tests) and `audio-host` (18) were run alone at 00:42 and `audio3d` (38) in the node suite of the locked job (SPEC-B 7.6), all passing.

### Q14 Ordnance

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Ordnance ranges, elevations and rates of fire within documented figures; per-battery gun counts match the verified battery table."
**Definition.** W.1 (p. 137): a verified weapons table with sources (infantry arms, cavalry arms, bayonets, artillery with ammunition, ranges, rates, crew roles, limber and caisson loads); figures to confirm from primary text; gaps (Blakely specifications, per-elevation range tables, chest rounds, recoil distance, fuse models, Confederate crew size) are register entries until a primary manual or ordnance report is read. W.2: every battery has a record ingested from the Official Records. B.6.14.1 (pp. 78-79) piece table with conflicting values shown as ranges (Napoleon shot 1,619 to 1,680 yd, canister 350 to 400 yd, crew 8 or 9, guns on the field 366 to 372 Union and 281 to 282 Confederate); B.6.14.2 (p. 79) ammunition and fuzes; B.6.14.3 (pp. 79-80) cycle time about 20 to 30 seconds per round (inferred); B.6.11 (p. 75) muskets two to three rounds a minute.
- **Q14.1 Weapons table** with sources and tiers for every weapon the product fires, conflicts as ranges. **Q14.2 Ranges and elevations.** For every projectile event the range does not exceed the documented figure for its type at the nearest documented elevation not below the shot's; elevations lie inside the documented points or a calibrated model labeled inferred whose output at each documented point equals the documented figure within its spread. Where no per-elevation table is documented (the W.1 gap) the item is COVERED by its register entry and keeps the verdict INCOMPLETE (X-09). **Q14.3 Ammunition by range band.** Canister inside 350 to 400 yd (double canister inside about 100), solid shot, shell and case beyond (p. 79, B.6.14.2, an inference for the engine); defective Confederate fuzes and overshooting on July 3 are depicted where documented (p. 137, V.1), marked. **Q14.4 Rates of fire.** Over every sliding 60-second window no piece or soldier fires faster than the documented maximum of its weapon (the plan prints the artillery cycle as "about 20-30 seconds per round for a trained detachment" and calls it inferred, p. 80, and the musket rate as "2-3 rounds a minute typical (M1861)" and the Sharps as "8-10 a minute claimed" with two Wikipedia keys, p. 75: a typical, inferred or lead-sourced figure is no documented maximum, so an item that rests on one is COVERED at best, which keeps Q14 INCOMPLETE, until a manual or report gives the maximum; X-46); a slower rate is allowed. **Q14.5 Gun counts.** For every battery and grid time, guns present by type and number equal the verified battery table (ranges stay ranges: G-11, G-13, G-14); army totals lie inside 366 to 372 and 281 to 282 as ranges. **Q14.6 Ammunition.** Expenditure does not exceed the documented per-battery stock or documented expenditure. The documented figures the plan prints are Smith's 4th NY 240 rounds (marker), Wheeler's 13th NY 850 rounds with 200 lost with a broken caisson, Bigelow about three tons including 92 canister and McCarthy (Cabell) 20 rounds at an advancing line early July 3 (p. 79). The typical stock of a six-Napoleon battery (288 solid shot, 288 spherical case, 96 shell and 96 canister, p. 79) is printed as documented (secondary) on the strength of a Wikipedia page and a report that it matches the 5th Maine battery figure: Wikipedia is a lead (p. 134, E.1, E.7), so under this file's rule that figure is a lead and not a bound until a primary ordnance document is read, and the totals of about 32,000 Union and 22,000 Confederate rounds are an open gap that the plan itself calls not verified (p. 79); an item that rests on either is COVERED at best (X-46). **Q14.7 Crew.** Eight or nine per piece is a selectable scenario; crew roles and the drill state machine follow the documented sequence of commands and the ten states of one gun detachment, whose timing is inferred (B.6.14.3, p. 79).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (weapon class, event) and (battery, time). PASS iff the property holds against a verified figure. A missing documented figure is COVERED (non-lifting) with its L3 entry; a count compared only with the plan's own text is not a verified table (X-29) and is BLOCKED-INPUT.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q14` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q14.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `kit/weapons.json`, `kit/artillery_batteries.json` (125 batteries), `kit/verified_batteries.json` (absent), `drill/*.json`, `app/data/battery-guns.json`, `artillery-train.json`, G-01..G-18 of `structures.json`, the fire events of the layer, the OR battery reports. `tools/bt/drill_check.py` re-verifies the drill items against the manuals' OCR; in this container 9 of the 16 pinned texts are absent, so it runs against a scratch root of symlinks (`tmp/bt-gates/root`, texts re-fetched from archive.org by the hashes in `library/acquired.json`), never by placing files in `tmp/library/misc`. The battery table is built by `python3 tools/bt/kit_verified.py build --out-dir <scratch>` (never without `--out-dir`, 1.6) or read from `kit/verified_batteries.json` once its owner writes it. Python and node, light to medium.
**Runs.** python, node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R13.G-01..G-18, R03.0000, R04.0000 (20 rows); the prepared G rows also pass Q08 (4.5).
**State.** The existing Q14 test matches the kit table, which is built from the plan's text; `drill_check.py` exits 0 against the scratch texts (287 items and 64 comparison rows); 29 batteries have gun types stated and 96 `gun types not given` (`kit/KIT-NOTES.md`). Pass 4 (SPEC-B 00:12 UTC, and the scratch build repeated for this file at 01:04 UTC): `kit/verified_batteries.json` is absent; the scratch build of `kit_verified.py` gives `verified_batteries` 131 batteries in 33 formations, 0 verified, 0 inferred, 0 conflict, 0 gap, 131 unverified; `check_kit.py` still validates 125 artillery units (the four `kit/*.json` tables were rewritten at 23:56 and 00:21 by another workstream and the checker's figures did not move); 9 of the 16 pinned OCR texts of `drill_check.py` are still absent from `tmp/library/misc`. The correction rows for the `R13.G` items are in `evidence_corrections/terrain-1.jsonl`: row-level records for `R13.G-01` to `R13.G-16`; `G-17` and `G-18` have none.

### Q15 Frame-time budget

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Frame-time budget at target agent counts in the headless benchmark."
**Definition.** D.2 O1 (p. 123): "Headless benchmark scenes at rising agent counts with measured frame-time"; D.6.1 (p. 128): an empty scene, then a crowd scene at rising agent counts, then a single film-grade frame, recorded in the hardware profile; D.7 phase 12 (p. 130): frame-time at target agent counts. N (p. 135): soldiers one-to-one up to the strength figure, no capping without an owner decision. DD-15 and DD-27: the product is the browser viewer, WebGL2 by default, WebGPU only on request.
- **Q15.1 Scenes:** empty, rising steps, the default scenario's full one-to-one count and the upper end of the strength ranges of both armies, with camera paths (overview, descent into the densest moments, a night scene with light sources, a storm scene, a time-lapse). **Q15.2 Measurement:** per-frame CPU and GPU time on the owner's GPU (the one in `GOVERNANCE/hardware_profile.json`) in a headless run, after a fixed warm-up and over a fixed duration; p50, p95, p99; renderer API, resolution, power and thermal state recorded; WebGL2 and WebGPU separately. **Q15.3 Budget:** p95 within T-Q15-1 to T-Q15-4 (20 ms paced, 10 ms CPU plus GPU uncapped, no long task over 50 ms, input answered within 100 ms; DD-G05). **Q15.4 No capping:** the counts are one-to-one; level of detail changes how a soldier is drawn, never whether; a pass reached by dropping soldiers is FAIL. **Q15.5 Jump latency** (shared with Q12.8).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (scene, agent count, camera path). PASS only from a run on the real GPU with the renderer string of that GPU and every scene inside the budget.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q15`; output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q15.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). It probes the renderer; on software rendering it writes every item NOT-RUN with the reason `no GPU in this container` and the exact owner step in `owed`: run the performance spec of `app/tests` on the machine of `GOVERNANCE/hardware_profile.json` with `BT_GL=gpu` and give back the result folder. A result produced there is ingested only through `tools/bt/gates/ingest_gpu_result.py`, which accepts a file only if its renderer string names no software renderer, its input hashes equal the current inputs and its command is inside it; the ingested record carries `renderer_class: gpu`. No frame time is claimed here; the stored `app/tests/results-perf.json` is not a fresh run (U-21).
**Runs.** **NOT RUNNABLE HERE: real GPU.**
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R01.0000 (1 row).
**State.** `perf.spec.mjs` wraps every threshold in `t.gpuOnly`; on software rendering it prints `# NOT RUN (NEEDS_GPU)`. No GPU time of the real crowd renderer has been measured (the progress note says `Real-GPU timing not measured`). Pass 4 of SPEC-B: no input changed (`app/tests/perf.spec.mjs` was last modified at 19:04 UTC on 13 November, `app/tests/lib.mjs` at 20:13 and `GOVERNANCE/hardware_profile.json` on 4 November); the specs' owner's one run of the paced WebGL2 frame-time test ended SKIPPED with five NEEDS_GPU assertions not run (22:23 UTC, 10 minutes), and the frame times it printed, 8.0 to 10.0 s at 1920 x 1080 with 165,000 placeholder soldiers and 360 guns, are a software figure and not a timing of the product.

### Q16 Photo-match

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Photo-match tests at known camera positions of period photographs (staged-photograph flags respected)."
**Definition.** G.5 (p. 134): procedural rock "validated by photo-match at documented camera positions". K.4 (p. 135): the Devil's Den photograph is staged and cannot be used as a candid reference. X.5 (e) (p. 137): reviewers check the render against photographs (Gate Q16). D.7 phases 4, 5, 13, 15 (pp. 129-130). B.9 Table 4 (p. 107): photographs with camera positions and rephotography.
- **Q16.1 The set P.** Photographs made in 1863 with a documented camera position (a non-lead source states it; a position derived from the match itself does not count) and its uncertainty. A documented position is the coordinate of the camera station with the lens and plate (or a calibration from mapped landmarks), stated by a non-lead source (DD-PM-01, the stricter reading of A-21); a statement in words (a place, a direction, a reference object) is a documented statement, and the pose built from it is tier inferred or plausible-procedural, never a documented position. The plan asks for photographs "with a known or recoverable camera position" and says "Camera positions beyond the NPS Rose Woods example were not found in the notes; recovering them is a task for the pipeline" (p. 109, B.9.2 step 7); the gate text says known (p. 136), and a position that the pipeline recovers from the match itself is a pose of the model and not a known position (X-44, DD-G16). A photograph without a documented position is BLOCKED-INPUT and registered. **Q16.2 Render** from the documented position and orientation at the photograph's date and time of day (structure states as photographed, such as the Leister wall photographed on July 6 and the gatehouse area photographed on July 7, p. 94 and S-075 on p. 102; weather, vegetation, shadows from the ephemeris). **Q16.3 Registration:** at least the recorded minimum number of stable landmarks matched, per-landmark reprojection error and RMS recorded against T-Q16-1 (UNSET until the first documented position exists). **Q16.4 Verdict:** match, partial or mismatch decided by the thresholds with the residual table; a verdict written only by eye is not a pass. The words of `tools/bt/photomatch/verdicts.json` map as `matches` to match, `roughly` to partial, `does not` to mismatch, and `cannot judge` is no verdict (the item stays BLOCKED-INPUT); the photomatch task's verdicts are conditional on its registered pose and are evidence, not the gate's verdict. **Q16.5 Staged:** a staged photograph (the Devil's Den sharpshooter pair, about July 6: p. 3, p. 71) is used for terrain and rocks only, never for the position of bodies or props ("Staged photographs (the Devil's Den sharpshooter) are used for terrain and rocks only, never for the position of bodies or props", p. 109; K.4, p. 135; DD-PM-05); its posture, arms and bodies are not used as a candid reference, and the match report says so. **Q16.6 Corrections:** a change made to improve a match is a recorded correction with its source (G.3); nothing is tuned silently.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a photograph of P. PASS iff the verdict is `match` by the metric. With P empty the gate is INCOMPLETE. For the R12 catalogue rows Q16 applies only to items whose type is photograph (4.7 CT-3); the other items are N/A for Q16 and judged by Q25.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q16` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q16` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q16.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs, all read from the files at run time (the figures here are those of 01:05 UTC on 14 November and are never constants of a runner): `tools/bt/photomatch/` with `catalogue.json` (109 Library of Congress records), `catalogue_additions.json` (20 further records that the first date filter dropped, DD-PM-03 and DD-PM-04, and its `skipped_search_results`; its own `_doc` still says 18), `photograph_registry.json` (one entry per record, 129, with `staged_flag` true for 15), `poses.json` (schema 2: 20 registered poses, 5 of position tier inferred and 15 plausible-procedural) with `resolved_poses.json`, `metrics.json` and `reports/`, `criteria.json` (pass criteria written before the renders they judge), `verdicts.json` (vocabulary matches, roughly, does not, cannot judge, its 16 pose verdicts and its `gate_q16` block), `position_statements.json` (145 statements from 68 sources: 135 of tier documented, 7 lead, 3 inferred; each is a statement and not a coordinate), `images_ledger.json`, `nps_then_to_loc.json`, `nps_then_now.json` (23 comparisons, no coordinates) and the deterministic renderer `run.mjs` with `compose.py`; the third pass of the photo-match owner adds a wide search of the whole Civil War group (`catalogue_wide_items.json`, 62 records by its owner's note, and `wide_images_ledger.json`) that the registry file did not yet hold when read; `app/docs/PHOTOMATCH.md`, which `make_doc.py` regenerates from those files and which is never read as an input (U-21); the evidence corrections for R12.032 to R12.035. The scripts `run.mjs`, `build_registry.py`, `build_statements.py`, `catalogue_additions.py`, `make_doc.py`, `wide_search.py` and `fetch_loc.py` write their own files or read the network and are not run by a gate runner (1.6). Python composition and metric; swgl renders. Heavy.
**Runs.** swgl (the geometry match does not depend on the GPU), python.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R12.001..R12.045, R12.0000, R02.0000 (47 rows); R22.0000 for the chapter (Q28.10).
**State** (01:05 UTC on 14 November, read from the data files and the test run for this file; the photo-match owner was editing them: `position_statements.json` was written at 00:59 UTC and `poses.json` at 00:54, and its note `horde/requests/bt-gates-photomatch-third-pass.md` of 01:02 UTC reports 189 registry records, 20 poses and 145 statements, where the registry file still held 129 and `verdicts.json` was unchanged since 21:05 UTC on 13 November). `PHOTOMATCH.md` says `Status: not passing`. 129 registry records; 0 documented camera positions (`verdicts.json` `gate_q16`: `passes` false and `documented_position_count` 0 by DD-PM-01: none of the 145 statements gives a coordinate, a lens or a plate); 20 poses with inferred or assumed positions; 16 pose verdicts: 0 matches, 5 roughly, 4 does not, 7 cannot judge; by record the registry holds 121 cannot judge, 5 roughly, 2 does not and 1 blank. By this file's rules every item of the registry is BLOCKED-INPUT (Q16.1: no documented position) and the verdict is INCOMPLETE whatever the counts become. The photo-match owner put one question to this workstream: whether a building roof that the National Park Service names as the camera place, with a cardinal direction word and the lens unknown (poses P27, P28 and P29, the Evergreen Cemetery gatehouse: horizontal uncertainty about 20 m, camera height an assumption of 8 m, heading a word with a sector of 45 degrees), is a documented position. This file answers no (Q16.1, X-44, DD-G16): a place and a direction in words are a statement, the gate asks for a position, and the set P stays empty until a non-lead source gives a coordinate with lens and plate or a calibration from mapped landmarks. A pose verdict is evidence about the model and never a Q16 result: `roughly` is a partial (not PASS), `does not` a mismatch, `cannot judge` stays BLOCKED-INPUT, and only `matches` at a documented position can give PASS. T-Q16-1 stays UNSET: the pass criteria that the photo-match task wrote before its first renders (`criteria.json`: C-P27-P28-1, and the skyline test with 0.5 degree for a match level and 1.0 for a partial against 24 control stations) are that task's own and are not offered as T-Q16-1; the three binary tests of C-P27-P28-1 are necessary conditions only (poses P06 and P07 pass all three though their verdict is does not) and a runner does not use them as the metric. Tests: `docs/battle-theatre/app/tests/photomatch-q16.test.mjs` 15 tests, 8 pass, 6 fail, 1 skipped at 01:07 UTC (11 tests with 6 pass, 4 fail, 1 skipped at 00:13 UTC in SPEC-B), red because the data files moved ahead of the counts it asserts (SPEC-B 5.4), which says that test and the data disagreed at that time and is no verdict on any photograph; `tests/photomatch.test.mjs` still asserts the first-run ledger hosts (request notes `horde/requests/bt-gates-photomatch-test-host-rule.md` and `bt-web-photomatch-old-test.md`).

### Q17 Coverage of the timeline

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Coverage: every timeline row has a visual realization or an open register entry."
**Definition.** D.2 O7 (p. 124): "Coverage matrix: every timeline row either has a visual realization or a register entry." Coverage map O7 (p. 5): "No gap larger than a stated interval without a labeled procedural fill". O.4 (p. 136): "Every unit-time slot has an activity (Section M.6) and a position region with uncertainty. A slot with no sourced activity is filled with the unit's last documented state plus a labeled routine, never left empty and never silently invented." B.2 (p. 9): open-gap nodes generate no behaviour and surface as map markers; plausible-procedural nodes generate seeded behaviour that is always visibly flagged. B.12.2 (p. 117): the epilogue rows are timeline rows. Section 6 defines the realization record.
- **Q17.1 Row set.** The 691 rows of T1, T2, T3 and the 14 epilogue rows E-001..E-014: 705 rows (X-04). **Q17.2 Realization.** The row's realization record is valid (section 6): executed in every scenario of the row's group, expected equals observed, inside the exact allowed window of the row (6.4 V4: no clock margin). **Q17.3 Register alternative.** An open L3 entry (1.4) for the row and, for an open-gap row, the visible marker at the right time and place; for a plausible-procedural fallback the marked seeded behaviour. **Q17.4 Slot coverage.** For every unit and every grid time between its arrival and departure there is an activity and a position region with uncertainty, sourced or a labeled routine; no slot is empty and none unlabeled (for R07.0000 the stated interval is zero). **Q17.5 Gap markers** exist in the evidence layer for every open-gap row.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a row. PASS iff a valid scene realization exists (section 6). COVERED iff there is no valid scene realization and an L3 entry exists (a card alone, a partial realization, a row with no scene consequence and an open-gap row with its marker are COVERED at best); the S-R is then LINKED-ACTIVE. FAIL iff neither. Q17.4 and Q17.5 are whole-product items. The verdict PASS counts COVERED (the gate text has the branch).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q17` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q17.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `GOVERNANCE/realizations/` (records `<shard>.jsonl`, `EXAMPLES.jsonl`), the realization harness `node tools/bt/realize/check.mjs --out GOVERNANCE/gate_results/evidence/Q17/RESULTS.json` (about 4 s of CPU and 9 s of wall time at 01:0x UTC, node, writes nowhere else), the timeline and epilogue seed files, `timeline_verified.jsonl`, `timeline_windows.jsonl`, the correction files, the register and `register/search_logs/`. Python (`q17_coverage.py`) reads the harness report and recomputes for every row the class (`scene` or `card`), partial status and the L3 test itself; it never uses the harness's `register_open` or `q17.ok`. The harness's own `q17.ok_strict` now applies V4, V6, V7 and V9 of 6.4 itself (DD-RZ-10: a realized row counts strictly only if it is a scene, shows the whole row, runs or excludes with a reason every scenario of its group and lies inside the exact time; `not_strict_because` says `card`, `partial`, `scenario_group_incomplete` or `clock_margin_used`), and its rows carry `allowed_exact`, `window_flags`, `record_reading`, `clock_tolerance_used` and `scenario_group`; the runner reads them as a cross-check and recomputes the windows and the group itself (6.4), so that a stale or edited `RESULTS.json` cannot pass a row (a stored file is valid while its `harness_digest` and `inputs_digest`, in four parts, equal the current ones: DD-RZ-09). **The runner does not extend `tools/bt/check_coverage.py`.** `tests/governance/realize-inputs.test.mjs` (the test named `the register half of the gate agrees with tools/bt/check_coverage.py`) runs that script, requires an empty stderr and asserts that its Q17 block (`rows` 691, `covered_by_open_register_entry`, `uncovered`, the first uncovered ids) equals the harness's register-only counts and that `ok` is false; a script that subtracted realizations from `uncovered` would break that test, which belongs to the realization workstream (X-52). `check_coverage.py` stays the weaker register-only count (`visual = set()`, the timeline seed only, the 14 epilogue rows not scanned) and its output is never a Q17 result. A node child (`q17_windows.mjs`) recomputes the exact allowed window of every row, with the harness's own `loadTimeline` and `epilogueRows` on an in-memory copy of `tolerances.json` whose clock tolerance is 0 (6.4 V4), and tests every record against it. Q17.4 and Q17.5 scan the layer (node child). Light.
**Runs.** python, node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R11.0001..0691, R11.0000, R22.0001..0014, R05.0000, R07.0000 (708 rows).
**State** (the harness was run for this file at about 01:05 UTC on 14 November to a scratch path, `node tools/bt/realize/check.mjs --out <scratch>`, 9.3 s of wall time and 3.8 s of CPU, harness digest fc85b8db0fca8cae, inputs digest eb6b98647d961029, `complete` true; the stored `RESULTS.json` of 00:42 UTC in `GOVERNANCE/realizations/` is not used): of the 691 timeline rows 4 are realized by scene (T1-003 and T1-019, weather and sky; T1-015 and T1-016, structure state, both partial because their records list parts of the row in `not_shown`, so they count strictly only with an open register entry; the first writing of this file counted 2 realized at 21:14 UTC because these two records no longer validated then, and their owner rewrote them at 00:14 UTC), 315 are covered by an open register entry only (any non-closed entry: the weaker reading) and 372 are uncovered (374 strict); of the 14 epilogue rows 12 are covered by an open entry only and 2 are uncovered; 20 rows have a record (the worked examples of `EXAMPLES.jsonl`, written 00:14 UTC) and 671 none; 10 rows lie outside the theatre clock (T1-001, T1-002 and eight rows between T3-209 and T3-217). The rows not realized fail with the codes `no_record` 671, `unit_not_on_field` 9, `unit_not_drawn` 8, `unit_not_in_layer` 6, `place_off_ground` 4, `time_outside_clock` 2, `place_unknown` 2, `adapter_missing` 1 and `structure_unknown` 1. The harness is tested by three files (`realize.test.mjs` 24 tests, `realize-rules.test.mjs` 29 and `realize-inputs.test.mjs` 15, all passing in the runs of SPEC-B pass 4, read from that file and not re-run for this one) and by its mutation check (`MUTATION-CHECK.json` of 22:04 UTC: 144 mutations listed, 13 run, 13 killed, 0 survived). By this file's rules at most 2 rows are PASS (T1-003 and T1-019, before the runner applies V1 to V11 and TL-3 to TL-8), none of the 315 is COVERED yet (no L3 entry exists), so the verdict is FAIL.

### Q18 Requirement coverage

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Requirement coverage: all 21 requirement IDs have sub-requirements, each passing or linked to an open register entry with an active reconstruction."
**Definition.** F (p. 134): "100% of sub-requirements are either passing or linked to an open register entry awaiting an owner decision with work continuing on the best reconstruction; anything else is unfinished work, not an exception." Z.1 to Z.3 (p. 138). The requirement set is R01 to R22 (A-01, X-07) and the twelve owner-chat rows.
- **Q18.1 Requirements.** R01 to R22 have wording equal to `_req_src.json` (never edited) and at least one sub-requirement each; OA01 to OA12 are present. **Q18.2 Coverage.** Every leaf sub-requirement is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE (L3, 1.4); each of the 22 top rows is COVERED-BY-CHILDREN (4.9 TOP-1), judged from the statuses of its children and never from its own `passes` or its own register link (K13, X-42); every requirement-level check item RC-Rnn.k of 7.1 is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE; the report gives counts per status and per class and lists every UNLINKED and LINKED (L1, L2) row and every uncovered RC item with its reason. **Q18.3 Populations.** Each expected population of 4.11 exists; a missing one is FAIL. The RC items exist by construction of 7.1; one whose check function does not exist yet is BLOCKED-INPUT and is covered only by an L3 entry that names it. **Q18.4 Register integrity.** Every linked entry resolves, is `open` or `awaiting owner`, has all the fields of Section R and App. A (`id`, requirement ids, description, why literal 100% is impossible or blocked, evidence searched with negative results, reconstruction, tier, marking, impact, owner decision requested, status, decision and date; p. 136-137, R) non-placeholder; entries are never deleted. The runner reads the folder `register/` as well as `register_index.json`: an entry file that is not in the index is reported (nine on 2026-11-14), and a link to it does not resolve until it is indexed and complete (1.4, A-57, X-49). **Q18.5 Flags honest.** No `passes=true` without a valid run record, none with `seeded_unverified` on a PDF row, none with NOT-RUN or BLOCKED-INPUT in an applicable gate. The stored flags are read once, after every status is computed, and compared with what the records support; they are never an input to any item verdict (K13). **Q18.6 Wording.** No sub-requirement text has changed since seeding (the seed generators run in memory, plus `gate_results/subreq_added.json` for rows added later); additions only.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a sub-requirement (a row of `requirements.json`, the OA rows included) or a requirement-level check item RC-Rnn.k; plus the structural checks. For a leaf row PASS iff it is PASSING or LINKED-ACTIVE; for one of the 22 rows `R01.0000` to `R22.0000` PASS iff it is COVERED-BY-CHILDREN (4.9 TOP-1); for an RC item PASS iff its item in `RC-Rnn.json` is PASS (PASSING) or an open L3 entry of the requirement names its id in the title or description (LINKED-ACTIVE); and every structural check holds. **No item reads a `passes` flag or a Q18 verdict**: Q18 runs after every other gate and the RC checks and judges statuses only (K13, 4.9 TOP-5), so R01.0000, which lists Q18 among its gates, is not its own support: its Q18 item is its derived coverage, and its `passes` is computed afterwards from the Q18 verdict (X-42). It is a structure-and-honesty gate and can pass while most S-Rs are LINKED-ACTIVE.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q18` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q18.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`; item ids `Q18/<S-R id>` and `Q18/RC-Rnn.k`). Inputs: `requirements.json`, `_req_src.json`, `register_index.json`, `register/*.md`, `register/search_logs/`, the populations' source data (4.11), `subreq_links.json`, all latest `Qnn.json` and `RC-Rnn.json` except its own. Python (`q18_requirements.py`), light, runs last; it recomputes link levels itself and does not use `check_coverage.py`'s weaker count. Evidence: counts per status and class, the lists.
**Runs.** python.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R01.0000 (1 row). Q18 judges every row of `requirements.json` and the 71 RC items although only R01.0000 lists it; the `passes` of R01.0000 needs the Q18 verdict PASS, which is computed first and does not depend on that `passes` (K13).

### Q19 Claim ledger

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Claim ledger: every visible-result datum has two independent sources or a flag; no lead-only support."
**Definition.** P.2 to P.4 (p. 136): claims (subject, predicate, time interval, place, unit, quantity, hedges, author, date written, source id, page, character-offset span), claim_support, conflicts; "A datum is documented only when a ledger claim from a non-lead source supports exactly it; a datum affecting a visible result needs two independent sources or an explicit flag (Gate Q19). Corroboration counts only independent witnesses (two accounts copying the same report are one)." D.4.3 stage 7 (p. 126): a claim reaches documented "when at least two independent sources support it, or one Tier 1 source. Single-source claims are kept and marked single-source."
- **Q19.1 Census** of every visible datum (positions and times, counts, kit items, structure states, weather and sky values, sound properties, vegetation states, hospital and casualty content), by class, with claim ids. **Q19.2 Ledger link:** each visible datum names claim ids that resolve in `claims` and `claim_support` to a span in a stored source. **Q19.3 Independence:** two sources are independent when neither is in the other's `independent_of` ancestry and they share no root (the root is the source both copy, for example the Official Records; a state monument volume that quotes the Official Records is derivative; the 1876 Bachelder troop maps are a compilation of the Official Records and testimony, not an independent witness; a later account that copies an earlier one counts once; leads (Wikipedia, forums, blogs, auction pages, wikis) never count). **Q19.4 Two or a flag:** at least two independent non-lead sources, or an explicit flag shown in the product ("single source", "lead only", "inferred", "procedural"); one Tier 1 source does not remove the need for the flag (A-07). **Q19.5 No lead-only support** of any documented datum. **Q19.6 Conflicts:** claims that disagree on time, place, number or sequence are grouped in `conflicts`; each value's support is computed by Q19.3; the default view's choice is recorded (class CF). **Q19.7 Built once:** later work queries the ledger and does not re-derive claims (P.4); a claim without a span fails.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a visible datum. PASS iff two independent non-lead sources support it. COVERED (the gate text has the branch) iff the flag is shown in the product and an entry names where the second source would come from. FAIL otherwise.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q19` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q19.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the claim ledger (announced by the world-content workstream, absent; the position ledgers and troop-map claims exist), the Source Manifest with `independent_of`, `date_written` and reliability tiers, `app/tools/claim-ledger.mjs`, the census. Python and node, light. Evidence: claim ids, the independence roots, the flag.
**Runs.** python, node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R03.0000, R03.C-001..C-047, R21.0000 (49 rows).
**State.** Ledgers exist for positions only; no index from each visible datum to its claims, no independence relation, no flag field (SPEC-B). Expect FAIL. Pass 4 (`verify_positions.py A` to `E`, sample 12, seed 3, 00:13 UTC, exit 0 each; read from SPEC-B): the `fidelity_check` counts per ledger are A `passed_full_read` 359, `corrected` 307, `downgraded` 1, `not_rechecked` 21; B 1,284, 541, 14 and 25; C 364, 156, 4 and 14; D 188, 224 and 1; E 130 and 70; `weak_source` is true on no quote of A to D and on 2 of E. No claim-ledger file for Section P exists (`GOVERNANCE/claim_ledger*`, `app/data/claim-ledger.json` and `app/data/claims.json` absent at 01:03 UTC; `app/data/claim-dispositions.json` exists and is the contract's one row per claim read), so the datum-to-claims index and the independence relation are still missing.

### Q20 Cavalry presence

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Cavalry presence: every documented cavalry unit appears at its documented times with its manifest, horses and arms."
**Definition.** K.8 (p. 135): cavalry kit is first-class, with the same manifest depth plus horse furniture, horse colour and type distributions, guidons and weapons; Spencer, Sharps, Burnside, Gallager, Smith, Merrill and Colt arms are assigned per regiment with the July 1 versus July 3 Spencer distinction preserved. B.6.15 (pp. 85-90): the orders of battle (B.6.15.1 and B.6.15.2, p. 85), the action table in time order from June 26 to July 16 with conflicts as ranges (B.6.15.3, p. 86), dress, arms and horses (B.6.15.4 and B.6.15.5, pp. 87-88), tactics and the 14 states for the engine (B.6.15.6 and B.6.15.7, p. 89), conflicts and gaps (B.6.15.8 and B.6.15.9, p. 89). D.2 V9 (p. 124): each cavalry fight has timed rows; Spencer carbines appear only where sourced; gait-cycle foot-contact test.
- **Q20.1 The set.** Every cavalry unit of the verified OOB (regiments, troops, horse-artillery batteries) of both armies, including those documented as detached or absent (never drawn, with the record). **Q20.2 Times.** Each unit is present exactly in the intervals of its documented actions (the B.6.15.3 table as a machine table, conflicts as scenarios: Stuart's arrival, the East Cavalry Field start at 13:00 or 15:00, Hunterstown), and arrives and leaves by march (Q04, Q05, Q06). **Q20.3 Manifest** for every soldier type present (trooper, horse holder, officer, trumpeter, skirmishers where documented). **Q20.4 Horses.** Counts by rider, led and draught horses; one trooper in four holds four horses when dismounted (p. 89); colour and type distributions, furniture and condition per regiment; horse casualties. **Q20.5 Arms.** Carbine, revolver and sabre per regiment from the sources; Spencers only in the 5th Michigan (all) and part of the 6th Michigan on July 3, never on Buford's line on July 1; no Henry (Q03). **Q20.6 States.** The 14 states of B.6.15.7 (march column to absent) are time-keyed per unit; dismounted fights show horse holders, thin skirmish lines and breechloader reloading; charges show the documented ground; the gait-cycle foot-contact test passes.

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (cavalry unit, check). PASS iff the check holds. No COVERED; brigade-level dress and arms that are thin are inferred or plausible-procedural, marked and registered, and the item then passes only against the flagged value.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q20` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q20.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `kit/cavalry_units.json` (62 cards), `app/src/history/cavalry.mjs`, `app/data/cavalry-horses.json`, the verified OOB (Q01), the action table as a machine table (to be built from B.6.15.3; `cavalry_units.json` holds an `action_table_note` only). Node and python, light. Evidence per unit: presence intervals against the table, manifest completeness, horse and arm counts.
