### Q21 Vegetation state check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Vegetation state check: crop, canopy and ground-cover state per field per date within the documented-or-ranged schedule."
**Definition.** L.7 (p. 135): a `vegetation_states` schedule per field, woodlot, orchard and yard: crop type, height and colour range for the date, tree leaf-out and canopy density for early July, orchard fruit state (register the gap), undergrowth, and battle damage over time; every value tagged and ranged; "Grass height has no sourced value in the notes". G.6 (p. 134): crops are attributes per field with documented-or-assumed status and height ranges, "never a single invented value"; chestnut and other post-1863 changes are a documented correction layer, verified before use. B.10.3 (p. 112). D.2 V13 test (p. 124): every vegetation polygon cites a source or is tagged inferred; field state at time t matches the state schedule.
- **Q21.1 Schedule.** For every polygon class (field, woodlot, orchard, yard and the classes the sources name) and every date of the window plus November 19, a `vegetation_states` entry gives crop or tree type, height range, colour range, canopy density, leaf-out, fruit state, undergrowth, ground cover (grass height, wetness) and damage events, each with tier and sources or a register entry; no single invented value. **Q21.2 Scene equals schedule.** For every polygon class and date, and on both sides of every damage-event boundary, the scene's realized parameters (crop height distribution, placed tree counts and canopy, leaf coverage, ground-cover state) lie inside the schedule's value or range. A scene that draws no trees or crops, or draws the modern aerial vegetation, is FAIL. **Q21.3 Events.** Documented changes occur in their windows and reverse on backward scrub (the Wheatfield wheat trampled or clipped from about 16:30 on July 2, S-033; orchard damage; felling for the Culp's Hill works). **Q21.4 Anachronism.** No species or plant structure later than 1863; the chestnut question is a correction layer verified before use. **Q21.5 Pictures.** Photo-match of vegetation where a documented position exists (Q16). **Q21.6 November 19.** Late autumn: trees leaf-off or nearly so, grass dormant, inferred (S-085).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (polygon class, date, parameter). PASS iff the scene value lies inside the schedule range and the schedule entry is tagged and sourced or registered. FAIL if the scene departs from the schedule or draws nothing. The evidence for the schedule itself is judged by Q02 and the class rule of R20.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q21` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q21.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `app/data/vegetation.json` (`bt-vegetation/1`), the ext module `app/src/ext/vegetation/` (listed in `src/ext/index.mjs`) and its hooks `checkVegetation(model, opts)` (extended at 00:41 UTC: undergrowth and fruit states, the share of trees kept, the species of Q21.4, the states asked for in descending against ascending time in `summary.backward` for Q21.3, and `summary.byClass`), `stateAt(model, unit, tMin, choices)` and `sceneMinute(t)` of `model.mjs`, and `checkScene(model, opts)` of `scene-check.mjs` (`opts.project(lng, lat)` and `opts.ground {W, H}` are the theatre's terrain projection and size; its result is labelled `Q21.2 scene equals schedule`) (rows of `app/docs/GATE-HOOKS.md`, 17,067 bytes at 00:41 UTC), the tests `vegetation.test.mjs` (26 tests), `vegetation-shell.test.mjs` (6) and `vegetation-scene.test.mjs` (3) as evidence that the hooks can fail, `tools/bt/vegetation/verify_claims.py` and `build_vegetation.py --check` (read-only: exits 1 when the committed file is not what the script makes), `evidence_corrections/web-nps-landscape.jsonl`. The hook's default grid (06:00, 12:00 and 18:00 of every day, every event boundary, 19 Nov noon, six sets of the three vegetation conflicts) is the floor; the runner adds both sides of every event boundary and every scenario of S* that reaches a vegetation unit. The floors that the hooks carry (a claim needs a quote of at least 12 characters, one of the two allowed tiers and a place in its source; the check must cover more than 400,000 states; `checkScene` must see more than 60,000 trees and 50,000 ground-cover triangles with trees placed at 75 to 125 per cent of a wood's density and 60 to 150 per cent of an orchard's) are minimums for the runner and are never lowered (T-Q21-1 to T-Q21-3, 10.1, DD-G17). Node or python, light; the swgl picture check (that the drawn trees and crops are what the model says) is a separate item.
**Runs.** node, python; pictures swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R20.0000 (1 row); R20.V-<class> when generated (4.11).
**State** (from SPEC-B pass 4 for 00:13 to 00:46 UTC; for this file at 01:06 UTC). `app/data/vegetation.json` (`bt-vegetation/1`) keeps changing: 883 units and 348 claims at 19:56 UTC on 13 November, 925 and 330 at 23:35, 925 and 377 at 00:13, 927 and 432 at 00:45, and 927 units, 481 claims and 17 gaps (1,787,180 bytes) at 01:06. `checkVegetation` ran at 00:13 UTC (SPEC-B): `ok` true, 555,000 states (925 units, 100 times, 6 scenario sets), 0 failures, `pure` true; extended, it gave 556,200 states and 0 failures at 00:45, and `checkScene` 157,590 states (34 times, 5 choice sets, 927 units), 0 failures, 124,549 trees and 147,165 ground-cover triangles (`spec-b-evidence/pass4/veg_scene_check.out`); the builder's `--check` printed `check: same`. `vegetation.test.mjs` (26 tests), `vegetation-shell.test.mjs` (6) and `vegetation-scene.test.mjs` (3) passed in SPEC-B's runs (00:14 to 00:44); the last two were run again for this file at 01:06 UTC: 10 tests, 10 pass (4.4 s). These checks test the model and the numbers handed to the graphics card: the first does not read a picture (its `notRun` list says so) and the second reads what the layer writes to the state texture and not what the shaders draw from it. What stands between them and the gate (read from SPEC-B Q21): the schedule is the data's own and mostly procedural (of 925 units 539 plausible-procedural, 133 inferred and 253 documented by their weakest part; field crops 1 documented, 10 inferred and 539 plausible-procedural draws from a district mix); the module draws on WebGL2 only and every picture of it came from a software renderer through `vegetation-browser.mjs`, which its header calls `(not a gate)` and which nobody ran for these notes; the ground picture under it is the 2022 aerial photograph (`VEGETATION-NOTES.md` section 5 says modern trees on verges, fence lines and in the town still show); 263 of the 377 claims were not placed on a polygon and cannot be checked against a field; no 1863 photograph is compared with the state (Q21.5, Q16). By this file's rules the model-side items can pass, every picture item is NOT-RUN and Q21.2 fails while the scene draws the modern aerial vegetation, so the verdict is FAIL or INCOMPLETE and not PASS.

### Q22 Weather state check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Weather state check: scene weather matches the weather timeline at every time."
**Definition.** L.2 and L.3 (p. 135): `weather_states` built from documented records (temperatures and conditions per day, fog at July 2 dawn, afternoon drizzle July 2, thunderstorms July 3, heavy rain from late July 3 into July 4, Plum Run overflow); the wind conflict (12 mph versus about 2 mph) carried, with 2 to 6 mph rendered pending resolution; "Do not use the unsourced "88F on July 1" or climatological records as 1863 observations"; humidity, dew point, pressure and visibility modelled, plausible-procedural, parameters exposed; rain wets ground, raises mud, floods low ground. L.8, L.9. B.10.1 (p. 110) tables; B.10.5 step 16 (p. 114): one timeline value drives light, fog, cloud, rain, wetness and exposure; presets cross-fade over 10 to 30 simulated minutes. D.2 V8 (p. 124): the weather timeline matches the B.10 table.
- **Q22.1 Timeline.** The weather timeline holds every documented value with its tier and source or its plausible-procedural parameters and equals the B.10.1 table, including the wind range and both wind scenarios (C-045). **Q22.2 Scene equals timeline.** At every grid minute and for every weather scenario each channel of the scene (precipitation type and rate, cloud cover, wetness, wind vector, fog density, temperature, creek level, mud and dust) equals the timeline at that time; during a cross-fade it equals the documented interpolation of the preset, to T-Q22-1 (the timeline's own precision: temperature to one degree F as printed, discrete channels exact). **Q22.3 One state.** Every consumer reads the same state object: the renderer, the audio planner (temperature for Q13), the effects (wind drift of smoke and dust), the ground (mud, ricochet), vegetation, the sky (Q10, Q11); two copies that can diverge are FAIL, shown by injecting a changed timeline value and observing every consumer. **Q22.4 Tiers.** No condition is presented as documented without a source; modelled parameters are marked. **Q22.5 Drawn.** The weather state is drawn or heard on every renderer path claimed; a state computed and not drawn is not a pass for that path (DD-27: the WebGPU path draws no weather yet, EG-W3D-G08).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item (channel, grid time, scenario) and (renderer path). PASS iff the scene channel equals the timeline within T-Q22-1 at that time. No COVERED; humidity, dew point, pressure and visibility are modelled, tagged and registered, so the scene can match them.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q22` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q22` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q22.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `environment/weather_timeline.json` (`segments`, `cloud_readings`, `jacobs_readings`, `wetness_schedule`, `wind`, `conflicts_carried_as_ranges`, `epilogue_nov_1863`, `gaps`), `app/src/environment.mjs` (`Environment`), `app/src/weather3d/timeline.mjs`. Python imports `check_environment.gate_q22()` (31 checks of the timeline itself; it reads `tmp/pdf/plan-clean.txt`, absent here, so the runner points its path in memory at `BIBLE/plan-clean.txt`), `weather_at`; a node child compares scene and timeline at every grid minute per weather scenario; a swgl child draws a sample. Heavy.
**Runs.** python, node; the drawn state on swgl WebGL2 (real-GPU confirmation owed).
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R15.0000 (1 row).

The per-minute scene-against-timeline scan does not exist. The main site's weather checks (`tests/environment/weather.test.mjs` passes, `verify-weather.test.mjs` fails by design) do not read the theatre's `environment/weather_timeline.json`. ### Q23 Marking check

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Marking check: every non-documented item carries a render flag; no unmarked procedural content."
**Definition.** U (p. 137): provenance overlay showing tiers by colour or glyph at the unit and object level, "hover or click shows source and register link. A published “what you are seeing” document accompanies the product. No unmarked synthesized content may appear as documented (Gate Q23)." App. B (pp. 138-139): `render_flag` 0 documented, 1 inferred, 2 plausible-procedural. C.10 (p. 121): a substitute is shown with that mark in the overlay, never documented. Z (p. 138): "labeling procedural content as documented; hiding a gap in the product" are forbidden. B.10.5 criterion 6 (p. 114): tier tags legible at night. DD-14 (audio disclosure in the panel and overlay), DD-22 (tier marks visible by default), DEC-006 (the process label dropped; tier marking stays).
- **Q23.1 Census of content classes** drawn or heard: units, figures and their items, officers' and commanders' appearance, horses, guns, the artillery train, works, fences, buildings, markers, roads, the ground, vegetation, weather, effects (smoke, fire, muzzle flash, blood, mud), sounds, casualties, hospital activity, animation clips, the Events list and cards, the epilogue; a class loaded and absent from the census is FAIL. **Q23.2 Flag in data.** Every item whose tier is not documented has `render_flag` 1 or 2 (open gaps have the gap marker class); a generator output with no `procedural` block or flag is FAIL. **Q23.3 Flag in the product.** With the overlay at its default state every visible entity of every class with flag 1 or 2 shows the glyph or colour plus dash (never colour alone); its card shows the tier, source and register link; sampled in frames and checked exhaustively on draw lists. **Q23.4 Substitutes.** Modern motion bases, synthesized or modern-recorded audio, open textures and models carry the "modern reconstruction" mark in the asset manifest, the register and the overlay (DD-14). **Q23.5 Individual level.** Individual soldiers' positions, actions and faces are marked plausible-procedural (EG-0001). **Q23.6 Night.** Marks legible at night (Q10.6). **Q23.7 Register.** Every register entry with a tier other than documented has a non-placeholder `marking` (the existing check; necessary and not sufficient, X-28). **Q23.8 Gaps visible.** Every open gap in scope has its visible marker (U-14).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a content item or class. PASS iff every item of the class is marked. An unmarked item is FAIL; nothing registered replaces a mark in the product.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q23` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q23` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q23.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the draw lists, the overlay code (`app/src/overlay.mjs`, `app/src/structures/overlay.mjs`), the ext hooks `tierName`, `tierMark`, `tierMark.style` of `app/src/ui/extensions.mjs` (a `null` tier on visible content is an unmarked item), `check_coverage.coverage()['Q23']` for the register half, the data flags. Python (register), node (data and draw lists), swgl (the marks are visible with the overlay on by default, DD-22). Heavy.
**Runs.** python, node, swgl.

No scan over every drawn class exists; the hooks judge one tier statement at a time; the ground is disclosed by one note (`GROUND_NOTE`), not per item. ### Q24 Screenshot regression for the viewer

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Screenshot regression for the viewer."
**Definition.** App. E (p. 142): gate for R01 and R19. D.7 phase 12 (p. 130): the viewer with time navigation; path re-render reproducible. The plan gives no baseline, tolerance or view list (A-27).
- **Q24.1 Baseline set B.** A named set of (view, time, scenario, renderer class, viewport, interface state) covering every panel and mode of the viewer, the scrubber states, bookmarks, the scenario switch, the provenance overlay on and off, day, night, weather, the epilogue chapter, at the viewports of `tools/accept/thresholds.json` (1440 x 900 and 390 x 844 today; FINAL-ACCEPTANCE step 7 names 360, 390, 768, 1024, 1440 and 1920 widths). **Q24.2 Compare.** Each fresh screenshot equals its baseline within T-Q24-1 (per-pixel colour threshold at most 0.1) and T-Q24-2 (differing-pixel ratio at most 0.002), through `tools/accept/lib/imgdiff.mjs diffPngs`. **Q24.3 Baselines are pinned.** Each baseline has a hash, the renderer class, the build and the decision that approved it. The runner never writes or replaces a baseline: `python3 tools/bt/gates/baseline_pin.py --propose` writes candidates under `gate_results/baselines/proposed/` and pinning them into `baselines/pinned/<renderer class>/` needs a recorded decision after the images were looked at. A software-GL baseline detects change between software runs and cannot certify the real GPU's picture. **Q24.4 Determinism.** Two runs on one build give equal screenshots within the measured noise floor; a non-deterministic frame is made deterministic (fixed seed, time, viewport) or excluded with a register entry. **Q24.5 Re-render.** A saved camera path re-renders identically from its cache hash (D.2 O1, p. 123).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a baseline entry. PASS iff the fresh image equals its pinned baseline within the thresholds on each renderer class claimed; NOT-RUN for a renderer class with no baseline. A FAIL is a regression, not a register item.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q24` (heavy: `tools/horde/bench.sh python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q24` started in the background, log in `gate_results/logs/`); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q24.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the viewer, the browser harness (`app/tests/lib.mjs`, `BT_OUT`, `BT_GL`), the baseline folders. swgl, heavy; two runs per entry for Q24.4.
**Runs.** swgl here; **NOT RUNNABLE HERE: the real-GPU baseline**, made on the owner's machine.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R01.0000, R19.0000 (2 rows); OA01.
**State.** No viewer regression test, baseline set or tolerance exists; the main-site harness has `diffPngs` and thresholds; `scrub.spec.mjs` and `casualties.spec.mjs` test same-run pixel identity (SPEC-B). Pass 4 of SPEC-B (read from that file): `app/tests/results/weather3d-webgpu/` (the weather and effects lane's stored run of 13 November, with a README) holds 16 WebGPU pictures that its owner says were looked at, `compare-summary.json` (WebGL2 against WebGPU per case: mean absolute difference per channel, rmse, p99, the shares of pixels over 8 and over 24 levels, luminance and sprite counts) and two budget-tier runs; `checkCompare(compareSummary, limit)` takes a default limit of 6 levels, `a harness value, not a project gate`, and `weather3d-gpu.test.mjs` pins that the 16 pictures exist. None of it is a baseline set for the viewer, a compare runner with a tolerance or a real-GPU picture.

### Q25 Library audit coverage

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Library audit coverage: every source in the acquisition list is ingested, blocked with register entry, or scheduled with a reason."
**Definition.** X.1 (p. 137): execute the acquisition list in Appendix C.1 end to end; "Track each source's status (scheduled, ingested, blocked, partial) in library/status.json and register blocks." X.3 (a) to (k): per-source audit steps (copy, edition and OCR quality and a second witness; clean OCR; split and extract; reliability tier and date written; claims; conflicts; prefer sources closer to the event; detect copying; record negative results; update the ledger and `library/status.json`; open register entries for unreadable or blocked sources). X.4: throttle about one request per second, check terms and robots rules, private working store for public-domain OCR, never redistribute copyrighted text. D.4.2 (p. 126): print-only works are acquisition targets; D.4.3 stage 14 (p. 126): read, blocked with reason, print-only. Z.7 (p. 138): the library status file shows no source silently skipped (Q25). Appendix C.1 (pp. 139-142).
- **Q25.1 The list.** A machine list of acquisition rows: every row of Appendix C.1.1 to C.1.4, every category of X.1, the 45 catalogue items of B.9, the reachable library of D.4.1, and every source that a recorded decision or an owner addition names for a requirement (the medical works of DEC-OA10-12 item 5 for OA12, 3.1 OA-G12), each with a stable id, aliases merged; regenerated from the plan text and the decisions and diffed against the status files; a row with no status is FAIL (silently skipped, X-48). **Q25.2 One terminal status per row.** `ingested`; `blocked` with an open register id and a recorded failed lookup (host, locator, date, response; no circumvention); `scheduled` with a reason, a named next action and a work-queue entry (never "for now", Law cl. 1). `print-only` is recorded as `blocked` with the reason print-only and an acquisition-target register entry; `partial` is split into an ingested part and a remainder that is scheduled or blocked; every status word of the three status files (`status.json`, `status_2.json`, `status_3.json`: eleven words, eight of them outside the three above) is mapped to those three by the table below (A-28, X-25, DD-G11), and a word that is not in the table is FAIL (unmapped status), never skipped and never guessed. **Q25.3 Evidence of ingestion.** File hashes, acquisition date, rights and licence note, OCR quality and, for the Official Records, the second-witness diff where a witness is reachable (X.3 a). **Q25.4 Terms and rights.** The request log respects the throttle; terms and robots rules were checked and recorded; blocked hosts (on 2026-11-13 babel.hathitrust.org and ehistory.osu.edu answered with their own bot block) are recorded as blocked and neither retried nor circumvented; copyrighted works are cited and consulted where lawfully reachable, never bulk-copied (p. 134, E.6). **Q25.5 Assets.** Each motion clip, sample, texture and model has a licence, source and method in the asset manifest; non-commercial items are excluded from any release export (p. 121, C.10). **Q25.6 Owner-only acts** (purchases, accounts, permission requests) are listed as exact owner steps, never done by Claude.

**The status words of the three files and what each maps to** (counted on 2026-11-13 at about 21:10 UTC from the files). The runner merges the files by source id: the highest-numbered file that holds an id wins, a later entry that says "unchanged" keeps the earlier record, and a difference of words between files is written into the item's evidence. 49 ids result: 11 only in `status.json` (36 ids), 7 only in `status_2.json` (32), 6 only in `status_3.json` (6, from the photomatch task of bt-web, 20:13 UTC), and 25 in the first two (none in the third). The join of a status row to its acquisition entries (`acquired.json`, `acquired_2.json`, `acquired_3.json`) is an id map built by the runner (`tools/bt/gates/lib/library_ids.json`); an acquired entry that no status row reaches is an untracked source, FAIL (silently skipped, p. 138, Z.7).

| Word | Ids after merging | Terminal status of the gate | What the record must hold, otherwise FAIL (incomplete record) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ingested` | 19 | `ingested` | the Q25.3 evidence: hash and acquisition date of the file or text in an `acquired*.json` entry, rights basis, text quality. In `status_2.json` and `status_3.json` the word means acquired and hash-recorded, not mined; the claims are judged by Q02 and Q19 |
| `scheduled` | 5 | `scheduled` | a reason that is not "for now", a named next action and a work-queue entry; a note such as `data, not text: outside this workstream` is a reason and not a next action |
| `blocked` | 3 | `blocked` | an open register id; the failed lookup (host, locator, date, response); no circumvention. A block is stale, and the item FAIL as retry owed, when a request to the same host succeeded after the failed lookup (an `acquired*.json` `fetched_at`) |
| `blocked-to-prior-research` | 2 | `blocked` | as `blocked`; the earlier research must be a dated record, and the staleness rule applies (LOC-MAPS says `retry at run time`) |
| `print-only` | 2 | `blocked` | the reason print-only, an open acquisition-target register entry and the owner step (a purchase or a loan) written beside it; the work is cited and consulted where lawfully reachable and never bulk-copied (p. 134, E.6) |
| `viewer-only` | 1 | `blocked` | the reason viewer-only: the terms read (host, locator, date, what they permit) and an open register entry; the source is never copied |
| `partial` | 12 | two items: the acquired part `ingested`, the remainder `scheduled` or `blocked` | a record for each part; a `partial` row with no record of its remainder is FAIL |
| `targeted-lookup-only` | 1 | two items: the lookups made, each hash-recorded, `ingested`; the bulk remainder `blocked` | bulk use needs a permission that nobody is asked for (DEC-019 clarification: no permission requests are sent; the owner may change that), written as the reason; with no lookup made the whole row is `scheduled` with its next action |
| `finder-not-authority` | 1 | by evidence: `ingested` as a lead when it was acquired and hash-recorded, otherwise `scheduled` with its next action | the tier is lead (p. 134, E.1, E.7): it supports no documented datum |
| `licence-checked` | 2 | one item for each source the row names: acquired and licensed `ingested` (asset manifest, Q25.5); not yet acquired `scheduled` (an account or a licence acceptance is an owner-only step, written beside it); a licence that forbids the use `blocked` with the licence text read | a row that names no source is FAIL |
| `unusable` | 1 (`status_3.json`) | the acquired file stays hash-recorded and gives no `ingested` part; the failed read is a `blocked` item; a route not yet tried is a `scheduled` item | for the failed read: what was tested, the result, the date and an open entry for an unreadable source (X.3 k, p. 137). For PM-GARDNER-SKETCHBOOK-TEXT the text layer holds no plate description and the page images are not fetched, so the page-image route is a `scheduled` item with its next action |
| any other word | 0 | none | FAIL: unmapped status |

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a source row. PASS iff `ingested` with its evidence. COVERED (the gate text has the branch) iff blocked with its failed-lookup record and open entry, or scheduled with reason, next action and queue entry. FAIL for no status or an incomplete record. For the R12 and R21 sub-requirements only PASS (and "used", 4.7 CT-2) counts toward `passes`.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q25` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q25.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the three status files of `GOVERNANCE/library/`, `status.json` (36 sources), `status_2.json` (32) and `status_3.json` (6: PM-LOC-DERIVATIVES, PM-LOC-RECORDS, PM-LOC-MASTERS, PM-NPS-THEN-AND-NOW, PM-NPS-SLAUGHTER-PEN-PDF, PM-GARDNER-SKETCHBOOK-TEXT; its `_doc` lists the words ingested, partial, blocked and unusable), merged as the table above says; the acquisition ledgers `acquired.json` (34 items and 1 blocked), `acquired_2.json` (99 items, 9 reverified, 9 blocked or failed) and `acquired_3.json` (78 items and 4 blocked or failed at 21:00 UTC; the bt-web tasks merge into it while they work), `candidates.json` (12), `figures-ledger.json` (2), `terrain/terrain_sources.json` (45 rows in five tables), the regenerated list (Q25.1), the id map between status rows and acquired items. Python, light. Evidence per row: status, hashes, the record of each non-ingested state.
**Runs.** python.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R12.001..R12.045, R12.0000, R08.0000, R21.0000 (48 rows); R21.L-<batch> when generated.
**State** (counted at about 21:10 UTC from the three status files; SPEC-B knew two). 49 source ids after merging (11 only in the first file, 7 only in the second, 6 only in the third, 25 in the first two); eleven status words, eight of them outside the gate's three (`partial`, `print-only`, `blocked-to-prior-research`, `viewer-only`, `targeted-lookup-only`, `finder-not-authority`, `licence-checked`, `unusable`); no status file carries a register id, so every `blocked` item is FAIL until its open entry exists, and none carries a work-queue entry, so every `scheduled` item is FAIL until it has one; LOC-MAPS (where `loc.gov IIIF`, note `retry at run time`) and NARA-WARREN are `blocked-to-prior-research`, both undated, and `acquired_3.json` records successful requests to www.loc.gov on 2026-11-13, so LOC-MAPS is a stale block (retry owed); Appendix C.1 is not a machine list (SPEC-B). Re-counted for this file at 01:00 UTC with `spec-a-evidence/library_words.py`: the same 49 ids and 11 words, and the table above reproduces exactly (0 mismatches); the acquisition ledgers hold 34, 99 and 78 items. The medical works that DEC-OA10-12 item 5 names are rows of the list by Q25.1: Letterman's `Medical Recollections` of 1866 is `ingested` in `status_2.json` (`LETTERMAN-1866`, with an entry in `acquired_2.json`); the `Medical and Surgical History of the War of the Rebellion`, Gross's `Manual of Military Surgery`, Tripler and Blackman's handbook and the Hospital Steward's Manual were downloaded by the medical workstream (its ledger `docs/battle-theatre/medical/SOURCES.md`, 00:50 UTC) and are in no status file and no acquisition ledger of `GOVERNANCE/library/`, so each is a row with no status, FAIL under Q25.1, until a status row exists (request note `horde/requests/bt-gates-medical-library-status.md`). `status_3.json` and `acquired_3.json` are written by the bt-web tasks while they work, so a second count can differ from the first.

### Q26 Casualty and hospital flow

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Casualty and hospital flow: every casualty event has a carry path to an aid station, field hospital or burial; field hospitals show the documented activity in the documented interval."
**Definition.** D.3.2 items 3 to 5 (p. 125): the loss-constraint solver assigns casualty events across the unit's documented engagement windows so totals match the documented counts within their stated range; known fates are pinned; "Wounded flow. Casualty events spawn the evacuation chain (stretcher or walking wounded, ambulance, regimental and divisional hospital, Letterman system) with times from the medical notes". B.8.5 (pp. 97-99): Letterman roles, the hospital table (B.8.5.2), night operations (B.8.5.3), carrying and evacuation behaviours with time windows (B.8.5.4, p. 99), horses and livestock (B.8.6, p. 99). B.8.8 must-show 6 (p. 102): operating tables by lantern at Spangler (S-049), the amputation pile at Weikert (S-047), ambulance flow, rain on exposed wounded (S-070), burial and horse burning after July 4. D.7 phase 8 exit (p. 129): "Regimental totals match documented losses within source range; same seed same output; time-jump equals forward play". Z (p. 138): leaving field hospitals or casualty carrying out is forbidden.
- **Q26.1 Every casualty event has a path.** For every killed, wounded, missing or captured event (from the solver and the pinned fates): wounded have a carry path (walking, helped, stretcher, ambulance) to a regimental aid station, a division or corps field hospital or a general hospital; killed have a documented disposition (left until a burial party, shallow grave, trench with headboard where known, the Spangler orchard burials); missing and captured have the documented disposition (prisoner collection, or a flagged count not animated). No casualty is left without a path and none vanishes. **Q26.2 Path validity.** Continuous, within the bearer, ambulance and walking ranges of Q05, ending at a documented site (B.8.5.2) inside that site's documented operating interval, in the documented sequence and windows of B.8.5.4; tier shown (inferred or plausible-procedural where unsourced). **Q26.3 Hospitals show the documented activity** inside the documented interval and nowhere else: opening and closing times, surgeon and staff counts as ranges, operating tables by lantern at night, the amputation pile, wounded counts as ranges (the Spangler count is C-038), ambulance flow, rain on exposed wounded on July 4, hand-offs (Weikert to Rock Creek and White Run, III and V Corps overnight, Granite Schoolhouse on July 3: p. 99), Camp Letterman from its construction on July 10 (opening July 20 or 22 is C-044; closing November 20, 1863 or January 1864 is disputed, p. 134, E.5). **Q26.4 Totals.** Regimental casualty totals equal the documented losses within the source range; same seed same output; a time jump equals forward play. **Q26.5 Ambulance Corps and trains** follow the documented ratios and dates (p. 98); vehicles pass Q03. **Q26.6 Horses.** Casualties and carcasses at the documented farms and battery positions at the documented times, the remainder plausible-procedural (B.8.6).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a casualty event; a hospital site and interval. PASS iff the property holds. No COVERED; litter-bearer detail, hospital rosters and many times are gaps (p. 5, coverage map O5), plausible-procedural, marked, entries open.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q26` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q26.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: the casualty events and flow data (announced by the world-content workstream, absent), `structures/hospitals.json` (23 sites), the solver output (`casualties.test.mjs`), `app/data/engagements.json`, `casualty-sources.json`, the cache. Node, light.
**Runs.** node.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R05.0000, R13.0000 (2 rows); the S rows with `H-`, `GROUP-` and `PERSON-` objects (3.2).
**State.** Casualties fall in their printed windows and stay where they fell; nothing carries a casualty and no schedule drives hospital activity: every item BLOCKED-INPUT (SPEC-B). Pass 4 of SPEC-B and a check for this file at 01:09 UTC: OA12 (field hospitals, battle trauma and period-accurate treatment) is mapped to this gate by DEC-OA10-12 (3.1 OA-G12); that decision's content note and its setting `Graphic detail` (Documentary as the delegated default, Reduced as the alternative) are in no file under `app/src`, `app/data` or `app/tests` (searched for the words at 01:09), the casualty-flow data is still absent (`app/data` holds `casualty-sources.json` only), and the output of the workflow script `bt-medical.mjs` is, so far, evidence files under `docs/battle-theatre/medical/evidence/` and no layer. ### Q27 Budget gate

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Budget gate: spend and compute against the Appendix F caps reported; cap breach triggers the stop condition (Section C and D.5.12)." The paragraph that follows the table in the plan is stored by `gates.json` inside Q27: "Run a fresh-context verification subagent for each milestone with instructions to flag only correctness and requirement gaps. Do not self-certify what you have not run." (A-30)
**Definition.** Section C (p. 133): every phase has a token and dollar cap in `budget/phase_budget.json`; "A cap hit is logged to the Decision Log only if finishing the phase would exceed it; the cap is raised only by the owner. Caps pace work; they never remove work." App. F (p. 143): all values start blank, are set from the first measured vertical slice and only the owner may raise them. D.5.12 (p. 128): phase cap, maximum failed retries, "No new passing features in N sessions" halts the loop and writes a report for the owner; "A phase that exits the budget with failing exit tests is not marked complete; it is escalated"; a spend-rate check. D.8.2 item 10 (p. 131): budget allocation and when to pause is the owner's decision. Z (p. 138): "letting a budget cap remove a requirement" is forbidden. J (p. 134): `progress.md` records spend against the cap.
- **Q27.1 Caps set.** Every phase row of App. F (phases 0 to 13 and the reserve) and every global guard has a value and an owner-approved flag; setting or approving a cap is an owner-only act (DD-25); with caps null the item is BLOCKED-INPUT. **Q27.2 Spend recorded** per phase (tokens, dollars, GPU hours, wall clock, storage, failures, retries) from machine records. **Q27.3 Reported:** each milestone report states spend against every cap and guard including the reserve. **Q27.4 Stop condition:** when a cap is hit the phase halts, the report is written, the Decision Log gets the entry if finishing would exceed the cap, the phase is not marked complete when its exit tests fail, and no requirement, item or check is dropped because of the cap. **Q27.5 Independent verification:** each milestone has a record of a fresh-context verification run (id, date, instructions "flag only correctness and requirement gaps", findings, disposition of every finding).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: (phase or guard, cap) and (milestone, verification record). PASS iff the cap is owner-approved, spend is recorded and reported, and any breach was stopped and escalated. With no cap there is nothing to be inside of: BLOCKED-INPUT with the owner step written (approve the caps after the vertical slice).
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q27` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q27.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `budget/phase_budget.json` (15 phases, every cap null, `owner_approved` false), spend logs, `progress.md`, the decision log. Python, light.
**Runs.** python.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R08.0000, R09.0000 (2 rows).
**State.** Nothing computes it; no spend record; no verification-run record; verdict INCOMPLETE.

### Q28 Epilogue gate (R22)

**Text (p. 136, Q).** "Epilogue gate (R22): the Nov 19 chapter has every B.12.2 row and every S-080 to S-088 state with a tier; Lincoln's clothing, voice and wording are used only where two sources were read in the audit and otherwise marked reconstruction in the viewer; the version of the address shown is a selectable alternative in the conflict register; no off-Gettysburg event appears in the film path; the jump is labeled as a device."
**Definition.** B.12 (pp. 116-119): B.12.1 the jump (p. 117), B.12.2 rows E-001 to E-014 (pp. 117-118), B.12.3 states S-080 to S-088 (p. 118), B.12.4 Lincoln and the front-row viewpoint (p. 118), B.12.6 (p. 119). R22 (p. 133, A): the default film path ends with the July 4 events at Gettysburg; at the end of July 4 the animation jumps to the Gettysburg cemetery on November 19, 1863, shows Lincoln from the front row; "Events outside Gettysburg are not shown." D.7 phase 15 (p. 130): "Gate Q28; photo-match against the 1863 photographs; sound intelligibility check from the front row".
- **Q28.1 Rows.** The November 18 and 19 chapter contains each of E-001 to E-014 with tier, source and the conflict or gap note; none dropped. **Q28.2 States.** Each of S-080 to S-088 is present as the state it prints, with a tier; the conflicts S-081 (Oct 17 or Oct 27) and S-083 (platform position: the Evergreen Cemetery side of the dividing fence per Wills 1992 and Frassanito 1995, against earlier park materials, p. 118) are selectable. **Q28.3 Lincoln.** For each attribute of Lincoln (clothing, hat, gloves, mourning band, boots, height, face, posture, voice, accent, wording, seat, horse): used as fact only where two independent non-lead sources were read in the audit and are in the ledger; otherwise shown as reconstruction on screen and in the card. The voice is synthesized from descriptions and labeled a reconstruction, never a recording (no recording of Lincoln exists, p. 118); the figure is built from the 1863 photographs under the commanders' likeness rule (p. 119, B.12.6). **Q28.4 Address versions.** The version shown is one of the values of a conflict record in the register (a new conflict id, A-31) that lists as selectable alternatives the text as delivered and taken down by Hale, the text the reporters printed on the day (the default, p. 118), the Associated Press and Philadelphia Press texts and each manuscript copy; each version carries its source and its word count only if read. **Q28.5 Film path is Gettysburg only.** The default film path is an explicit list of rows, each inside the Gettysburg area (loaded ground, town, cemetery) per the gazetteer; none of T3-168 and later (Monterey Pass, the pursuit, Williamsport, Falling Waters) is on it; those stay scrubbable as optional chapters (p. 117). A journey to Gettysburg is not shown ("Events outside Gettysburg are not shown", p. 133, R22); E-001, Lincoln's arrival at the Gettysburg railroad station on November 18 (p. 117), is inside the area and is shown. **Q28.6 The jump is a device.** The viewer labels the transition as a time jump, not as history; the world state is interpolated through the documented intervening changes (the August purchase of the 17 acres, the layout work, burial parties, the start of reinterments, the season turning from midsummer to late autumn) with tier plausible-procedural (p. 117, B.12.1). **Q28.7 Chapter and scrub.** November 19 is its own chapter on the same scrubbable timeline; the viewer can scrub within it and leave the front-row viewpoint for the free camera (Q12 over the chapter). **Q28.8 Front row.** The viewpoint is a generic period audience member at eye height; seat, distance and sight lines are computed from the platform footprint once S-083 is settled; "speech intelligibility at the front row is a computed result, not a guess" (p. 118). **Q28.9 Pacing.** Everett's oration is compressed with a visible time-skip; Lincoln's remarks play in real time, paced to the shorter range (under two minutes in EWH, a few minutes at most in W-CONS: "Pace the performance to the shorter range", p. 118); the crowd is a range (15,000 to 20,000) with plausible-procedural composition (S-087). **Q28.10 Photographs.** Photo-match of the chapter against the 1863 photographs (Q16), among them the two procession photographs reported as identified at the Library of Congress in 2006 (a claim to verify before use).

**Item and per-item pass rule.** Item: a row, a state, a Lincoln attribute, the Address version, the film path, the jump. PASS iff the check holds. COVERED (the gate text allows it) only for a Lincoln attribute marked reconstruction on screen and in the card with its register entry.
**Runner.** Command: `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py Q28` (light, run directly); output `GOVERNANCE/gate_results/Q28.json` (items `id`, `pass`, `reason`, `evidence`). Inputs: `epilogue_seed.jsonl`, `structures/state_schedule.json` S-080..S-088 and the records WS-080..WS-088, `environment/ephemeris_1863.json` (18 and 19 November), `weather_timeline.json epilogue_nov_1863`, the conflict register (to be extended, X-34), the film-path object and the Nov 19 scene (absent), Hale, the Associated Press and Philadelphia Press reports and the Lincoln manuscripts (to be read; EG-0022, EG-0120). Python and node, the labels on swgl, the intelligibility computation in node. Light.
**Runs.** python, node; labels swgl.
**Gates.** `gate_ids`: R22.0000, R22.0001..0014 (15 rows); the S rows S-080..S-088 (4.3 SS-11).
**State.** Nothing checks the gate: no Address conflict record (`conflict_register.json` has C-001..C-047 only), no film path object, no Nov 19 scene; the 14 rows are `seeded_unverified` with lead sources (Wikipedia, a student blog, a popular compilation; p. 117). INCOMPLETE at best. Pass 4 of SPEC-B (00:2x UTC, by modification time): no input changed (`epilogue_seed.jsonl`, `conflict_register.json` with 47 conflicts and no Address entry, `app/src/clock.mjs` and `environment/` unmodified since 18:30 UTC on 13 November). Each of the 14 epilogue rows has supplementary correction records only (lookups in `evidence_corrections/web-newspapers.jsonl` and `timeline_corrections/`: partially supported, or no passage found in the saved pages) and no row-level record (counted at 01:05 UTC; SPEC-A 1.3: R22 0 of 14 row-level); none of them is a film path, a label or a conflict-register entry.

## 3. Derived gates and domain gates

### 3.1 Derived gates OA-G01 to OA-G12 for the twelve owner-chat rows

The twelve rows `OA01.0000` to `OA12.0000` have `origin: owner_chat` and no `gate_ids`: OA01 to OA09 come from the first owner chat additions and OA10 to OA12 were added on 2026-11-13 (`DEC-OA10-12` in `decision_log.md`, line 325: a delegated decision on how they are handled, not an owner decision; it maps OA10 to the gates Q03, Q07, Q09, Q16 and Q21, OA11 to Q08 and Q17 and OA12 to Q26 in prose, and the `gate_ids` of the three rows are empty like those of OA01 to OA09). They are kept apart from the plan's wording (CLAUDE.md). An S-R "passes only when its gate passes" (p. 136, Q), so a gate must exist: the derived gates below define one per row from the owner's words, using plan gates wherever they apply; for OA10 to OA12 the derived gate uses every gate that the owner's words and the decision reach, which is the stricter reading of the decision's mapping (DD-G14, X-53). Output `gate_results/OA01.json` .. `OA12.json` (same schema, `python3 tools/bt/gates/run_gate.py OA01`); thresholds are recorded decisions (1.7); they are reported separately from the 957 plan rows. Where an owner word cannot be decided by a machine (`accurate`, `Hollywood-grade`, `complete`), PASS also needs the recorded acceptance of frames or lists that were looked at (U-10), and the claim the product makes never goes beyond the record (Law cl. 3, p. 132; DEC-003). A plausible-procedural fill that is drawn, marked and registered keeps an item at the tier of its evidence status (U-02 P2) and does not block PASS of OA10 to OA12; an open gap that is shown only as a marker does (4.9 TOP-3; A-55). `requirements.json` is not given `gate_ids` for them (only the four fields of 1.6 are writable); `apply_passes.py` holds this table (X-40).

| Row | Owner text (requirements.json) | Derived checks | Plan gates used | PASS needs | Not passing when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OA01 | Battle Theatre is a separate page or section with its own renderer, entry button and Back with no leaked WebGL or audio; read-only reuse of the 16 tour stops and 512 monuments as camera bookmarks | (a) loads as its own page or section with its own renderer instance and an entry button on the main site; (b) after Back no WebGL context and no audio context owned by the Theatre is live, across repeated enter and back cycles (count T-OA01-1, the count in `leak.spec`); (c) bookmarks derived read-only at load from the main site's 16 tour stops and 512 monuments, counts equal the main-site data at build, no write path, main-site data hash unchanged (`ext-site-source.mjs --check`); (d) the Virtual Walkthrough and Monuments sections still work (regression against the published build, FINAL-ACCEPTANCE step 3) | Q24 | a, b, c, d on swgl browser runs (real-device behaviour owed) | any of a to d fails or is not run |
| OA02 | Every soldier individually animated; total rendered equals the number present, no performance capping without a recorded owner decision | (a) per unit, scenario and grid time the instance count equals the present strength (impostors and LOD counted); (b) no cap constant or budget limit on the figure count in the code; (c) every instance has its own animation phase and variant from the counter-based generator (run seed, agent id, time bucket; p. 125, D.3.2); (d) the individual-level content is marked plausible-procedural (EG-0001) | Q01.6, Q15, Q23 | a to d at the upper end of every strength range and at the default | any count differs, any cap exists, or a figure is not individually seeded |
| OA03 | Complete roster section of both armies; source and rights per name (the plan has no roster requirement) | (a) every unit of the verified OOB has a roster record or a registered gap with the count of names missing; (b) every name row has source id, rights status and tier; (c) the section is in the product; (d) only public-domain or openly readable sources, no logins, no circumvention | Q02, Q25 | every unit's roster complete against the best surviving lawful public source, plus a to d; not literally satisfiable for the Confederate army (only about one third of company muster rolls survive, p. 9, B.1) | any unit with a registered gap is LINKED-ACTIVE (EG-0129); OA03 cannot pass without a recorded interpretation of "complete" (A-41) |

The denominator D is every documented unit-level quantity of the claim ledger (position region and window, activity class, orientation or formation), tier documented and `verified_primary`; D may only grow | Q04, Q05, Q06, Q19 | M at least 97% (T-OA06-1, the owner's figure) on the default scenario and each scenario the quantity belongs to; per-quantity results published; D not narrowed | M below 97%, or D not machine-listed ("well over" has no number: 97% is the floor and the actual is reported, A-41) |

Every timeline row in it is in the Events list, jumpable, and its realization plays (4.1) or, for an open-gap row, its gap marker shows | Q17, Q12 | every row in the interval PASS (TL-9) or gap-marked K5; none merely linked without a visible marker | any row in the interval without an Events entry, a jump or a marker |

the claim made to users stays under Law cl. 3 and DEC-003, DEC-OA10-12 item 3) | (a) extent: E is the union of the places of every timeline row and state-schedule row of the window as the gazetteer gives them, listed by the runner; each place of E lies on the loaded ground or is listed as beyond it with its rows (the harness reports `place_off_ground`); (b) terrain: the B.9 reconstruction (pp. 103-109; G.1 to G.3, p. 134) with the residual report per area (RC-R02.1), and on every tile and object `tier`, `source_ids` and an error estimate (G.8, p. 134); No owner-only step in this class is performed by Claude; permission requests to third parties for rosters were withdrawn by DEC-019 (public sources only). OA10 to OA12 add no owner step.

### 3.2 Domain gates for state-schedule rows (class SS)

The two listed gates (Q07, Q09) do not fit every S row: the schedule names fences, buildings, hospitals, works, gun emplacements, environment objects, a person, a road and nine epilogue states. Counted by object-id prefix in `state_schedule.json`: `bld-` alone 21, `H-` and `bld-` 14, `E-` 12, `WS-` 9, `FEN-` 6, `G-` 6, `H-` 4, `ENV-` 3, `FEN-` with `bld-` 3, `E-` with `G-` 2, and one each of `P`, `E` with `FEN`, `ENV` with `FEN`, `FEN` with `P`, `GROUP`, `PERSON` with `bld`, `P` with `bld`, `RD` with `bld`; two rows are `out_of_battle_scope` (S-078, S-079). An S row is judged by the gates of **each object it names**, by prefix, and passes only if every object passes (A-12, X-35).

| Object-id prefix | Meaning | Gates that judge the state change | Also |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bld-` (with `#part`) | building, barn, house, church, station, gatehouse | Q09 | Q03.3 (exists on its date), Q23 |
| `H-` | hospital site | Q09 (use state) and Q26 (activity in its interval) | Q03.3 |
| `FEN-`, `P-01`..`P-14` | fence, wall, gate | Q07 | class PE for the P ids |
| `E-` | earthwork, breastwork, barricade | Q08 | Q04 for the builder unit |
| `G-` | gun emplacement | Q08 when prepared; Q14 | |
| `WS-` | epilogue state S-080..S-088 | Q28 | Q07 for a fence or wall in the state (S-082, S-084); Q21 for ground cover (S-084, S-085); Q11 and Q22 for S-086 |
| `ENV-` | environment object | by channel: crop (S-033) Q21; water and weather (S-069 Plum Run) Q22; a held position (S-067 withdrawal) Q04, Q05, Q06; a cemetery ground (S-045, with a `FEN-` object): Q07 for the fence part, the ground part has no gate: BLOCKED-INPUT | |
| `GROUP-` | group of sites (S-070 hospital sites in cold rain) | Q26 and Q22 | |
| `PERSON-` | a named person (S-072 Armistead dies on July 5) | Q26 and Q02 | |
| `RD-` | road or railroad (S-076 traffic resumes July 10) | none: no gate of the plan judges a road state; BLOCKED-INPUT until a road-state check is defined | Q03.3 |
| any other prefix | | BLOCKED-INPUT: the mapping is extended by a recorded decision, never by treating the row as N/A | |

**Out-of-window rows** (S-078, S-079; and any later row such as the 1895 and 1910 fires). The transition lies outside the window (p. 102, B.8.7: "Not part of the battle animation"). The item is a negative check: for every grid time in the window the object's state equals `from_state`, the info card states the later date with its source, and the product never draws the transition.
**Rows the engine must never schedule** (p. 102, B.8.7): the McPherson barn does not burn on July 1 or at any time in the battle; the Bliss buildings do not burn on July 2; the Emmitsburg Road fences are not destroyed before July 2-3; no avenue exists before the park era. They are global assertions of Q09 (the first two), Q07 (the third) and Q03.4 (the fourth) and fail the gate if violated, whatever the row-level results.

## 4. Pass rules per sub-requirement class

`requirements.json` holds 969 rows in ten classes on 2026-11-14 (counted from the ids; every row has `source_status: seeded_unverified` and `passes: false`; the rows OA10 to OA12 were added on 2026-11-13):

| Code | Class | Ids | Rows | `gate_ids` | Underlying data |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TL | Timeline rows | R11.0001 to R11.0691 | 691 | Q17, Q12 | `timeline_seed.jsonl`, `timeline_verified.jsonl`, `timeline_windows.jsonl`, `timeline_corrections/` |
| EP | Epilogue rows (B.12.2) | R22.0001 to R22.0014 | 14 | Q28, Q17 | `epilogue_seed.jsonl` (E-001 to E-014) |
| SS | State-schedule entries | R14.S-001 to R14.S-088 | 88 | Q07, Q09 | `structures/state_schedule.json` (S-001..S-079 from B.8.7, S-080..S-088 from B.12.3) |
| WK | Construction chronology | R14.E-01 to R14.E-18 | 18 | Q08 | `structures/structures.json`, `works_stages.json` |
| GE | Gun emplacements | R13.G-01 to R13.G-18 | 18 | Q14 | `structures.json`, `kit/artillery_batteries.json` |
| PE | Pre-existing features | R13.P-01 to R13.P-14 | 14 | Q03 | `structures.json` |
| CT | Terrain source catalogue | R12.001 to R12.045 | 45 | Q25, Q16 | B.9 tables 1 to 5 (pp. 104-109), `library/status*.json` |
| CF | Source conflicts | R03.C-001 to R03.C-047 | 47 | Q02, Q19 | `conflict_register.json`, `conflicts_structured.json`, `decisions/conflict_defaults.json` |
| TOP | Top-level acceptance | R01.0000 to R22.0000 | 22 | the requirement's gates (App. E) | n/a |
| OA | Owner chat additions | OA01.0000 to OA12.0000 | 12 | none (3.1) | owner chat, `decision_log.md` (DEC-OA10-12 for the last three), DD-nn |
| | Total | | 969 | | |

The timeline rows map to R11 ids in table order (R11.0001 = T1-001, R11.0226 = T2-001, R11.0474 = T3-001, R11.0501 = T3-028; T3-131 is absent); T1 has 225, T2 248, T3 218 rows. Each class lists: the item, the evidence status the row needs (section 5), the conditions that together give `passes=true`, how a link counts (LINKED, not passing) and what never passes. "Registered" means an entry of level L3 (1.4) unless the text says L1. Class rules add to U-01 and U-02 and never replace them. An S-R of a class this file does not define (4.11 until its rule is applied) can be LINKED at most and never passes.

### 4.1 TL: timeline rows R11.0001 to R11.0691

**S-R text.** `Timeline row T1-001 (tier documented) has a visual realization or an open register entry.` The tier in brackets is the seed tier. **Gates.** Q17 (item: the row), Q12 (item: the row's seek targets); Q04, Q05, Q06, Q23 and, for NIGHT rows, Q10 apply to the content the row realizes.
- **TL-1 Effective tier** (1.3). Information on 2026-11-14, unchanged since 2026-11-13 (not a rule; counted for this file from the files): seed tiers documented 580, inferred 55, open gap 44, plausible-procedural 12; the overlay `timeline_verified.jsonl` (DD-08, DD-09) holds documented 452, inferred 144, open gap 71, plausible-procedural 19 and `unclassified` 5 (T2-121, T2-133, T2-148, T2-176, T2-178: such a row has no effective tier, is treated as an open gap and cannot pass until a correction record gives it a tier, A-53, DD-G15), which differs from the seed on 163 rows (140 lower, 23 higher; each higher one needs its matched citation, 1.3); `status_effective` over the 691 rows verified_against_or_text 265, partially_verified 200, gap 130, inferred 44, failed_lookup 22, claim_unsupported_by_or_text 22, recheck_failed 8. Of the 452 documented rows 251 are verified_against_or_text, 192 partially_verified, 6 recheck_failed and 3 claim_unsupported_by_or_text, so 251 is the most that can reach `verified_primary` on today's records, before the runner re-matches their extracts (TL-3). The correction folders keep growing: at 01:05 UTC `timeline_corrections/` held 382 records over 282 row ids (231 row-level and 151 supplementary; `tl-04.jsonl` appeared at 01:04 UTC) and `evidence_corrections/` 1,793 records over 169 ids; the counts of 324 and 226 at 00:33 UTC are superseded, and section 5 says which record gives a row its status.
- **TL-2 Row kinds** K1 scene event, K2 environment, K3 structure or hospital, K4 context with no scene consequence, K5 open gap, K6 night, and the kinds of the realization record that count for each (section 6). A card in the Events list is not a realization of any kind (K4).
- **TL-3 Evidence status.** Effective tier documented needs `verified_primary`, inferred `verified_inferred`, plausible-procedural `procedural_registered` (section 5). Any other status, or effective tier `open gap` or unclassified, does not pass. The runner re-matches the row's `citation` and `passage_ref` quote (or the correction record's citation quotes) in the stored source text in this run (`tools/bt/or27_find.py` normalized view for the Official Records, the named non-lead source otherwise); a stored `verified` flag is not evidence.
- **TL-4 Realization.** The Q17 item is PASS: a valid scene realization of kind K1, K2, K3 or K6 (section 6). K4 and K5 never pass.
- **TL-5 Scrub (Q12) per row.** Seek targets: the row window's earliest, default and latest; rows with no clock minute (`phrase_only`, `no_date`): the bounds of the date or part-of-day window and three seeded interior times. For each target and each scenario of the row's group the digest after a random-access seek equals the digest after sequential playback, the round trip A to B to A returns the identical digest (coverage map 12, p. 4), no unit pops.
- **TL-6 Units.** Q04, Q05, Q06 hold for the row's units (the `entities.units` of its record, leaves of a formation included) at the realized times in the scenarios of the group: nothing before its arrival window, speeds within range, a continuous path from the previous position.
- **TL-7 Marking and independence.** Q23 holds for the realized content (the tier flag is shown). A documented row whose datum has one independent source carries the visible flag "single source" (Q19); a visible datum with two independent non-lead sources needs none.
- **TL-8 Conflicts.** Every value of every conflict the row names (its conflict column, `C-nnn`) and every conflict of the group reaching the row's units is a selectable scenario and the realization is valid under each, or the record states which values it excludes and why; a printed time cell that allows two windows (6.4 V4) is handled the same way, each reading realized or excluded with the stated reason. The row is shown as a window, not a falsely exact minute (p. 124, D.2 V4).
- **TL-9 Passes.** TL-3, TL-4, TL-5, TL-6, TL-7 and TL-8 all hold (and Q10 at the realized frames for a NIGHT row). A declared binding of the record (6.4 V2) needs its review record.
- **TL-10 Linked (not passing)** when any of TL-3 to TL-8 cannot be met: effective tier open gap or `unclassified`; status partially_verified, gap_registered, failed_lookup, claim_unsupported or lead_only; kind K4 or K5; realization absent or invalid; Q12 or Q10 not run. The entry must be L3. For an open-gap row it is the one the plan's gap tables name ("Gaps and sources to acquire": T1 p. 22; the open-gap index of T2 pp. 37-38; T3 p. 57) with the settling source, and `searched` shows that source was read or unreachable (negative result with host and date). For a plausible-procedural row the entry holds the exhaustive search (Law cl. 3a).
- **TL-11 Never passing.** A row whose realization is a card only; a row realized by an unmarked procedural fill; a K5 row; a partial realization.
- **TL-12 Gate-level coverage versus passing.** Q17 is a coverage gate: a K4 or K5 row, a card-only row or a partial one with an L3 entry is COVERED (the gate verdict PASS can include it) and its S-R is LINKED-ACTIVE.

### 4.2 EP: epilogue rows R22.0001 to R22.0014 (E-001 to E-014, B.12.2)

**S-R text.** `Epilogue row E-001 (PDF tier documented; sources are encyclopedia/blog leads, so verify before use) is realized or registered.` **Gates.** Q28, Q17. **Underlying row.** `epilogue_seed.jsonl`.
- **EP-1 Evidence.** The rows cite W-CONS (a Wikipedia article that flags several of its own claims), GC-COMP (a student blog post) and EWH (a popular compilation): all leads (p. 117, B.12 evidence quality; p. 134, E.1, E.2). Effective tier documented needs a non-lead source read in the audit: Hale's report, the Associated Press and Philadelphia Press reports (Chronicling America), the Lincoln manuscripts and papers (Library of Congress), NPS and ABT pages, Lamon, Hay, Nicolay, Wills (p. 117: they are "the Phase 1 targets for this section"; p. 118, B.12.5). Until read the row is `lead_only` and cannot pass.
- **EP-2 Lincoln items.** Where a row states anything about Lincoln's clothing, voice, wording, seat, horse or manner, the item passes only if each such attribute has two independent non-lead sources read in the audit; otherwise the product marks it "reconstruction" (render_flag 1 or 2) on screen and in the card (p. 136, Q28). The wording of the Address shown is a selectable alternative in the conflict register (A-31); the default is the text reporters printed on the day, the revised copies stay available (p. 118, B.12.4).
- **EP-3 Conflicts carried.** E-004 start (09:30 or about 11:00), E-005 arrival (about noon), E-006 start (derived backward from the 16:00 end; the range flagged), E-007 weather (cloudy clearing at the prayer, default, versus a perfect morning), E-010 order (hymn after Everett or Glee Club ode before Lincoln), E-011 reaction (muted default, applause alternative) and wording, E-013 salute (one source): each a selectable scenario with its sources.
- **EP-4 Film path.** The jump is a presentation device, labeled in the viewer as a time jump (p. 117, B.12.1); the chapter is its own time chapter; the viewer can leave the front-row viewpoint; the default path goes from the end of July 4 to November 19; rows T3-168 onward remain scrubbable as optional chapters and are not on the default film path (p. 117).
- **EP-5 Passes.** EP-1 (effective tier and evidence), EP-2, EP-3, a valid scene realization (kinds K1 or K2: the crowd, platform, Lincoln figure, sky; a text card is not enough) at the row's time inside the chapter, Q12 over the chapter's seek targets, Q23 marking and the Q28 per-row checks. LINKED-ACTIVE when any fails and an L3 entry exists (EG-0022 and EG-0120 exist for the Lincoln gap and the Address).

### 4.3 SS: state-schedule entries R14.S-001 to R14.S-088

**S-R text.** `State-schedule entry S-001 changes state at its documented window.` **Gates.** Q07 and Q09 as listed; the gates that actually judge a row are those of 3.2 for each object it names.
- **SS-1 Domain.** Every object named by the row passes the gates of 3.2. A row with an unmapped object, or an `RD-` object, cannot pass.
- **SS-2 Evidence status.** Documented tier: `verified_primary` for the transition and its window (the schedule's `claim_support_verified` must be true and re-matched). A row whose every cited key is weak (Wikipedia, Stone Sentinels, forums, the blogs the plan flags) was already lowered to inferred by the structures workstream (`downgraded: PDF printed documented but every cited key is weak`) and needs `verified_inferred` to pass. A row with tier `contested` carries every value as a selectable scenario. Counted on 2026-11-14 (unchanged since 2026-11-13): `claim_support_verified` is false on all 88 rows and 38 rows carry `weak_source: true`, so no S row can pass on the schedule file alone; all 88 have a row-level evidence record (section 5). Evidence corrections (`evidence_corrections/fence-1.jsonl`, `fence-2.jsonl`) enter through section 5.
- **SS-3 Window.** `time.window = [earliest, latest, default]`. A clock window: the change is shown inside it. A date-only or "morning" window is the plain reading of the source and the entry is flagged (p. 100, B.8.7 header): the flag is visible and the row cites its source for the date; for Q09 objects the item is COVERED (X-09). `earliest` not printed ("by X"): the change is placed at the latest bound and flagged. Windows are never reversed and never invented.
- **SS-4 State function.** For every grid time and scenario `state_at(object, t)` equals `from_state` before the window, `to_state` after it and a transitional state inside it; backward scrubbing reverses every transition (p. 100, B.8.7). Progressive states (burning, a work rising, a fence breaking) are progressive in the draw state; at least one intermediate draw state or progressive parameter exists strictly inside any window longer than one minute; for a point change the visible span is T-Q07-1 (DD-G02).
- **SS-5 Shown.** The object is drawn in the state at that time (draw list and, for a software-GL sample, pixels). An object with no geometry (11 records without geometry and 5 with only a point, per the structures README) fails; it must be modelled (Law cl. 1).
- **SS-6 Cause.** Where the plan names a cause the cause appears in the window: soldiers pulling rails, shell strikes, crossing and breaking of fences (p. 137, V.2), the 14th Connecticut firing the Bliss buildings (p. 137, V.4), wounded arriving at a hospital, the builder unit at a work. An unsourced cause is shown plausible-procedural and marked.
- **SS-7 Conflicts.** The date flags and conflicts are scenarios: S-001 June 26 or 27, S-037 Sherfy barn (C-026), S-077 Camp Letterman opening (C-044), S-062 fence loss times.
- **SS-8 Passes.** SS-1 to SS-7 and the global assertions of 3.2 hold.
- **SS-9 Linked (not passing).** A flagged window whose source is unresolved with an L3 entry (COVERED in Q07; in Q09 the item is COVERED and keeps the Q09 verdict INCOMPLETE, 1.1); an undrawn object; an `RD-` row; a row whose transition has only lead support.
- **SS-10 Out-of-window rows** (S-078, S-079): 3.2; PASS on the negative check plus the evidence status of the stated later date.
- **SS-11 Epilogue states** (S-080 to S-088, B.12.3): judged by Q28 (a tier on every state; the conflicts S-081 start date October 17 or 27 and S-083 platform position carried as scenarios; S-085 and S-086 inferred with the reasoning shown, S-086 derived from the ephemeris at 39.82 N in the plan against 39.83 N in B.10.2, A-17), plus Q07 for fences and Q21 for ground cover. Q07 and Q09 are N/A for them unless the state names a fence or a building.

### 4.4 WK: construction chronology R14.E-01 to R14.E-18

**S-R text.** `Construction chronology entry E-01 is built on screen, never pre-placed.` **Gate.** Q08. The text is a catch-all; the row's own B.7.2 content (pp. 90-92) fixes its type and the check follows the type (A-15).

| Type | Rows (B.7.2) | Check |
|---|---|---|
| Construction | E-01 Seminary rail barricade, E-02 Culp's Hill north slope (6th Wisconsin, about a dozen spades), E-03 123rd NY works, E-04 Cemetery Hill "fortified", E-05 Greene's main line (about 06:00 July 2, 3 to 4 h, about 5 ft, about 300 yd), E-06 traverse, E-07 Kane and Wadsworth's men, E-08 Williams's division, E-09 extension at 18:30 (about 800 yd), E-12 East Cemetery Hill lunettes (builder unknown, existing by about 16:00), E-14 Weed's stone breastworks | Q08.1 to Q08.4, Q08.7 |
| Occupation | E-10 Steuart's brigade in the empty lower works after dark July 2 ("Occupation, not construction") | the works exist from E-05, E-07 and E-08, are empty from 18:30 until the occupation, the occupying units pass Q04, no construction animation |
| Damage | E-11 shelling and retaking on July 3 (about 04:00 to 11:00) | stage 6 "damaged" appears inside the window, shelling events by the named batteries |
| No construction | E-13 Vincent's brigade (natural ledges), E-15 Hazlett's guns hand-hauled (no prepared emplacement), E-16 Ward and Smith at Devil's Den | no work object exists at that place at any time in any scenario; guns and units appear as the G rows say; the "no works" fact is marked |
| Open gap | E-17 rail works on Cemetery Ridge ("No source found ... Do not animate rail piles as documented"), E-18 Confederate works | nothing drawn as documented; anything drawn is flagged plausible-procedural; the gap marker is shown |

- **WK-1 E-17 and the delegated decisions.** DD-02 chose to build E-17 on screen in the Official Records window (`Built on screen in the OR window; PDF wording kept as scenario`; Hancock OR27-1-R076 p. 370; Gibbon R098 p. 416); DD-FENCE1-08 confirms it. The row then follows the construction type with documented existence and materials and plausible-procedural dimensions registered; the plan's reading is a selectable scenario in which nothing is drawn as documented. E-03 (DD-03: morning of July 2 instead of July 1) and E-05 (start conflict: night of July 1-2 or about 06:00) are scenarios the same way; DD-04 and DD-05 change G-03, G-11, G-12, G-18, P-14 (4.5, 4.6).
- **WK-2 Evidence status** by component as the row prints it (E-01 "documented (existence, method); inferred (start window)"): each component needs its status of section 5; weak-source-only components (E-04 "Soft", Wikipedia) are `lead_only` until a non-lead source is read.
- **WK-3 Crew (Q08.3).** The builder unit named in the row is the crew (present by Q04), the tool count is the printed one (E-02: about a dozen spades, capped at 12 for that regiment, p. 93, B.7.4); where the builder is unknown the work is built by a labeled stand-in crew flagged "builder unknown" (p. 90, B.7.1; E-12, E-04).
- **WK-4 Passes** (construction type): Q08.1 to Q08.4 and Q08.7 PASS, WK-2, Q23 marking of every plausible-procedural parameter (B.7.4 labeled assumptions: stage shares, regiment offsets, thickness and profile, the Seminary barricade height and rails, tool mix), scenario coverage. The other types pass on their check in the table plus WK-2.
- **WK-5 Linked (not passing).** E-18; E-17 while only the plan's reading is accepted; E-04 on lead support; rows whose builder, time or position is an open gap; works not yet drawn (a registered unfinished build).
- **WK-6 No modern profile.** No 1863 profile is derived from modern rebuilt works (Culp's Hill, Little Round Top, Cemetery Ridge walls of 48 to 52 in.; p. 94, B.7.6); the profile is the qualitative one (log close-fence with stone and earth, about 5 ft), tier inferred; DD-FENCE1-20 marks every 1863 dimension a plausible-procedural gap.

### 4.5 GE: gun emplacements R13.G-01 to R13.G-18

**S-R text.** `Gun emplacement G-01 carries gun type/count and prepared-vs-improvised state.` **Gate.** Q14 as listed; prepared emplacements also pass Q08 (A-13, X-12).
- **GE-1 Record.** Each G record holds the gun type(s) and count(s) with sources and tier, the state as the plan prints it (prepared, improvised, "no works described"), the positions by time (B.6.14.7, p. 81) and the battery's presence window. Ranges stay ranges where the plan prints them: G-11 6 to 23 guns as batteries arrived and left; G-13 about 80 to as many as 120 Union guns; G-14 138 to 179 estimates, reasoned 142 to 155 (p. 94, B.7.5); G-16 Hurt's guns against Miller's as a conflict.
- **GE-2 Q14 item.** The piece type is in the verified weapons table; the count at each grid time equals the verified battery table for that battery (a documented range stays a range); no gun exists before the battery's arrival (Q04) or after a documented loss.
- **GE-3 State rules** (pp. 93-94, B.7.5 "Engine rules"). Prepared emplacements (G-03 lunettes, G-12 in-line ridge battery) are built on screen like any work (Q08). Improvised positions (G-01, G-05, G-06, G-07, G-10, G-11) get no earth: guns unlimbered on natural ground with trail marks, hand-hauling or prolonge retirement where sourced. "No works described" positions are open-ground battery positions logged as a gap. DD-04 (Stevens' Knoll earthworks for G-18, per-gun small earthworks for G-03 on the evening of July 1, G-11 split into 11a improvised and 11b a slight earthwork on July 3, G-12 no earthwork at Cushing with the wall pre-existing) is the default with the plan's reading selectable; both pass their checks.
- **GE-4 Evidence.** Documented components need `verified_primary` from the Official Records reports (Hunt, Wainwright, Alexander, Cabell, Carter, Hazard, Whittier and others, local in `tmp/library/or27`), not Wikipedia, Stone Sentinels, HistoryNet or forum keys (several G rows rest on them). Conflicts (G-10 losses 65 horses or 80 of 88) are scenarios.
- **GE-5 Passes.** GE-2, GE-3 and GE-4, Q04 and Q06 for the battery and its train, Q23 marking. **Linked (not passing):** a "no works described" gap with only a logged gap, a count that is only a plan range with no verified battery table, an emplacement not yet drawn, and a Q14.2 item that rests on a register entry (it is COVERED and keeps Q14 INCOMPLETE, so the G row stays LINKED: 1.1, X-09).
